# nova — Project Structure

> Orientation map for AI agents and new contributors. Read this first instead of
> crawling the tree: it explains what every crate/module is for, how data flows,
> where state is persisted, and which files to open for a given task.

`nova` is a cross-platform (Linux and Windows desktop + Android) media catalog / player app in
the style of Stremio. It talks to Stremio-protocol addons over HTTP and loads explicitly installed
sandboxed JavaScript ZIP packages. AniKoto, Re:ANIME and Supernova sources live in the
separate `nova-addons` checkout; no JavaScript provider or ZIP repository is bundled. Settings → Addons can browse Stremio's official public directory or an explicitly entered standard directory before installation. It plays direct URLs with an
in-window **mpv** player and can stream torrents through an embedded BitTorrent
client. A recent feature adds **cross-device sync** over
[iroh](https://iroh.computer) (the `iroh` branch).

- Language: Rust 2024, UI in [Slint](https://slint.dev) 1.18
- Root package `nova`; heavy subsystems split into leaf crates under `crates/`
- Two entry points: desktop `src/main.rs`, Android `android_main` in `src/lib.rs`; both call `app::run()`

---

## 1. Quick start

```sh
# Dev shell (Nix) — provides Rust, Slint, mpv, native dependencies, and Linux cargo-sweep
nix develop              # Linux desktop toolchain
nix develop .#android    # + NDK/SDK/JDK/cargo-apk2
nix develop .#windows    # MinGW cross-build tools + pinned mpv/FFmpeg bundles

# Build / check
cargo check              # whole workspace (desktop default features)
cargo build              # desktop binary: target/debug/nova
cargo build --profile cd-release # tagged distribution profile: target/cd-release/nova
cargo run                # run desktop app
cargo dev                # retained live-preview alias; currently compiled UI with a warning

# Make targets (enter `nix develop` or `nix develop .#android` first)
make linux-run
make linux-run-preview
make linux-run-release
make android-build
make android-build-release
make android-build-release ANDROID_TARGET=armv7-linux-androideabi
make android-build-release-universal
# Tagged CI uses fat LTO; local Make release targets accept the same profile:
make android-build-release-universal RELEASE_PROFILE=cd-release
make android-run
make android-run-release
# For an emulator: append ANDROID_TARGET=x86_64-linux-android

# Tests
cargo test                              # root app unit + integration tests
cargo test --workspace                  # also leaf-crate unit + documentation tests
cargo test -p nova-sync --lib            # sync crate unit tests (fast)
cargo test --test ui_settings_sync -- sync_overflow:: # headless Slint UI regression tests
cargo test --test ui_discover --test ui_library --test ui_player # isolated UI suites
python3 -B -m unittest discover -s .github/scripts -p test_package_android.py

# Android Make rules use cargo-apk2 (not cargo-apk): it compiles android/java/
# to DEX and declares the foreground service + sync job. They enable the
# Android Slint backend with `--features android`; enter `nix develop .#android`
# before invoking them. Make targets never enter Nix shells themselves.

# Windows x86_64 check (cross-build from Linux)
cargo check --workspace --locked --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu
```

Pull requests and source/build changes pushed to `main` run Linux formatting,
Clippy and workspace checks, the shared OpenGL state guard regression on Linux,
and Windows GNU and ARM64 Android target checks. Linux also checks the desktop build with update checking omitted. `check-android` uses the
Android Nix shell with `--no-default-features --features android,update-check --lib -p nova`
and `--target aarch64-linux-android`.
Pushing a Git tag selects the `cd-release` Cargo profile through the workflow's
`NOVA_RELEASE_PROFILE`, building four Android APKs, a Linux
x86_64 `.deb` and AppImage, and a Windows x86_64 ZIP, then attaches all seven
files to a GitHub Release. Android release filenames are
`Nova-Android-arm64-v8a-<version>.apk`, `Nova-Android-armeabi-v7a-<version>.apk`,
`Nova-Android-x86_64-<version>.apk`, and `Nova-Android-universal-<version>.apk`
(version is the tag without its leading `v`, with `/` replaced by `-`).
The universal APK contains all three architectures and compresses native libraries
with extraction enabled; individual ABI APKs retain uncompressed, aligned libraries
for direct loading. ARMv7 supports devices
running a 32-bit ARM Android userspace, including compatible TV devices.
The Windows bundle is named `Nova-Windows-x86_64-<version>.zip` (tag without
the leading `v`, with `/` replaced by `-`). `.github/scripts/package-windows.sh`
places the executable, DLLs, and license/source notices directly inside it.
The Actions upload uses `archive: false`, so downloading the build artifact
returns that ZIP directly. The release job downloads it by artifact ID with
`skip-decompress: true` to preserve the ZIP for publishing. Linux release
files are named `nova-x86_64-<tag>.deb` and
`nova-x86_64-<tag>.AppImage`; both come from one native Ubuntu 22.04 build.
The `.deb` declares shared-library dependencies, while the AppImage bundles
libraries using linuxdeploy. Nova uses Slint's Winit/FemtoVG backend; Qt is
not required.

Windows keeps its native title bar and caption buttons; a Winit window-attribute
hook in `src/app/run.rs` requests the dark native frame before creation.
Context menus use Slint-rendered popups instead of Win32/muda
(`SLINT_NO_MUDA`, set before service/window initialization in `src/app/run.rs`).
`crates/ui/build-support.rs::compiler_configuration` selects `fluent-dark`
for every Windows UI compilation unit using Cargo's target OS, including
Linux cross-builds, so menus remain dark even with a light Windows system theme.
Other targets retain their existing widget style and menu presentation.

The tagged Android workflow runs three parallel `android-build` matrix jobs:
ARM64 (`aarch64-linux-android`), ARMv7 (`armv7-linux-androideabi`) and x86_64.
Each invokes the single-target release Make target and uploads its signed APK
with stored, 16 KiB aligned native libraries and `extractNativeLibs=false`.
The dependent `android-package` job downloads the three separate input artifacts.
`.github/scripts/package-android.py --abi-apk ABI=PATH` (repeated for all three)
verifies each input's version, native layout and signing certificate, requires
identical shared DEX/resources/manifest/license payloads and compression, and
merges only their validated ABI-specific libraries. The three final per-ABI APKs
retain their original payload and native storage policy. The final universal
APK instead deflates each native `.so` and sets only the compiled application
`android:extractNativeLibs` boolean to true; the binary XML patch validates the
element, Android namespace and attribute resource ID. The script aligns and signs all four with
the same selected-profile key cargo-apk2 uses (environment override, then Cargo metadata),
and checks signatures, signing certificates, exact ABI sets, native libraries,
and unchanged payload bytes/compression except those two explicit universal
transformations. Verification requires stored native entries on 16 KiB data
boundaries with extraction disabled for the input and per-ABI outputs, and
deflated native entries with extraction enabled for the universal output.
All signed outputs also pass `zipalign -c -P 16`. Python regressions in
`.github/scripts/test_package_android.py` cover both policies, exact manifest
changes, UTF-8/UTF-16 binary XML and rejection of other payload changes.
All four APKs share one final Actions
artifact for the release job to collect after `android-package` succeeds.
Without `--abi-apk`, the packager still accepts the local
`make android-build-release-universal` intermediate. `.github/scripts/test_package_android.py`
checks ABI coverage against Cargo's targets, parallel-input completeness,
shared-payload conflicts, split payloads, native library completeness,
compression/alignment, direct-loading manifest policy and corruption rejection
using temporary APK fixtures; the Linux CI check runs it without Android tooling.
Packagers read the selected profile directory explicitly: `target/cd-release`
for Linux and each Android build input, and `target/x86_64-pc-windows-gnu/cd-release`
for Windows. Outside CI, `NOVA_RELEASE_PROFILE` defaults to `release` in these
scripts, and Make's `RELEASE_PROFILE` also defaults to `release`. Android signing
for `cd-release` uses `CARGO_APK_CD_RELEASE_KEYSTORE` and
`CARGO_APK_CD_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD` when set, otherwise the matching Cargo
metadata entry; the tracked key retains update compatibility for authorized
personal builds, while store releases require a private key override.

Cargo aliases live in `.cargo/config.toml`. Android linkers come from the
`.#android` dev shell env vars (see `.cargo/config.toml` comments); that shell
also provides `cargo-apk2` (packaged by the flake) and unsets `CPATH` (the
stdenv's host include path, which the NDK clang would otherwise pick up).
Target-specific `BINDGEN_EXTRA_CLANG_ARGS_*` also pass the NDK sysroot and API
26 target to host libclang when generating Android bindings.
The Android shell supplies Rust standard libraries for ARM64, ARMv7 and x86_64.
`nix/native-media/{default.nix,release.json}` fetches minimal mpv and six shared
FFmpeg libraries for those ABIs and Windows x86_64 from a pinned
[nova-vendor release](https://github.com/misobarisic/nova-vendor/releases).
`androidMedia` and `windowsMedia` remain development-shell build inputs;
Nix verifies archive hashes and caches the downloads. Build recipes, native
ABI checks, smoke probes, source archives and measurements live in nova-vendor.
Android mpv explicitly links static NDK libc++/libc++abi through its C linker;
bundle checks resolve strong dynamic imports against the packaged libraries and
API-26 system stubs, including C++ runtime symbols.
Native binaries are GitHub release assets. Linux retains system mpv/FFmpeg.

Android exports `NOVA_ANDROID_LIBS` for both `build.rs` and the shell's
cargo-apk2 runtime-library override. `android/cargo-apk2-readonly-libs.patch`
replaces existing staged library entries before copying, so repeated dependency
copies and rebuilds accept read-only Nix inputs without changing the originals.
`android/cargo-apk2-uncompressed-libs.patch` honors the existing
`application.extract_native_libs = false` metadata when adding native libraries,
including release profiles. Only the native-library `aapt add` step disables
compression; other entries retain the packager's existing compression policy.
The pinned SDK 35 packager already runs `zipalign -P 16` before signing, suitable
for direct loading on both 4 KiB and 16 KiB devices ([Android zipalign guide](https://developer.android.com/tools/zipalign)).
`android/cargo-apk2-version-code.patch` reserves Android installation epoch 2:
`(2 << 24) | (major << 16) | (minor << 8) | patch`. This makes the 0.0.x release
sequence install over older local 0.1.0 APKs (code 16777472) while keeping the
public version name unchanged. Development builds share their base release's
code. The real packaging probe verifies this migration; release packaging
rejects APKs from the old epoch before publishing.
`android/cargo-apk2-receivers.patch` adds receiver metadata to the pinned
packager for the private release-notification dismissal receiver. The feature
job and reminder Python tests validate service IDs, lifecycle gates and receiver
declarations without producing an APK.
`NOVA_NATIVE_CA_BUNDLE` embeds the built dependency's Mozilla CA roots;
`nova-player` installs them into `<cache>/native-media/cacert.pem` and enables
TLS verification. FFmpeg 9's JavaVM registration is linked from `libavcodec`.
`libmpv2-sys/use-bindgen` remains enabled on 32-bit Android for target-correct
layouts. ARMv7 Skia still builds from source because upstream's Android binary
cache covers ARM64/x86_64 only; the shell supplies its Python/GN/Ninja/Git tools.

The Windows shell exports `MPV_SOURCE` with import libraries/DLLs in `64/`,
configurations in `metadata/` and native licenses in `licenses/`. Both target
shells provide `NOVA_NATIVE_NOTICES`, generated from the built dependency's
sources/configuration and original license files, for About. APK verification
requires mpv and all six FFmpeg libraries for every ABI and checks their actual
ELF architecture. Windows ZIPs include media DLLs, configurations and licenses.
The native recipe's README and RESULTS in nova-vendor document selected
playback/subtitle and packet-copy remux features, build times, sizes and probes.

The workspace's `dev` profile disables debug information and strips symbols;
the default `test` profile inherits these settings. Incremental compilation
remains enabled. This reduces linked executable size while making development
and test backtraces less detailed. Release and `dev-release` also strip symbols.
The `cd-release` profile inherits `release` (optimization level 3 and stripped
symbols), enables fat LTO, uses one codegen unit and disables incremental
compilation. Tagged Android/Linux/Windows builds use it; ordinary local release
builds retain their current compilation settings.

The 27 generated UI leaf libraries and the type-only `nova-playback-api` library declare `[lib] test = false` to skip empty
unit-test harnesses during default workspace test selection. Their normal
libraries still build for the app and root UI integration suites; the main
`nova-ui` library keeps its seven real unit tests. Explicit `--lib` or
`--all-targets` selection can still request the empty harnesses. Documentation
tests remain enabled separately. Plain root `cargo test` and targeted
`--test ui_*` commands already exclude leaf-library unit harnesses.

**Features** (root `Cargo.toml`):
- `update-check` (default): GitHub release checks and device-local About controls; omit with `--no-default-features --features desktop` for distro-managed builds. Android Make targets include it unless `UPDATE_FEATURES=`.
- `desktop` (default): enables Slint's Winit/FemtoVG backend, the pinned FemtoVG fork’s COLRv1 glyph painter, and `unstable-winit-030` window access for native player cursor visibility.
- `android`: enables Slint's Android Activity backend (Skia on Android);
  Official Android builds use `--no-default-features --features android,update-check`.
- `live-preview`: retained opt-in development feature, currently compiled UI with a build warning. The page factories need native compiled globals/handles; interpreter hot reload requires a separate bridge.

---

## 2. Workspace layout

```
nova/
├── .github/                  # GitHub Actions automation
│   ├── scripts/package-android.py # derive, align, sign, verify APK variants
│   └── workflows/
│       └── build-release.yml  # PR checks; tagged Android + Windows release builds
├── Cargo.toml                # workspace + root package `nova`
├── about.toml                # accepted SPDX licenses for the in-app catalog
├── build.rs                  # version identity, license catalog; native link directives
├── build-support/version.rs  # Git tag/commit resolution, shared with app unit tests
├── .cargo/config.toml        # `cargo dev` alias; Android linker notes
├── flake.nix                 # devShells: default, Android/Windows pinned native media
├── nix/native-media/         # pinned nova-vendor release fetchers, hashes and native notices
├── Makefile                  # Linux and Android build/run targets (use active shell)
├── android/                  # Android source, signing key, and resources
│   ├── java/                 # Java services and activities compiled to DEX by cargo-apk2
│   ├── cargo-apk2-Cargo.lock  # Dependency lock for the pinned upstream APK packager (CLI workspace only)
│   ├── keystore/
│   │   └── debug.keystore    # Tracked debug key for local APK signing; not for publishing
│   └── res/                  # Launcher/TV artwork and native PiP action translations
├── assets/                   # App logo, fonts, backdrop scrim, onboarding concepts, and in-app license/vendor catalog templates
│   └── onboarding/           # Generated first-run onboarding screen concepts
├── vendor/                   # local Slint patches
│   ├── i-slint-backend-android-activity/ # Slint 1.18 window-lifecycle patch + NOVA_PATCH.md
│   └── i-slint-compiler/      # Slint 1.18 compiled-root embedding patch + NOVA_PATCH.md
├── src/                      # root app crate `nova`
├── crates/                   # leaf crates (see below)
│   ├── ui/src/backend_text.rs # Rust-side UI text formatting/translations, owned by nova-ui
│   ├── ui/translations/      # Slint translation catalogs: <code>/LC_MESSAGES/nova-ui.po (build-time bundled)
│   ├── ui-shared/            # canonical UI models, globals and page bridge contracts
│   ├── ui-settings/          # compiled Settings navigation shell and submenu factories
│   ├── ui-settings-*/        # thirteen independently compiled Settings submenu bodies
│   ├── ui-home/              # separately compiled Home tree
│   ├── ui-discover/          # separately compiled Discover tree
│   ├── ui-calendar/          # separately compiled Calendar tree
│   ├── ui-detail/            # compiled Detail shell and nested factories
│   ├── ui-detail-*/          # Tracking, active entry editor, Streams and Episodes
│   ├── ui-library/           # separately compiled Library
│   ├── ui-more/              # More, About/Licenses and Downloads roots
│   ├── ui-player-osd/        # compiled playback overlay and gestures
│   ├── ui-onboarding/        # compiled first-launch wizard
│   ├── providers/            # Normalized provider contract, addon metadata/mapping services, QuickJS host, ZIP packages
│   ├── playback-api/         # shared ephemeral playback commands/snapshots
│   ├── party/                # temporary invited watch parties, independent of sync
│   ├── download/             # durable stream jobs + progressive HTTP transfer
│   ├── tracking/             # local anime tracker mappings, progress projection, persistence
│   ├── list-services/        # bounded official Simkl/MDBList/Trakt APIs, authentication and pure transport tests
│   └── stremio/              # official account API, watched bitfields and library reconciliation
├── scripts/package-addon.py  # explicit-source deterministic ZIP build + runtime validation
├── tests/                    # headless Slint UI integration tests
│   ├── ui_*.rs               # 20 small linked UI suites, isolated cases
│   ├── <suite>/              # separate case files; not individual Cargo targets
│   ├── support/              # subprocess isolation and shared UI helpers
│   ├── {filter_pill_touch,settings_control_feedback,theme}.rs # independent inline harnesses
│   ├── tracking_sync.rs      # independent backend/sync integration test
│   └── tmp_snap.rs           # existing diagnostic rail probe
└── docs/
    ├── addon-packages.md         # local ZIP install, authoring and paired package transfer
    ├── calendar.md               # library release planner, views and schedule rules
    ├── library-behavior.md       # library entries, buckets, badges and menus
    ├── metadata-pipeline.md      # metadata/artwork pipeline and cache policies
    ├── platform-storage.md       # per-platform data and cache locations
    ├── player-behavior.md        # next-episode offers and stream selection
    ├── profiles.md               # personal profiles, startup selection and account-wide sync
    ├── project-structure.md      # this file
    ├── repository-follow-ons.md  # maintenance, Android restoration/screenshots and font validation follow-ons
    ├── stremio-sync.md           # account linking, two-way library/progress merge and mapping limits
    ├── list-services.md          # Simkl/MDBList/Trakt selected/full scope, credentials and guarded additive sync
    ├── sync-follow-ons.md        # remaining sync feature ideas
    ├── sync-hardening-plan.md    # implemented correctness/recovery contracts and remaining validation
    └── tracking-behavior.md      # anime tracking, account linking and progress projection
```

Documentation filenames use lowercase words separated by hyphens. Name the
subject first, then the purpose where useful (`library-behavior.md`,
`metadata-pipeline.md`, `sync-hardening-plan.md`). Use `follow-ons` consistently
for deferred work. Keep repository-level conventions such as `README.md` and
`AGENTS.md` unchanged, and update links when renaming a document.

### Crate dependency graph

```
                                 ┌─────────────┐
                                 │ nova (root) │  binary + lib (cdylib/rlib for Android)
                                 └──────┬──────┘
  ┌──────────────┬──────────────┬───────┼──────┬──────────────┬──────────────┐
  ▼              ▼              ▼              ▼              ▼              ▼
  nova-ui        nova-player    nova-media     nova-download  nova-torrent   nova-sync    addons
  │              │              │              │              │
  slint,         nova-config,   nova-config,   serde,         nova-config,   (standalone)
  nova-config,   nova-ui,       slint,         tokio,         nova-storage,
                 slint          addons,        reqwest        iroh,tokio
  nova-ui-{settings,home,discover,calendar,detail,library,more,player-osd,onboarding}
  nova-ui-shared                image,webp

                                                              │
                                                              nova-storage (redb)
```

`nova` and `nova-sync` also depend on the small `nova-playback-api` contract crate
(Serde only); the native player stays independent of the networking transport.
Casting is disabled in shipped builds starting with v0.0.40: runtime entry
points in `casting.rs` return without installing callbacks or handing off media,
and `CastBridge.available` defaults to false to hide UI and skip keyboard/search
entries. Android omits the casting service registration. Implementation, saved
remux preferences and isolated UI/protocol tests remain for future work.
`nova` also depends on `nova-cast` (oxicast Google Cast and UPnP/DLNA) and
`nova-cast-media`, which depends on `nova-cast` and `nova-remux`. The latter
compiles `packet_copy.c` and its streaming implementation `stream_copy.c`
against the existing system or bundled FFmpeg headers; it inspects the source
and copies compressed packets without an audio/video transcoding pipeline.
`nova` and `nova-media` also depend on `nova-providers`, which depends on
`addons`, `nova-config` (shared stream policy), `reqwest` (blocking HTTPS), `scraper`, `quick-xml` (bounded feeds), and `rquickjs`/QuickJS. Android
enables QuickJS's `bindgen` feature; the Android Nix shell supplies libclang
and target-specific NDK sysroot arguments.
`nova` also depends on `nova-tracking`, which depends on `nova-storage`, Serde, reqwest, URL parsing and zeroized credentials;
the tracking actor owns local state, shared plaintext credentials and authenticated delivery. Chrono supplies local calendar dates; `getrandom` supplies OAuth state/PKCE entropy. Android tracker HTTPS uses a preconfigured rustls client with bundled Mozilla roots, matching the other Android HTTP clients and avoiding the platform verifier's JNI/Kotlin runtime requirements.

| Crate | Path | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `nova` | `src/` | App logic, UI bridge, entry points. Depends on everything. |
| `nova-ui` | `crates/ui` | Compiled AppWindow and page hosts, Rust-side localized text (`backend_text.rs`) and packaged emoji fallback (`fonts.rs`). `src/window.rs` preserves the AppWindow API and installs compiled page factories; `src/{settings_factory,page_factories,collection_factories,playback_factories}.rs` forward callbacks to root routing functions. Depends on the page crates and `nova-ui-shared`, Slint, `i-slint-core` for fonts, `nova-config`, and `serde_json`. |
| `nova-ui-shared` | `crates/ui-shared` | Canonical native model types, Theme/Anim/navigation/input/settings globals, page bridge contracts, `SettingsContent` submenu contracts and `DetailContent` nested-page/editor contracts under `@NovaUiShared`. AppWindow owns one shared globals bundle that embedded pages reuse; the generated `SharedRoot` is never mounted. |
| `nova-ui-settings`, `nova-ui-home`, `nova-ui-discover`, `nova-ui-calendar`, `nova-ui-detail` | `crates/ui-{settings,home,discover,calendar,detail}` | Separate AOT visual trees importing the existing page sources in `crates/ui`. Each `*-module.slint` forwards properties and callbacks through its shared bridge. Uses experimental Slint library/factory APIs with the pinned compiler embedding patch. Build scripts embed assets and read the canonical `nova-ui.po` catalogs directly through the compiler catalog-domain override; no generated translation copies or new persisted state. |
| `nova-ui-detail-*` | `crates/ui-detail-{tracking,tracking-entry,streams,episodes}` | Independently compiled Detail children. Tracking and Streams own full-screen scrolling; Episodes reports body height into the retained shell scroll. One active tracking-entry factory follows stable IDs across model replacements. `nova-ui-detail::prepare_subpages` resets transient state before construction; `install_subpages` installs weak callbacks/factories using the same window adapter and globals. |
| `nova-ui-library`, `nova-ui-more`, `nova-ui-player-osd`, `nova-ui-onboarding` | `crates/ui-{library,more,player-osd,onboarding}` | Library, three distinct More/About/Downloads roots, the complete player overlay, and onboarding compile independently of AppWindow. Permanent `collection-bindings.slint`/`playback-bindings.slint` retain backend links; conditional hosts supply only mount inputs. AppWindow retains More transitions, navigation chrome, player transparency and overlay ordering. |
| `nova-config` | `crates/config` | Shared settings types, runtime cache settings and per-use image resolution preferences, platform app paths, playback-rate bounds/helpers (`clamp_playback_speed` / `quantize_playback_speed`), `smart_collections.rs` (validated type/watch-state/genre/runtime rules) and `stream_filters.rs` (advertised offer facts, exact-height resolution/codec/language normalization and filter policy, plus shared `RESOLUTION_CHOICES`), `catalog_grouping.rs` (Original/Season/Series defaults, opt-in Series inference and per-installation/catalog/surface presentation overrides), `playback_preferences.rs` (ISO language catalogue via `isolang` and semantic audio/subtitle choices, per-title overrides, subtitle appearance/timing defaults and field-level title patches, and track matching), `fnv1a`, `now_secs`/`now_ms`. Image resolution fields are optional longest-side limits; settings JSON normalization drops retired controls and migrates the legacy downscale flag only to the player image use. Depends on serde/serde_json and `isolang`; per-device override baselines remain local. Leaf. |
| `nova-ui-settings-*` | `crates/ui-settings-{addons,categories,image-cache,display,p2p,player,look-feel,sync,downloads,downloaded-episodes,tracking,home,theme}` | Each Settings submenu is a separate compiled library. `nova-ui-settings` depends on these leaves and installs their weak factories in `src/submenus.rs`; leaves depend only on Slint and `nova-ui-shared`. The tracking leaf presents Settings → Integrations, including tracker accounts and the TMDB key editor; its internal section ID remains `settings:tracking`. Backend fields use `SettingsBridge` directly; shell-local keyboard/editor state and measurement use `SettingsContent`. |
| `nova-storage` | `crates/storage` | Platform-agnostic persistent store (redb) at `<data>/nova.redb`. Logical values remain JSON. Scalars and sync records stay in `kv`; registered Library/history collections and their baselines use independently encoded `state_rows_v1` rows (`collections.rs`), with descriptors in `kv`. Disposable metadata uses `metadata_cache_v1`; durable device-local counters use the typed `local_sequences_v1` table through `try_allocate_local_sequence`. Both binary payload tables use `codec.rs` (adaptive zstd level 3 with checksums). Equivalent JSON writes are skipped. Leaf, depending on redb, serde, serde_json and zstd. |
| `nova-media` | `crates/media` | Platform HTTP transport (`net`, optional `net/updates.rs` bounded GitHub requests with ETag and server cooldown support) + shared decoded/poster cache (`cache`), byte-budgeted decoded LRU keyed by URL and resolved processing recipe, single-file processed `.variant` envelopes with embedded usage metadata, batched disk recency and size eviction in `cache_gc.rs` behind an app-injected policy backend, JPEG/WebP encoding, optional resizing, atomic replacements and cancellable cache maintenance. Transient recipe publication tickets order overlapping loads/refreshes/maintenance independently of LRU residency; registration snapshot watches keep maintenance from replacing newer use registrations. Serde/JSON encode the versioned envelope metadata. Display fitting uses actual resized dimensions so Android Skia receives valid pixel buffers. Android builds bundled libwebp with the NDK CC/AR from `flake.nix`; artwork decoding is limited to JPEG, PNG, and WebP. |
| `nova-providers` | `crates/providers` | Normalized provider models and typed external catalog IDs (`ids.rs`), Stremio adapter, and sandboxed QuickJS runtime with bounded crypto helpers (`aws-lc-rs`). `registry.rs` dispatches immutable installed package snapshots; `metadata.rs` searches installed addons, proves weak catalog ties using bounded episode evidence, and caches identity/enrichment results; ordinary main-series details can add missing regular episode coverage from explicitly matching IMDb/TMDB TV parents; `metadata/coverage.rs` reconciles split/merged numbering, partial refreshes and source ID changes against established episode IDs, retaining stream ownership and watch progress; `metadata/cache.rs` persists bounded confirmed mappings through an app-injected storage backend; complete results refresh after 30 days and incomplete episode fields after five minutes; `metadata/cinemeta.rs` repairs official Cinemeta episode artwork using one-to-one normalized-title matches from its live endpoint (nearby air dates disambiguate repeated titles), preserving episode identities; `sequence.rs` shares forward/reverse episode alignment, recognizing explicit season/cour labels before subtitles so later entries can find a parent series with an earlier start year, and safely maps available prefixes of ongoing seasons, including an explicitly labelled first cour with a shorter declared total than the canonical season. Standalone later-season entries numbered as season 1 use their explicit title label only when complete regular counts prove a single season; forward streams and reverse enrichment share the rule without changing saved IDs or display numbering. `stremio.rs::normalize_detail_request_identity` normalizes returned canonical IDs only with explicit, nonconflicting catalog aliases and matching media type, including legacy Kitsu requests and common underscored ID fields; canonical evidence uses the existing `novaExternalIds` extension while episode IDs remain unchanged. `package.rs` validates bounded ZIP v1 packages, assembles explicitly ordered scripts and exposes declared assets. `registry.rs` serves installed `nova-provider://<id>` packages only; production sources live in the separate sibling `nova-addons/addons/<id>` checkout, while `test-fixtures/addons` remains test-only. `plugin_settings.rs` owns injected per-plugin preferences, immutable snapshots/generations, a bounded persistent TTL/LRU cache for discovery, verified stream lists and normalized metadata, plus an optional process-only pool. `torrent_metadata.rs` verifies bounded v1 metainfo. Dependencies include QuickJS, `aws-lc-rs`, `zip` and `semver`; no default addon repository or embedded JavaScript providers. |
| `nova-stremio` | `crates/stremio` | Official Stremio account/code authentication and library API with bounded HTTPS, opaque raw-library field preservation, watched-bitfield decoding/encoding, and pure field-level two-way reconciliation. Initial linking unions positive membership/history; subsequent edits use action timestamps and acknowledged baselines. App-side verified native bindings keep cold canonical imports virtual rather than duplicating saved titles/history; Home collapses existing cards only by current confirmed episode identity, preserving storage. No Slint dependency. |
| `nova-list-services` | `crates/list-services` | Bounded official Simkl, MDBList and Trakt APIs with injectable HTTPS transport, verified accounts, redacted credentials, Simkl device authentication/refresh and persistent incremental checkpoints, paginated library/history, exact episode watched state, playback/scrobble and MDBList static-list/collection operations. No UI or storage dependency; `src/api.rs` has fake-transport protocol regressions; `src/trakt.rs` owns current public-client device authorization/refresh, applied-limit pagination, exact episode scrobbles and a bounded public movie-summary cache for required canonical scrobble metadata. The crate README records API capability limits. |
| `nova-tracking` | `crates/tracking` | Local per-title anime tracking domain: account-scoped targets, explicit episode assignments, mapping validation, watched checkpoints, forward progress projection, semantic events (`journal.rs`), field-specific manual edits (`edits.rs`), completion prompts and grow-only handled receipts (`completion.rs`), and a durable progress outbox (`outbox.rs`) with account/mapping guards, attempt leases, retry backoff and service cooldowns. Versioned JSON persistence through `nova-storage`. `service.rs` defines tracker capabilities; `api.rs` implements bounded MAL REST and AniList GraphQL calls with injected transports; `auth.rs` implements native public-client OAuth, MAL PKCE/refresh and AniList manual token/PIN sign-in with a minimal authorization URL. Tokens are redacted and zeroized in memory; `credentials.rs` stores versioned plaintext tokens, restores verified accounts and persists MAL refresh rotation; `peer.rs` defines version-2 tracking links with intrinsic bulk provenance (version-1 explicit links remain readable), preferences and shared credentials and local-history projection. `callback.rs` captures bounded loopback returns; `cache.rs` bounds metadata results and expiry; `resolution.rs` ranks title/alias and release-context candidates and grades exact title/year evidence without activating mappings. Opening the Tracking page drafts coverage from the first ranked release for a verified connected service unless source IDs are ambiguous or only historical; Choose another release exposes alternatives before confirmation. Already linked active releases are omitted from automatic suggestions. |
| `nova-download` | `crates/download` | Durable stream-job model, manifest helpers, cancellation, progressive/resumable HTTP file transfers (`http.rs`, durable `.part.resume.json` validators; unvalidated partials restart; private resolver requests omit signed URLs/validators and restart partials), and finite HLS offline bundles (`hls.rs`, parsed with `m3u8-rs`). `capacity.rs` reads available download-filesystem bytes without creating files (Unix `libc::statvfs`, Windows `GetDiskFreeSpaceExW`). HTTP/HLS requests share bounded, cancellable HTTP 429 recovery; `httpdate` parses date-form `Retry-After`. `artifact_filename` uses `aws-lc-rs` SHA-256 of the download key plus a safe media extension, keeping disk/partial/validator names bounded independently of readable UI labels. |
| `nova-player` | `crates/player` | In-window mpv player (desktop + Android), source-guarded embedded chapter metadata (`chapters.rs`: validated starts and titles, `ChapterRow` model; timeline dividers, current/hover/scrub title and accessible Chapters submenu use existing release-only seeking without additional decoding or connections), live source-owned OSD facts (`media_badges.rs`, no filename inference), URL-free addon-resolution loading/error overlays (`wait_for_source` / `source_failed`, excluded from decoder timeouts), session-guarded subtitle-inclusive screenshots (`screenshot.rs`: asynchronous mpv capture, configurable lossless PNG/WebP or quality-controlled JPEG/WebP encoding, bounded portable show/season/episode/playhead filenames and optional show folders; `gpu_screenshot.rs`: Android HW+ native-display-size offscreen RGBA readback on the existing GL thread, with worker PNG/JPEG/WebP encoding (`png`, `image`, `webp`), cancellation and unchanged decoder/pause/position; private temporary staging, desktop Pictures/Nova and Android MediaStore/app-scoped Pictures publication), shared GL state isolation (`gl_state.rs`: Windows entry preparation and host restoration, Android host restoration; `glow` on both targets and Linux EGL tests), session-scoped Android renderer recovery (`android_recovery.rs`, desktop-runnable lifecycle tests; renderer recovery retains effective rate and semantic track preferences) and version-independent named `loadfile` options (`mpv_load.rs`, real-mpv paused load/reload regression). `PlaybackReadiness` exposes source-guarded native buffering/seekability/EOF and an approximate playable-ahead duration (local files use remaining duration). Normal window rendering disables mpv target-time blocking and uses zero video timing offset so Slint animation frames do not wait on video FPS. Android clears its content-FPS hint for active Slint animations as well as visible/fading OSD, then restores container FPS × playback speed for video-only playback; callback-driven idle redraws remain unchanged. `tests::measure_native_video_ui_composite_timing` is an opt-in baseline/current desktop timing diagnostic. `set_party_playback_rate` applies an in-memory 0.9–1.1× override above saved title/global preferences; new party sources load paused and retain EOF until the coordinator returns the room to its lobby, and clearing the override restores ordinary speed/load/EOF behavior. `subtitle_position.rs` retains the bottom-OSD clearance implementation using its measured viewport fraction and animated opacity, with runtime input disabled by `OSD_LIFT_ENABLED`; explicit subtitle position edits still replace the stored baseline; the retained render/restore logic preserves native ASS letterboxing and authored signs without forcing style overrides. `addon_subtitles.rs` exposes unloaded addon choices with session-local negative menu IDs and adds a selected local subtitle without reopening video; loaded files survive Android decoder recovery. `track_preferences.rs` parses mpv track metadata into semantic language/kind descriptors; the shared config matcher applies per-title or global audio/subtitle choices without persisting stream-local IDs. Automatic leaves mpv track selection in control; an unavailable preferred subtitle resolves to Off. Per-stream HTTP header and external subtitle arrays use `libmpv2-sys`; `crates/player/Cargo.toml` regenerates its bindings on 32-bit Android to match target pointer sizes and struct layouts. External video launch (`open_external`) is disabled at the desktop process boundary while progress tracking is reworked; Android retains video-MIME `ACTION_VIEW` for stream fallback and system *browser* (`open_browser` — `xdg-open`, or on Android `ACTION_VIEW` marked `BROWSABLE` + `FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK` so only web-link handlers can claim it, for links like addon config pages); Android JNI glue. |
| `nova-playback-api` | `crates/playback-api` | Serde-only shared contract for session IDs, media identities, opaque stream offers, commands, capabilities, playback snapshots and app handler/completion callbacks. Carries display/control state rather than source URLs, headers or credentials; no storage dependency. |
| `nova-cast` | `crates/cast` | Bounded mDNS/SSDP and manual receiver discovery, Google Cast through pinned oxicast, separate UPnP/DLNA control, explicit replacement and media ownership, cancellation and transport-only disconnect. No Slint or sync-peer dependency. `vendor/oxicast/NOVA_PATCH.md` records narrow scoped-IPv6 and diagnostic changes. |
| `nova-cast-media` | `crates/cast-media` | Route-bound, session-owned HTTP delivery with opaque resources, HEAD/single-range/CORS handling, origin-aware private upstream requests, finite HLS graph rewriting, WebVTT/SRT sidecars, preparation policy and cancellation/RAM limits. Depends on `nova-cast` for text-track contracts and `nova-remux` for inspection/packet copy. |
| `nova-remux` | `crates/remux` | Safe native FFmpeg inspection and bounded RAM fragmented-MP4 packet-copy production, plus finite-artifact regression helpers. A small C shim compiles against target-matching system/bundled headers, with custom AVIO callbacks, shared timeline anchors, timestamp checks, cancellation and fragment quotas; no audio/video decode/encode pipeline or FFmpeg executable. |
| `nova-party` | `crates/party` | Lazy independent iroh endpoint (`nova/party/3`) for temporary capability-invited parties, with no sync/pair/package protocols or persistent membership. `engine.rs` owns host-authoritative clock anchors, buffering barriers, finite disconnect grace, expiry, revocation and lobby-return on EOF; `service.rs` provides serialized foreground-worker create-lobby/join/select-media/clear-media/report/generation-guarded-command/leave APIs and nonblocking snapshots; `ticket.rs` bounds capability invites; `wire.rs` bounds frames. Each device resolves its own stream and keeps tracks/quality/preferences local. |
| `nova-torrent` | `crates/torrent` | Embedded BitTorrent (librqbit); resolves `infoHash` → loopback HTTP URL for mpv. The root manifest pins the merged `librqbit-utp` Windows UDP receive-error fix until it is released. |
| `nova-sync` | `crates/sync` | Cross-device sync over iroh (generic record store + ALPN protocols, zstd-compressed frames; `Store::resolved_progress` supplies read-only views for confirmed episode aliases using existing action clocks), plus separate bounded raw addon-package (`package.rs`) and ephemeral playback-control (`playback.rs`, `nova-playback-api`) transports on the same endpoint; emits structured `tracing` events/spans, with subscriber setup owned by the root app. |
| `addons` | `crates/addons` | Stremio addon protocol: URL builders + response parsers; optional blocking HTTP client (`client` feature). |

---

## 3. Root crate `nova` (`src/`)

### Entry points
- `src/main.rs` — desktop `main`; installs jemalloc on Linux, selects the GUI subsystem on Windows so launches do not open a console, and calls `nova::app::run()`.
- `src/lib.rs` — re-exports leaf crates under stable paths (`crate::storage`, `crate::player`, `crate::torrent`, `crate::download`, `crate::net`, `nova_ui::*`), declares `pub mod app`, and holds `android_main` (Slint Android backend init + `app::run()`).
- `src/version.rs` — display/base release identity and release-build eligibility from `build-support/version.rs`; debug assertions always disable automatic checks.
- `src/diagnostics.rs` — initializes the process-wide `tracing-subscriber` once without replacing an embedding subscriber. `RUST_LOG` filters events (default `warn,nova_sync=info`; `RUST_LOG=nova_sync=debug` enables sync diagnostics). Desktop writes to stderr; Android writes directly to logcat under `Nova`, including job-only startup. Sync leaf code emits structured events/spans through `tracing`; it does not install a subscriber.

### `src/app.rs` — the spine
Defines the shared state and the UI bridge:
- **`Shared`**: personal and view state in a distinct `Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<Shared>>` for each activation — installed addons, catalog/library/progress, Home rows, modal/stream state, settings, playback preferences, and Calendar/Downloads artwork. Device download settings and filters here are view mirrors. `SessionState` resolves the captured session and returns an owning lock guard; switching never overwrites an outgoing allocation.
- **`Bridge`** (`Clone`): weak window, `SessionState` and an `Arc<DeviceRuntime>`. Device resources include the physical player, downloads, poster pipeline, generations, usage scans and last-stream dispatcher; `AccountRuntime` retains account sync startup/recovery controls. Tracking/Stremio handles belong to `ProfileSession`, with old workers draining before another activation of the same profile starts. Every UI-mutating method runs on the main thread.
- Data model types: `Installed`, `CatDef`, `TypeDef`, `StreamSource`, `PendingStream`, `StreamUi`, `ModalItem`, `DetailSnapshot`, `EpisodeProgress` / `PlaybackHistory`, `PlaybackTarget`, `ContinueEntry`, `UpcomingEntry`, `LibraryEntry`, `AddonStore`, `WatchStatus`, `ViewingPhase` / `ViewingActivity` (`watch_state.rs`), `MetaHeader`, `CacheSettings` (from config).
- Poster pipeline: dual-priority `hi`/`lo` request channels and worker pool; image/cache ownership lives in `nova-media::cache`, while the app keeps request and UI publication state. Detail episode/season jobs use unique job-ID registrations and a shared four-slot pump; `page_artwork.rs` owns the separate four-worker Calendar/Downloads queue.
- Declares the `app` submodules (below) and re-exports their `pub(crate)` items.

### `src/app/*` module map

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `onboarding.rs` | Conservative device-local first-launch gate, resumable pending marker and durable completion. Probes fallible storage before startup defaults; existing databases without a pending marker and corrupt state never trigger setup. Completion flushes existing settings/sync persistence before the local marker transaction. |
| `desktop_window.rs` | Desktop-only local window memory (`desktop:window:v1`): first launch maximizes, later launches restore normal logical size and maximized state plus the separate playback fullscreen choice. The existing 250 ms UI tick debounces writes for two seconds; close/shutdown flushes. Minimized, playback and fullscreen transition dimensions never replace catalog geometry. Native desktop pointer visibility follows the OSD fade, activity and player exit, and remains visible for the fullscreen end menu. No sync domain. |
| `run.rs` | `app::run()`: window setup, renderer env (`SLINT_BACKEND=winit`, `SLINT_RENDERER=femtovg`), poster worker pool, **all callback wiring** (`app.on_*`), storage init, startup restore, `start_sync`. Desktop sync/torrent/download startup is queued after the first completed render; pending sync projection precedes initial models. |
| `bridge.rs` | `Bridge::new` / `Bridge::app()` accessor. |
| `profile_session.rs` | Account/device/session lifetime boundaries, stable activation state, retained provider authority and session-owned tracking/Stremio/Simkl/MDBList/Trakt handles. The directory drains canceled actors; UI callbacks resolve the selected session while captured continuations retain their original allocation. |
| Provider request ownership | `profile_session.rs::provider_inventory` captures packages, capability flags and enabled metadata addons from its own state/store. `crates/providers/src/registry.rs::OwnedProviderSnapshot` scopes registry and `metadata.rs` inventory reads; `crates/media/src/net.rs::fetch_bytes_with_progress_for_owner` retains that snapshot and settings authority through dispatch, progress and enrichment. Global configuration writes cannot replace a queued request's code or credentials. Media's unit fixture uses a test-only ZIP dependency to exercise public package parsing. |
| `addon_picker.rs` | Native local ZIP selection: desktop `rfd` dialog and Android `AddonImportActivity` document-picker/JNI bridge return bounded bytes to `Bridge::import_addon_zip`. |
| `addon_browser.rs` | Settings → Addons has Installed/Browse tabs. Browse reads Stremio's official JSON directory or a user-entered HTTPS directory manifest's advertised `addon_catalog`, with search, Catalogs/Streams/Subtitles filters, logo/name/description cards, installed status and explicit Install/Configure actions. Listing caches are in-memory per profile for 24 hours, fetches are limited to 4 MiB/500 entries, logos to eight concurrent workers, and source generations/profile contexts reject stale replies. Public URLs/manifests are validated; local-server entries are excluded. Custom directories show their declared name and a source link; no third-party community directory is bundled. Configure opens the existing system browser and asks the user to paste the configured install URL back into Installed; URL and ZIP install/sync stay on their existing paths. `nova_media::net::public_directory` shares desktop/Android TLS, requires HTTPS documents, validates each redirect destination and rejects HTTPS downgrades. Each hop resolves and pins only public addresses, rejects mixed private/public answers, and disables proxies and fallback DNS. At most four resolver workers run; queued logos wait within the shared 20-second request deadline. Logos have a 2 MiB body cap and strict 1024px source-dimension limits and an 8 MiB decoder allocation budget before raster decode; only 64px thumbnails enter the shared processed cache. No persisted keys or wire changes. |
| `addon_packages.rs` | Local ZIP validation/import, content-addressed archive registration, desired package descriptors, missing-package reconciliation and automatic paired-device transfer; see [addon packages](addon-packages.md). |
| `addon_mgr.rs` | Install/remove/refresh addons, manifest cache, addon picker rows, and the Settings → Addons row model (`apply_addon_rows`): transient `AddonRow.busy` and enabled/artwork updates preserve existing cards through manifest refresh, driving the Lucide refresh spin and disabling repeated activation (static when motion is disabled); external addons offer Configure after a successful `<addon base>/configure` probe and open the system browser. Installed packages declaring configurable capabilities instead open an in-page Streams/Metadata modal; `addon_config_*` AppWindow properties and `addon_capabilities_changed` in `run.rs` drive it. Effective manifests refresh after each choice, persisted as optional `AddonStore.capabilities` in existing addon JSON and `SyncedAddon.capabilities` in the existing sync domain (old installs default both on). Installed rows use shared phone/desktop artwork cards; manifest logos load through `nova_media::net::fetch_image` on workers using the shared image cache and platform transport (desktop and Android), with an uppercase name initial while missing or unavailable. Installed rows support transient name/description/URL search and manifest capability filters; contextual sources show a capability caption. Filtered actions resolve the row URL to the installed identity. `addon_copy_link` copies the install URL (`row.url`, still in the model). Publishes enabled/available addon snapshots to the metadata broker. |
| `catalog_grouping.rs` | App-side catalog projections, exact-owner display action registry, reversible raw-to-visible rebuilds, source chooser, profile settings/device overrides, the default-off `catalog_grouping_infer_series` switch and guarded visible-only metadata scheduling (two in flight, twelve attempts per view). Display markers never become native IDs or synced data. |
| `catalog_detail.rs` | Opening-owned virtual season view: exact metadata owner/generation, profile and native inventory/evidence fingerprints bind allowed episode IDs. Full metadata remains intact; only explicit season view/Watch Now/watch/download actions are scoped, with an All seasons escape for stale proof. Ordinary next-episode and tracking retain full native inventory. |
| `home_catalog_fetch.rs` | Home-only bounded raw catalog backfill: original mode fetches one page; enabled grouping can inspect at most three pages / 120 raw candidates per source, advancing raw cursors and rejecting repeated pages without full enrichment. Featured and row generations remain independent. |
| `catalog.rs` | Discover: raw catalog pages stay separate from display groups; raw-count cursors, repeated-page detection, three-page automatic backfill waves and an explicit Continue footer distinguish exhausted, failed and paused sources. Reprojection preserves focused source/season, viewport anchor and decoded images; poster deliveries guard display identity as well as generation/URL. Catalog fetch and pagination (failed requests preserve retry cursors; empty failures expose Retry), reuse of decoded Detail/saved Library poster URLs in browse and search, metadata prefetch that publishes missing poster artwork, and bounded revalidation of watched saved series even when their cached inventory is complete. Home/Library visits, addon readiness and a one-minute UI tick schedule at most four sequential titles per five-minute batch while foreground and visible; recently watched titles have a 30-minute cooldown, older titles six hours, with two slots reserved for overdue recent titles and the remainder rotating through unvisited/oldest deadlines. Manual statuses stay excluded; derived Completed/Ended does not prevent refresh. Requests share prefetch coalescing, retain known episode artwork/aliases, reject stale profile/source-generation or removed-title completions and rebuild Home through progress projection. Addon/type/catalog/genre pickers (browse inventory excludes catalogs requiring a search query and catalogs rejected by declared resource/type capabilities), independent temporary search filters, debounced search after 2 characters that preserves the editor’s trailing spaces, progressive per-source search results with stable deduplication and Unicode-aware title ranking, stale-query invalidation, independent search-result pagination, and device-local recent-search history (20 unique query/context pairs, most recent first; individual entries can be removed; addon/catalog/type/genre restore with same-addon then global fallback). Back cancels pending work and clears the query and its filters. Search visibility callbacks borrow retained previews and copy only typed identities for visible unloaded poster candidates, with row-ID and generation guards; addon descriptions/JSON are not cloned on scroll. |
| `detail.rs` | Opening Detail keeps cached content visible and rechecks metadata on workers for both series and movies; empty/seasonless caches count as missing so a fresh series response populates its episode picker; movies refresh independently of stream discovery. Search/featured/Android Detail entries reuse the decoded poster/header cache. On entry, `restore_modal_header_from_cache` prefers nonempty resolved detail text over catalog/library snapshots; header, cached episodes and the primary action are published before mounting. Cached episode preparation reads only the selected title; Detail queue/reminder flags do not rebuild the offscreen queue or Calendar, and Home shortcuts reuse the loaded episode inventory. Loading series offer Choose an episode until a target or a seasonless fallback is known; identical metadata responses retain genre-pill models. Missing or failed Detail posters use the shared mapped-artwork repair, with typed identity/current-URL guards and decoded-only URL persistence. Episode cache refreshes retain previously known artwork by stable native episode ID when optional enrichment returns no image; fresh nonempty URLs replace it. Episode/season completions paint their own pixels directly into matching stable-ID rows, independently of decoded LRU residency; opening tokens, current URLs/recipes and settings revisions reject stale deliveries. Rebuilds preserve already displayed pixels only for matching artwork identities. Detail opens at the top with fresh keyboard indices. Initial episode population selects the season with the latest nonempty watch progress (including completed episodes), falling back to a valid saved season or the first season; it stays on page zero without revealing an episode. Background metadata refresh preserves the chosen season, and explicit catalog virtual-season scope takes priority. Transient snapshots retain only tab/season/filter; the initial tab is selected before mounting, late episode metadata preserves the active tab, and only explicit keyboard navigation or playback-return reveals move the scroller. Detail modal: meta fetch, seasons/episodes (**paginated 50 per page** — `EPISODE_PAGE_SIZE`, page stored on the modal; row construction and episode requests cover only the current filtered page), fullscreen stream-selector state and origin-aware Back (`AppWindow.preserve_back_focus` anchors focus before teardown; Detail episode-search cleanup avoids focusing its covered page while the selector is open), last-watched episode restoration on selector/playback exit (season, page, filter and deferred card reveal), per-addon filter pills, pinned download rows; movie Start/Continue opens the selector beside the library action. Episode entry prefers an available thumbnail over the fallback poster; oversized cached player previews resize on a profile-scoped worker, with opening/request/generation/settings guards and no overwrite of a newer backdrop fetch. Streams render per addon as each answers (no wait for the slowest), reconcile the visible page by stable ID to preserve existing reveals and hold timers, keep configured installed-owner order in All and addon filters regardless of cache hits or response timing (each owner’s ranking stays intact), and the request's downloaded rows are pinned on top from the moment the search starts. Current addon ordering is reapplied on every stream publication and local/synced reorder; All and addon pills update immediately while retaining the selected addon. Stream identity and page follow the cursor only during active keyboard navigation; touch/pointer publications preserve the chosen page so late higher-priority results do not get skipped. |
| `calendar.rs` | Standalone library release planner: Month/Week/Timeline, show posters in Home’s 2:3 proportions (independent of Home’s episode artwork setting), future next episodes or all past/future releases (default), period counts, name search, stable release identities, viewport-driven artwork loading through `page_artwork.rs`, and backward/forward local date navigation (Timeline is a continuous schedule, loaded in 60-release batches around a selected date, with Earlier releases, automatic forward loading and Today reset). Reads existing cached episode metadata; no new persisted state. See [Calendar](calendar.md). |
| `downloads_page.rs` | Device-local Downloads page projection: series/season stacks, cached episode numbering and artwork, viewing filters/search/sort, movie/recovered-file groups, and transfer actions through the download coordinator. Stable group IDs plus requested/displayed URL-and-recipe identities preserve current images and allow cached replacements to replace nonempty older art; requests use `page_artwork.rs`. A retained input signature skips idle 250ms data rebuilds while keeping artwork retries active; it tracks download/data/image revisions, profile activation, language/day and page controls, and resets on reopening. |
| `page_artwork.rs` | Shared Calendar/Downloads artwork scheduler: transient typed URL/resolved-recipe identities, view generations, cancellation flags, URL deduplication, 30-second failed-request cooldowns and at most four worker/delivery slots. A shared completion acknowledgement holds each slot until every UI subscriber paints or discards its envelope, including failed event-loop dispatch; fanout shares one backing pixel buffer. Visibility callbacks preload one viewport above/below visible cards; offscreen or inactive models release images. Window overlay/return gates also deactivate jobs when route flags remain set; removing a Detail/player overlay reactivates the retained view and recreated delegates reschedule visible cards. Successful completions paint directly, even when the decoded LRU cannot retain them. No persisted state or additional retained pixel cache. |
| `season_downloads.rs` | Profile-owned ephemeral season and failed-job replacement plans. Sequential bounded stream listings, immutable primary and latest manual outlier release preferences, duplicate pack-source rejection, listing-only size estimates, actual storage capacity, review and explicit confirmation. Captured opening/profile/provider/settings identities guard all selections; cancellation never touches the queue or failed artifacts. Shared `DetailBridge` / AppWindow `season_download_*` fields and callbacks wire Episodes, the existing fullscreen selector and the compact review through page factories and `run.rs`. |
| `similar.rs`, `similar/tmdb.rs`, `recommendations.rs` | Profile-owned similar-title retrieval and Home's Because you watched catalog. With a configured TMDB v3 key, the Rust adaptation of Nuvio’s TMDB recommendations flow takes priority, resolves typed TMDB or IMDb identity without title search, preserves provider ordering and maps results to addon-compatible IDs. The masked key field is in Settings → Integrations and persists/syncs per profile as `CacheSettings.tmdb_api_key`. Without a key or successful TMDB results, explicit source recommendations take priority. Fallback separates animation from live action and requires anime evidence for anime seeds; it prioritizes Anime/Animation filters and verifies missing origin through the installed owner. Genres alone or one actor are insufficient: matches need a shared genre plus credited creators, multiple actors or substantive synopsis overlap including a shared phrase. Specific genres improve ranking only. Retrieval has an 18-fetch/20-second budget with TMDB (including IMDb conversion), otherwise an eight-fetch/12-second budget (up to four candidate origin checks), bounded responses and a 15-minute memory/local metadata cache. Matching cached rows publish without waiting for the network; refresh failures retain compatible results for up to seven days. Normal title opening hydrates compatible disk or memory results on a profile-owned worker and starts the bounded lookup regardless of the selected Detail tab or direct stream view, including Library and forced Overview navigation; catalog/episode prefetch does not fan out TMDB calls. Detail and Home subscribers share one in-flight lookup per title/configuration. Successful results retain their 15-minute TTL, empty attempts cache for five minutes, and any TMDB API/transport failure pauses further TMDB calls for that profile/key for one minute while installed-source fallback remains available. Detail publishes poster cards with opening/image-revision guards. Only a first uncached empty-to-populated delivery increments the transient `DetailContent.similar_reveal_request`; cached rows do not replay that entrance, while tab returns and poster updates remain immediate. Home uses up to three recent playback seeds and excludes saved/played/hidden titles. |
| `downloads.rs` | Durable one-at-a-time stream download queue, HTTP/torrent workers and explicit provider-resolver downloads, stable per-job torrent owners restored before cache cleanup, pinned-row state, auto-delete-watched, and the Settings → Downloads → Downloaded episodes list (`completed_episode_jobs`, with one typed-title metadata read per publication). Automatic watched cleanup validates current profile, watched/settings and playing-HLS authority at its claim boundary, then batches manifest persistence and artifact/torrent cleanup off the UI thread. Captures timing-provider coordinates and retains fingerprinted offline intro/recap metadata inside `DownloadJob` (`downloads:v1`); completion preparation, cache upgrades and removal reject cancelled/replaced jobs and late publications. |
| `episodes.rs` | Pure episode/progress helpers: ordering, labels, badges, filters, `progress_map_key`, and UTF-8-safe Gregorian release-date parsing (`iso_date`/`iso_days` shared by Detail dates); season card artwork prefers a season-specific backdrop and falls back to that season’s first episode thumbnail in episode-number order. The full-list helper supports dateless-only completion; the Completed bucket and library watched/unwatched toggle mean all dated released episodes watched, retaining "Caught up" playback text for an unaired tail. |
| `viewing_journal.rs` | Profile-owned synced dated viewing sessions and yearly recap. Measures active playback with monotonic samples, excluding pauses, buffering and seek jumps; stores durable per-session JSON checkpoints and separate episode/title completion dates. All-time/year filters aggregate full history, with UTC day/month allocations, rewatches and cached released-episode coverage for show completion. |
| `activity_sync.rs` | Per-scene bookmark records/tombstones and independent viewing-session checkpoints, atomically persisted with profile snapshots. Seeds existing archives, projects inactive profiles, and preserves profile retirement. Stats projection/decoding runs on workers; the journal merges incoming sessions while retaining newer active samples. |
| `bookmarks.rs` | Profile-owned synced bookmark archive grouped by typed movie/show identity, with search, latest-added/title sort, stable scene IDs, note editing/deletion and exact scene opening through the normal source selector. Remote scene opening binds the selected receiver offer’s Start response to its new session before issuing a guarded seek in `remote_playback.rs`; no wire fields are added. Owns the backward-compatible scene-note schema and bounded artwork publication. |
| `smart_collections.rs` | Synced profile-setting CRUD and automatic Library membership from watched state and cached genre/runtime facts. Existing device overrides apply to the whole rule set; current selection/editor state stays transient and resets on profile change. |
| `stream_filters.rs` | Profile-scoped synced offer rules with existing device overrides: resolution/size caps, exact-height resolution exclusions and ordered/required preferences, codec allow-list, ordered/required audio languages and explicit unknown-metadata policy. Empty preferred-resolution lists stay unrestricted; exclusions and the height cap win. Resolution sorting keeps preferred heights best-first before the selected numerical direction, without rounding nonstandard heights into tiers. Structured addon facts take priority over conservative advertised-text parsing; local downloads remain pinned and normal/automatic/remote-receiver selection uses the target device’s effective rules. |
| `watch_queue.rs`, `watch_queue/model.rs` | Profile-scoped typed movie/exact-episode plan with synced membership, coherent order/autoplay and durable cursor. Detail/episode options append; More offers individual play or explicit receiver-owned queue runs. Artwork and source work retain profile/identity guards. See personal workflow module map below. |
| `playback_tools.rs` | Profile-local scene bookmarks with optional notes and per-title secondary-subtitle preferences and Automatic/Top/Bottom secondary placement. Automatic placement inspects primary ASS events/styles where available, retaining manual controls and authored primary placement. The player menu and paired-device controller can seek/edit/delete current-episode bookmarks with compact right-aligned Lucide actions, keeps drafts on failed saves, and retries another offered source while retaining playhead and semantic audio/subtitle selections; native secondary rendering lives in `crates/player/src/secondary_subtitles.rs`, with source/track-scoped ASS placement caches in `secondary_subtitle_position.rs`. |
| `sync_inspector.rs` | Read-only Settings → Sync diagnostics: safe domain inventory, retained deletions, pending local application, peer exchange snapshot clocks and watched/unwatched action-register winners, including confirmed episode aliases. Exchange clocks are watermarks, not delivery receipts. |
| `watch_state.rs` | Shared library/Home activity classification and migration. `library_title_completed` uses a movie's native `id\x01id` playback record without episode metadata; Library filters, card status and Library/Detail watched actions share that completion rule, and completed cards omit the redundant Seen badge. `AwaitingReturn` distinguishes returning releases from an active backlog; `viewing_activity:v1` checkpoints sync independently of library entry edits and update atomically with local progress in `sync.rs`. Library/Detail offer Wait for new episodes / Resume watching for unstarted backlogs. Migration reads one title’s episode metadata at a time outside the shared-state lock, then borrows current history and validates typed identity to establish completion checkpoints. |
| `home.rs` | Home page: featured catalog banner, an ordered mix of built-in and addon catalogs, Continue Watching, New episodes and Upcoming rebuilds. Episode rails use small typed identity/latest-progress snapshots, read one title’s episode cache at a time outside the shared-state lock, borrow current history for decisions, and retain only row display fields; decoded image lookups occur after unlocking. Upcoming day rows retain their full-list action indices. Synced `CacheSettings.home_catalog_sources` picks the rotating banner; `home_rows` selects, orders and toggles built-in Continue Watching / Watch history / New episodes / Upcoming and addon poster rails (each section indexes its own contiguous card range, with native flicking and detail navigation; its See all header opens the shared Home grid subpage), addon sources are fetched from the device's enabled addons through shared `home_catalog_targets` / `home_catalog_fetch` helpers; featured and poster-row generation guards, cache limits and publication remain independent; Home grouping applies per contributing catalog before featured merging/rail limits, keeps bounded raw batches in memory and on disk, and preserves distinct virtual seasons even when their native title ID is shared; disabling or removing an addon immediately removes its cached featured titles and rails while desired unavailable installs retain their cache. Settings → Home manages every landing catalog together (visibility, up/down, remove, and one add picker including removed built-ins), while featured sources remain separate. `HomeRowSource` / `HomeRow` in `nova-config` lazily migrate legacy `home_row_sources` and the Continue/Upcoming toggles, preserving order and disabled built-ins; edits persist the new list and compatibility projections. Home publishes `home_catalog_order` and compact `home_catalog_positions` through AppWindow; section heading-to-card gaps are 12 logical px (4 px layout spacing plus 8 px rail inset); each grid cell wraps a measured layout in a Rectangle with explicit content height so captions and poster rails cannot collapse or overlap; its grid layout and keyboard traversal follow that order, omitting empty rows. Additional catalog results use the device-local `home:catalog-rows:v1` metadata cache and the standard image cache, so selections sync while fetched data stays local. The synced `CacheSettings.home_episode_artwork` toggle selects episode thumbnails or series posters for Continue/New episodes/Upcoming; new settings and older snapshots without a saved preference default to series posters, while explicit saved preferences remain unchanged. Missing episode images fall back to the series poster. New episodes (`HomeRowSource::NewEpisodes`, section ID/view `-3`, keyboard zone 4) offers awaiting-return titles separately, earliest unwatched dated release first, with an available count and newest-release sorting. Home revalidates New episodes rows against live watched progress at publication, pruning both displayed cards and action indices so a delayed pre-sync projection cannot restore a completed episode. Its removal snapshots the current episode IDs in `new_episodes_hidden:v1`; later releases surface the title again. Explicit custom Home layouts keep their selection and can add the built-in through the picker. On Hold/Dropped are omitted from all automatic episode rails. Continue Watching offers an active viewing run’s latest in-progress episode (whatever its air date — starting it was explicit), or — once the latest episode is finished — the first dated, released, not-yet-watched episode (via `next_episode_to_watch`, `episodes.rs`), so the next episode appears before it is started. Dateless episodes never surface on their own (no schedule to count down to); Upcoming shows only the nearest future episode per caught-up series (in its rail, grid and calendar); its caught-up check (`upcoming_tally`) skips them the same way, and the library "N left" badge counts dated episodes only (a started dateless episode still earns ▶ Resume, which outranks even "Caught up"). **Movies are covered too** (one entry while in progress, dropped once watched — they have no "next"): the per-item decision is the pure `continue_resume_id` helper (unit-tested here). Items the user removed from Home are filtered by `continue_hidden` (`id -> removal unix secs`): an id stays hidden while `hidden_at >= updated_at_secs`, so any later resume (local, or a synced peer's progress) brings it back, and stale stamps are pruned/rewritten by the legacy Home rebuild; manual-action workers apply the same clock comparison without writing a captured full map. Card menus (Play, Enter series, Remove from Continue Watching) dispatch `Bridge::continue_picked` (resume-and-play; the card tap too), `continue_enter` (open the detail page without starting playback) and `continue_remove`, each resolving the card back to its library entry. Triggers are Library-parity: each card owns its native `ContextMenuArea` for desktop right-clicks, while touch opens the page-level `MenuSheet` — whose own Cancel row closes it — from both a hold and a mouse right-click. |
| `library.rs` | My Library: entries, categories, persistence (`read/write_persisted_library`), lightweight filter/sort and card projections that borrow playback history (episode-cache reads outside the shared-state lock; single-item actions resolve typed identities from the painted card order and retain the clicked return index without re-filtering or sorting; Home opens preserve the existing Library return target; in-place playback badge updates share the card projection while retaining live poster buffers; poster dispatch carries typed identity and URL), similar-name duplicate suggestions and reviewed source replacement using current confirmed episode aliases or conservative title/start-year evidence for progress copying. Entering My Library prefetches only missing episode/header metadata (movies need header metadata only); complete cached entries keep their cache. Missing/failed posters separately run Detail's mapping/enrichment transport and retain fresh native episode aliases even with populated legacy caches. The Settings "Prefetch episode metadata" toggle gates Discover only (`catalog.rs`). Displayed sort/filter order gets priority; session-only typed prefetch state coalesces queued work and retries missing metadata after five minutes on success, one minute on failure, or a changed addon inventory. Manifests becoming ready retry an open Library. Full behavior matrix (buckets, badges, checkmarks, menus, categories): `docs/library-behavior.md`. |
| `watch_history.rs`, `watch_history_actions.rs` | Home Watch history projects up to 100 unfinished, meaningfully played titles outside the visible Continue Watching row, including partial rewatches. Chooses the latest episode per typed title before filtering completion, so finishing an episode never resurrects older partial progress. Hold/Dropped titles are absent from the resume row. Completed records remain in progress and in the explicit View watch history grid (`HomePage.view = -4`), with last-played dates; it is reachable from the landing footer even when the resume row is empty. New unfinished viewing runs qualify on Home after 30 observed playing seconds (pauses, buffering, seeks and suspension gaps do not count); legacy valid resumes remain eligible. Uses optional `EpisodeProgress.history` snapshots (including `engaged_secs`) for non-library titles, with cache-only metadata repair. Shares catalog poster cards, grid navigation and artwork publication via `home_history_archive_cards` / `HomeBridge.history_archive_cards`. History cards have desktop popup menus and touch hold/right-click sheets offering Play, Enter series and Remove from watch history; stable typed title identities and captured popup identities survive model reordering. Removals persist in `watch_history_hidden:v1` and sync per typed title in `watch_history_hidden`; newer genuine playback resurfaces a dismissed title without changing watched status. Exact episode/resume selection is scoped to one modal opening; late metadata never replaces it. Default/legacy Home layouts enable History after Continue Watching; explicit custom layouts retain their choices. |
| `playback.rs` | In-app playback flow, deferred addon resolution inside the loading overlay with request-scoped cancellation/errors and resume prompts, request-header forwarding, immutable source display/history snapshots and actual-playback timestamps, episode progress tracking (movies included: `open_player` arms a `PlaybackTarget` keyed to the movie's own id, and a resume clears any Continue Watching hide stamp), global-default and title-specific playback-rate methods (`apply_default_playback_speed` / `nudge_playback_speed`) and live-session updates. Title-specific speed edits are persisted by `playback_preferences.rs`; transient 2× hold previews restore the effective rate without saving. Torrent settings runtime cache. Captures separate text-only episode/title header fields for the OSD; screenshot callbacks run native capture on workers and publish short-lived localized notices only for the current player session. |
| `playback_end.rs` | Session-scoped ordinary EOF flow: waits five foreground seconds at actual native EOF, then offers Stay in player, Next episode (when a released successor exists), and Show details. Seeking, source replacement and close reset the wait; Stay or backgrounding suppresses that EOF until playback leaves the end. `run.rs` wires the three actions and refreshes after player/progress polling. `crates/ui/playback-ended.slint` / AppWindow render a motion-aware fullscreen menu over the dimmed loading artwork. The default-off `CacheSettings.auto_next_episode` field syncs independently with device overrides; verified bounded cached discovery precedes an eight-second fullscreen source preview, and ordinary `stream_picked` performs the successor handoff with an ephemeral start-over intent. No preview state persists. |
| `playback_actions.rs` | Shared UI-thread dispatcher above mpv for local controls and authorized remote commands, including chapters, bookmark actions, subtitle appearance, second-language subtitles, intro skipping and source recovery. Relative seek/volume/rate resolve on the receiver; transient preview release restores its current effective rate. Stop cancels pending resolution as well as active playback, and explicit close shares progress, torrent, brightness and Detail restoration cleanup. Ordinary EOF retains the paused final frame and seekable timeline; party EOF remains coordinator-owned. Controller role rejects local commands. |
| `party.rs` | UI-thread watch-party coordinator and local media binding; PartyBridge/AppWindow callbacks, single-worker foreground reporting, host timeline intents and transient player corrections.
