More opens the party page before playback; the player badge reopens only an active/connecting room. Creation opens an empty lobby with a shareable invite. Hosts choose/change titles manually from their current page, retaining navigation and filters, and publish the new media only once its local stream is loaded and seekable; the same room/invite/members survive title changes, player close and EOF. Existing connected members join each new title’s initial wait barrier; new arrivals during playback are admitted only once ready. Guests use their own Discover search and choose the same episode/edition. Confirmed episode aliases take priority; private-source title/number matching requires explicit source selection and an edition warning. Party sources bypass history/Ask prompts and open at the compatible host timeline. Setup cancellation restores the original source's pause state; Android foreground return waits for a fresh host update, overriding ordinary auto-continue and external-player recovery. Room state/invites are memory-only; remote-control roles are exclusive. Coordinator regressions live in `party_tests.rs`. |
| `remote_playback.rs` | Ephemeral paired-device playback coordinator: exclusive idle/local/controller roles (including loading), boot/session guards, receiver-owned stream lookup and opaque offers, explicit replacement confirmation, remote command queue and snapshots. The receiver uses its installed addons, downloads and preferences and captures screenshots to its own destination. More → Sync’s device-local `remote_control_enabled` (default true, excluded from settings sync) gates incoming snapshots, stream lookup/start and commands; opting out revokes queued receiver work/offers and remote speed holds while preserving local playback and normal sync. Foreground UI readiness gates service; background ticks never launch playback discovery or controller polling; detached starts preserve playback context while browsing. `wire_remote_playback` installs callbacks and `attach_playback_service` registers the UI adapter with the sync engine, and the 250 ms `run.rs` tick schedules foreground controls every 250 ms while playing/interacting or every second while paused, with immediate queued commands. Visible Home summaries poll every 30 seconds when idle or five seconds with a remote session; the Home section setting and page visibility gate this work. Devices/selector poll every five seconds and refresh on opening. Failed peers back off for 5/15/30/60 seconds, with manual Refresh bypassing backoff. One discovery fan-out attempts at most 32 peers, rotating oldest attempts and retaining skipped status; membership and engine identity guard late results. Pure cadence/backoff/cache regressions live in `remote_polling_tests.rs`. Live sessions are not persisted or synced; the transport boot counter is device-local. |
| `subtitles.rs` | Session-bound Stremio subtitle-addon discovery (three workers, bounded responses), candidate deduplication/language labels, lazy subtitle downloads and selection cancellation. Player menus share unloaded candidates with native tracks; preferred languages can load a matching addon subtitle after playback starts. `crates/player/src/subtitle_sync.rs` aligns downloaded primary and secondary tracks independently using live cues from the existing mpv connection. An embedded full-dialogue track visible in the other slot, a confidently aligned addon, or sufficient retained embedded-reference evidence supplies the anchor; the hidden secondary reference remains available when that slot is free. Two unaligned external tracks with no trustworthy anchor remain unchanged. Distinctive matching text can establish an offset early; cross-language timing correlation requires at least 90 seconds and agreement across both halves. One bounded worker performs matching without mpv/Slint access; source/seek/slot/track/reference-delay/manual-edit guards reject stale results. Inventory parsing follows track-list changes, and paused playback skips alignment polling. Corrections and manual ownership are per slot/track and stream-local; selected-addon Android recovery remaps both IDs atomically, without per-title preferences or sync changes. The existing delay editor shows the effective correction; an explicit timing edit replaces it with the viewer's absolute value and disables further matching, while restoring style defaults rearms alignment. Temporary `<cache>/subtitles/` files live only for the playback session and never enter sync. |
| `playback_preferences.rs` | Resolves the typed current movie/series identity and owns per-title OSD preferences for audio track, subtitles, speed, subtitle appearance and intro skipping (control 4). Each field saves independently for all episodes of a series or for one movie. Stores a flat JSON map at `playback_preferences:v1` with one entry per title/control; the same controls are synced in the `playback_preferences` string domain with per-control LWW values and reset tombstones. “Use global default” removes that title/control override. The OSD's effective speed is separate from the global Settings speed; a transient 2× hold preview never persists. Settings → Player language pickers edit the globally synced audio/subtitle defaults. |
| `stream_prefetch.rs` | Shared process-memory stream request cache and in-flight coalescing for the visible picker and speculative next episode. Successful nonempty responses without warnings retain at most five minutes, 32 entries and 16 MiB (2 MiB per response); settings/owner revisions reject stale reuse. Speculative completion also checks the foreground playback session and authoritative successor. Picker joins take ownership, avoiding duplicate requests or loss during playback close. No URLs, tokens or cache rows persist or sync. |
| `intro_skip.rs` | Public IntroDB/AniSkip intro/recap timings from current typed source IDs and captured episode coordinates; bounded background fetch, compatible-cut validation, logical/native session guards, shared seek action and synced `intro_skip_mode` (Off/Prompt default/Auto), with independent title override control 4 in the existing playback preference store/domain. Download completion probes local duration through `nova-player::probe_local_duration` and prepares timing metadata with bounded retries; offline restore revalidates saved intervals and duration. Valid IntroDB timings with confidence in 0–100% can offer a manual prompt; automatic skips use the synced, profile-owned `intro_skip_confidence` preset (Any/25/50 default/80/95%). `DownloadIntroSegment.confidence_percent` retains scored evidence offline (`null` for unscored providers); missing legacy scores retain the old 80% lower bound, and legacy `prompt_only` entries remain conservative. Dismissal is per segment/native playback session, suppresses later automatic skips of that segment, and never seeks or changes settings. |
| `profiles.rs`, `profiles/{store,storage}.rs`, `profile_actions.rs`, `profile_net.rs` | Account profile metadata/retirement, device-local active selection/startup toggle (`profiles:device:v1`, default on), explicit durable `ProfileStore` and scoped facade. Incoming state/settings/provider preparation precedes teardown and selection publication; package archives are restored before addon rows. Captured continuations retain their own session allocation; completion always runs, new canceled requests and stale UI publications are rejected. Personal keys/domains use `profile:{id}:{logical}`; pairing/Sync settings and device resources remain shared. See [Profiles](profiles.md). |
| `next_episode.rs` | Session-scoped end-of-episode offers: final two minutes capped at 10%, sequential regular successor selection, stale action guards, and progress finalization before opening the next episode’s streams by ID. The default-enabled `CacheSettings.prefetch_next_episode_streams` toggle appears in Settings → Player for the built-in player; it warms the same verified successor up to 30 seconds (another 5% for short episodes) before the offer, with per-session/episode/settings/owner deduplication and pause/background guards. Behavior in [player behavior](player-behavior.md). No persisted offer or prefetch state. |
| `cache_gc.rs` | Local DB cleanup markers, the media GC backend and Library artwork URL preference. `run.rs` retains the worker until shutdown; settings changes and Android resume wake cheap maintenance gates. Provider expiry scheduling uses the same marker helpers from `plugin_cache.rs`. |
| `last_stream.rs` | One shared last-selected stream fingerprint in the `last_stream` domain (`selection` key), seven-day expiry, bounded cache-file reads and asynchronous atomic cache writes/expiry. Startup migrates the old local file with its historical clock, restores the record owner even with networking off, and remote projection updates an open selector without echo or autoplay. `streams.rs::stream_choice_identity` hashes owner and movie/episode scope independently of presentation; `detail.rs` promotes a returned match after downloads and publishes `StreamRow.last_used` through the existing stream model bridge. A separate device-local `series-stream-v1.json` cache retains up to 128 show-scoped release preferences for 90 days, with bounded reads and the same asynchronous atomic writer; it stores hashes and recognized technical facts, never playback URLs or release text. Explicit usable episodic picks (including native anime types) update a type-qualified show scope independently of other shows. |
| `persistence.rs` | Ordered background Library/progress serialization and save queue, pending snapshot read visibility, periodic-checkpoint coalescing, retry retention and pause/shutdown/sync flushes. Jobs retain a durable `ProfileStore`, not an activation; coalescing includes that owner. Registers authoritative collection policies; `run.rs` starts the process writer after migration/projection and retains it through shutdown. |
| `progress_projection.rs` | Coalesced profile-owned Library/Home follow-up for watched edits and episode metadata changes. Whole-library alias reconciliation stays off the title-opening path; metadata cache writes queue the guarded worker, and pending history writes are flushed there. Detail only restores a late proven opening-season anchor while that opening remains untouched. Detail patches existing visible episode/season models and loaded Watch queue flags immediately; the worker waits for the ordered progress writer, snapshots in short lock batches, prepares Library/Continue/New episodes/Upcoming/history data with bounded typed metadata caching, and publishes current results without metadata I/O. Profile, request/data revisions, settings and Library view checks reject stale deliveries. Successful relevant snapshot/cache writes invalidate pending work through `sync.rs`; queued Library/progress edits invalidate before persistence. Legacy startup/root-page refreshes keep their existing synchronous path. No new persisted state or sync schema. |
| `posters.rs` | Poster / backdrop / episode-thumbnail image pipeline (desktop priority queue + Android fetch path). Successfully decoded Detail/prefetch posters update matching Discover browse/search and Library rows by media type/ID, including URLs retained through scrolling; late image completions check the current requested URL. Missing or failed Discover browse/search and saved poster URLs trigger shared, cooldown-limited metadata recovery independently of episode prefetch and populated episode/header caches; repair tries enabled metadata sources until a poster decodes, including current nonconflicting mapped IDs routed only to their owning addons (bounded to 16 visited endpoints), rather than stopping when episodes are found; pending manifests do not spend repair cooldowns and becoming available retries only queued missing URLs or confirmed failures, never merely unloaded rows; a distinct cached poster is tried before metadata; Library visits skip loaded posters and coalesce pending image loads; recovery also caches enriched native headers/episode aliases so Continue/Upcoming consume the same metadata. Recovered URLs persist and refresh Library, Home and a matching Detail view; clearing image cache then hitting an expired saved URL runs this same repair. Library image completions resolve current identity/URL after model reordering. Home cards use the synced `home_episode_artwork` setting to choose episode thumbnails or series posters, with missing episode art falling back to the series poster; this is separate from the episode-list `show_unwatched_thumbs` setting. The Home worker retains identity/load/failure state only; Slint owns live/current/crossfade pixels, reacquiring evicted current images through generation, identity and revision guards. Detail has its own recipe-scoped request and cannot replace a different grid use. Detail episode and season thumbnails share at most four workers; episode jobs cover only the current 50-row filtered page. Job-ID registrations distinguish queued cancellation from running requests, and completion slots remain occupied through UI delivery to bound pending pixel buffers. Successful pixels paint current row identities directly; opening tokens, current URLs/recipes and settings revisions guard publication. `fetch_episode_home_poster` shares worker/UI delivery, image-settings revision retry and failed-artwork recovery for Continue/New episodes/Upcoming, while each rail retains its row matching and Upcoming day propagation. Cached posters remain visible while thumbnails load; asynchronous completion checks both series ID and selected artwork URL, and Upcoming also refreshes matching selected-day cards. |
| `updates.rs` | Feature-gated foreground GitHub release coordinator. About supports manual checks and a device-local automatic-check toggle; debug and untagged/dirty development builds skip automatic checks. Successful checks cache for one day, failures back off 15 minutes, and server cooldowns also gate manual requests. A nonmodal bottom-right notice defers around playback/onboarding/dialogs and opens the GitHub release page; automatic notices show at most once per day. State is stored only at `updates:v1`; no sync or installer. `crate::version` exposes build identity from `build-support/version.rs`: matching clean release tags use `v<version>`, development uses last reachable version tag plus short commit and optional dirty suffix. |
| `about.rs` | Standalone About navigation; flips page flags without mounting Settings or refreshing its storage/service models. License models are loaded once by `run.rs`. |
| `settings.rs` | Settings capture/persistence, `update_control_rows` for identity-preserving Addons/plugin configuration/Home catalog switch updates, torrent settings, episode resume behavior, and application-owned cache maintenance workers on desktop and Android. Settings → Player has language search pickers for globally synced `default_audio_language` and `default_subtitle_language`, plus globally synced `playback_speed`; these use the existing settings record. `keep_controls_visible_while_paused` uses the same settings record and device-override path, preserving Android on / desktop off defaults for missing fields. Image-use resolution dropdowns and compression controls autosave immediately, support search/keyboard/touch, and stay available when disk caching or compression is off; a re-encode snapshot fetches each source URL once, rebuilds registered uses from current recipes, and supports cancellation. Cache maintenance reports progress/final counts; clear removes all image variant files; usage readouts use coalesced worker scans only while Settings is mounted. |
| `plugin_settings.rs` | Explicit `ProfileStore` adapter for provider settings and detached incoming-profile preflight, including retained-record recovery. Per-field sync and `ApplyingGuard` projection retain owner identity; provider capabilities include originating addon/package/settings revisions. Addons gear fields include masked inputs, explicit Save/Enter, independent cache clearing, and confirmed configuration removal across paired devices. |
| `settings_sync.rs` | Setting-scope models and override callbacks. Device-local `CacheSettings.sync_overrides` retains fallback shared values; enabling starts from the current effective value, disabling restores the latest account record. UI changes/persistence use `ApplyingGuard` so policy changes do not echo. Integrations exposes the standard sync badge/comparison/device-override controls for `tmdb_api_key`; `backend_text::setting_sync_value` masks configured keys in both local and shared comparison values. Tracking’s automatic preference is labelled Synced and stays sync-only; its stored client registrations remain device-local and are passed internally to sign-in, without exposing ID/redirect fields in account cards. |
| `streams.rs` | Stream-row mapping (`StreamSource`), bounded Stremio request-header/subtitle extraction, and display-text helpers. Explicit Stremio `externalUrl` rows become validated browser-only `StreamSource::ExternalUrl` destinations before media/hash/resolver classification; ordinary `url` fields remain in-app media regardless of addon names or domains. Browser rows hide size/transfer UI, offer only Open in browser, and cannot enter recommendations, next-episode autoplay, downloads or receiver offers. `StreamCardText` separates release titles from addon/quality and descriptive metadata; size pills prefer numeric addon hints or known download totals, falling back to advertised text sizes. Saved download text stays unchanged. `StreamPreference` compares the same addon’s hashed binge/release family and recognized resolution/codec/source facts; completed downloads with present artifacts also qualify, using retained release text across the local/online boundary, and `play_download` remembers that show preference; `recommended_stream` requires family evidence or all three technical facts, retains addon ranking on ties and defers to an exact Last used match. Detail promotes one confident recommendation after downloads, passing `StreamRow.recommended` to the selector’s translated Recommended caption and title star; playback stays manual. |
| `tracking.rs`, `tracking/ui.rs`, `tracking/worker.rs`, `tracking/setup.rs`, `tracking/peer.rs`, `tracking/background.rs`, `tracking/completion.rs` | Bounded tracker actor handle and semantic history journal; UI callbacks capture stable source context and enqueue commands. Fresh tracker IDs replace older source claims, which remain bounded historical discovery hints. Ambiguous or historical IDs expose verified release choices before alignment; explicit suggestion reload bypasses the tracker search cache. The worker owns OAuth sessions, public registrations, metadata resolution/cache, explicit coverage confirmation/repair, local state, event consumption and serialized remote delivery. `tracking/peer.rs` publishes durable local changes, guards automatic credential rotation against newer shared records or disconnect tombstones with an atomic local/shared/baseline commit, atomically applies shared links/credentials, pauses conflicting coverage, and acknowledges the captured tracking-domain digest. `tracking/background.rs` owns overlapping Android sign-in service leases through callback reception and token persistence. `tracking/completion.rs` owns stable-token rating drafts, mapped-release grouping, score-only decisions and handled-receipt publication/projection. Explicit AniList/MAL linking can offer a rating for an already-watched finished local release without accepting old progress for upload; implicit refresh/peer links do not create these prompts. UI publication hops onto the event loop with generation guards; shutdown invalidates sessions. Read failures require explicit backed-up recovery and do not block playback. |
| `stremio.rs`, `stremio_apply.rs`, `stremio_selection.rs` | Worker-owned Stremio account connection, initial merge preview, durable reconciliation/retry state, shared-credential verification and UI delivery. Materialization prepares title-sized patches off-thread; optimistic clock/native-state checks and nonblocking locks reject stale or busy UI delivery. The ordered persistence worker saves accepted patches before acknowledging them. Unchanged projections skip writes; visible models refresh once per changed batch, with hidden Home/Library work deferred until reveal and Detail refreshed only for affected identities. Settings → Integrations uses code/browser sign-in; foreground polling and local mutations wake the actor. Statuses retain the failed phase and numeric HTTP/API errors; local imports use fallible snapshot writes. Full-library mode remains the legacy default; selected-title mode gates imports, cached work and delivery on explicit account/native bindings. `stremio_selection.rs` owns catalog search, reviewed episode mappings, per-title exclusions, legacy defaults and consent checks. See [Stremio sync](stremio-sync.md). |
| `list_services.rs`, `list_services_trakt.rs`, `list_services_tests.rs` | Profile-owned Simkl/MDBList/Trakt actors with verified account credentials, selected/full-library scope, per-title exclusions, guarded additive library/history merge (individual API mutation rejections stay pending without blocking later titles; cancellation/authentication/rate-limit/storage failures still abort), real-duration resume checkpoints and incremental Simkl state; `list_services_trakt.rs` adds browser device sign-in, guarded refresh rotation and scoped real player start/pause/stop observations that suppress competing bulk writes; retained native EOF stops once, seek/replay rearms, and invalid items cannot block unrelated queued titles. Per-device semantic watch/grant boundaries prevent old or paired history from gaining upload authority. Controls have a reliable cancellation lane; ordered external patches recheck durable scope and native snapshots. The six per-title UI fields connect once through persistent `DetailBindings`; conditional `DetailHost` remounts must not relink them. See [list-service sync](list-services.md). |
| `stremio_events.rs` | Atomic Stremio source-event clocks alongside library/progress persistence; external-origin guards prevent downloaded history from acquiring fresh local upload authority. |
| `stremio_metadata.rs` | Worker-only metadata adapter using immutable native snapshots and the account’s installed addon descriptors. Missing/stale native aliases can refresh through enabled Nova sources and the ordinary provider metadata broker under a shared fetch/enrichment deadline. Exact episode IDs and current confirmed aliases authorize each independent native binding, even for equal parent IDs; native inventories remain intact. Partial seasons retain complete remote inventories and unmapped progress. Snapshots refresh on native identity/alias changes. Unsupported/ambiguous identities remain pending with reasons and ordinary type/ID diagnostics. No addon installation or title-only matching. |
| `media_identity.rs` | Pure cross-provider episode equivalence from cache inventories and received global episode history. Current, one-to-one IMDb/Kitsu episode aliases share progress across saved parent IDs; opaque IDs stay scoped and stale/ambiguous/contradictory mappings do not join. Complete equal episode coverage identifies duplicate Library/Home representations while partial-season releases stay distinct. Native playback IDs, stored records and tombstones remain unchanged; no new persisted keys or sync domains. Library display groups memoize episode-proof and provider revisions, not playback/settings revisions; accepted background projections warm the same guarded UI cache, avoiding another metadata scan after watched edits. |
| `sync.rs` | Cross-device sync integration: atomically decompose/persist app state into records, request automatic sync only after actual successful local mutations, apply remote records under `ApplyingGuard`, More → Sync UI, pairing events. `sync_reconcile_progress_aliases` materializes current one-to-one episode aliases from device-local caches at startup, after sync and after metadata delivery, so different native IDs agree on watched/resume state; its guarded snapshot/baseline writes do not mint actions or alter wire records. Explicit Watch/Unwatch gestures carry an optional device/boot/sequence marker in local JSON progress so rapid opposite actions under different provider IDs retain their actual order, including same-value reaffirmations; projections and ordinary checkpoints retain that marker without creating a new action. Ambiguous/stale aliases and native tombstones cannot restore history. Remote image-recipe revisions rebuild shown Calendar/Downloads identities and current Detail season/page/hero requests once after the batch; unrelated settings/progress projections retain their lightweight path. Global audio/subtitle language defaults and playback speed use the existing settings record; per-title audio/subtitle/speed overrides use the separate `playback_preferences` string domain with independent LWW clocks and reset tombstones, without a wire-schema or ALPN change. |
| `qr.rs` | Shared QR encoding of sync (`NV1`) and watch-party (`NVP2`) invite tickets: renders the original ticket to a Slint image (`image-rendering: pixelated`) for desktop and Android. The Android scanner decodes the same payload, so display and scan agree by construction. |
| `android_bg.rs` | Android-only JNI glue (`#[cfg(target_os = "android")]`): starts/stops/updates the download foreground service, starts/stops the independent browser sign-in foreground service, and schedules the periodic sync `JobScheduler` job; hosts the headless sync JNI entry. Android API calls live in `android/java/`, so Rust only loads a class through the Context class loader and calls a static method. |
| `casting.rs` | External Google Cast/DLNA output coordinator, independent of paired-Nova control: session/source/profile generations, connect-first target selection, source and RAM delivery leases, verified pause/position handoff, ownership-safe recovery, coalesced commands, receiver progress/queue observations and UI projection. A driven worker runtime owns receiver control and media resources while browsing or Android is suspended. Shared models and actions travel through `CastBridge`; see [casting behavior](casting.md). |
| `android_cast.rs` | Cross-platform service contract with Android JNI implementation: generation-owned `CastServiceLease`, immediate cancellation for notification Stop, coalesced desired-state commands and discovery multicast leases. `NovaCastService.java` owns a private `connectedDevice` foreground service, bounded wake-lock heartbeat, notification/MediaSession controls and network callbacks. Last lease release tears down only its own platform generation. |
| `android_qr.rs` | Android-only JNI glue (`#[cfg(target_os = "android")]`): launches `QrScanActivity` and decodes each camera frame's luma plane with `rqrr`, then joins the scanned invite on the UI thread. The camera pipeline itself (permission, Camera2 session, preview, `ImageReader`) is Java. |
| `io.rs` | KV JSON read/write, explicit local metadata cache key selection, atomic file writes, hashing, formatting. |
| `clipboard.rs` | Best-effort system clipboard writes (`copy_to_clipboard`): Android via the activity's `ClipboardManager` over JNI (`player::set_clipboard`), Windows via native `CF_UNICODETEXT` ownership transfer, Linux via `wl-copy`/`xclip`/`xsel` (close stdin and require successful exit before accepting a helper), macOS via `pbcopy`. Used by More → Sync "Copy identity"/"Copy invite code" and Settings → Addons "Copy link". |
| `i18n.rs` | UI language (Settings → Display → Language): `apply_language` points Slint's bundled catalogs at the stored language (`slint::select_bundled_translation`) and hands the same setting to `nova-ui::backend_text`; it also refreshes already-materialized Rust text, including remote device statuses and playback phases/prompts without reconnecting. `language_labels` builds the picker list from `nova_config::Language::ALL`. |
| `tests.rs` | Unit tests for the app modules (moved out of `app.rs`). |

The Home landing also has an independent featured-showcase fetch: Settings →
Home stores selected addon URL/type/catalog-ID/genre entries. Its Add catalog
dialog offers enabled catalogs and only the genre options declared by each
catalog; the configured list contains only the user's selections. `home.rs`
restores device-local catalog batches (`home:showcase:v1`) before the first
frame, including while an enabled addon's manifest is loading. Live fetching
requires the matching addon to be available. Each selected catalog contributes
at most five distinct displayed titles; the cache keeps enough extra candidates
to deduplicate overlapping catalogs. Failed requests retain that catalog's last
successful result. Refreshed batches are persisted immediately but wait in
`Shared::home_showcase_refresh` until the next automatic/manual carousel step.
Navigation anchors to the current item's identity in the new order; artwork
uses the existing memory/disk image cache and the next target is prefetched.
Sparse previews hydrate through ordinary meta endpoints for the current and
next slide (two active chains, up to four endpoint candidates each), preferring
the catalog owner and enforcing requested ID/type. Posters serve as artwork
fallbacks when backdrops are absent or fail; richer fields survive sparse
refreshes and are saved back into the Home cache. Failed images/partial metadata
retry on rotation or a Home revisit after a 30-second cooldown. In-flight URL
checks keep obsolete images from clearing a newer backdrop.
Separate metadata/list generations reject stale catalog and artwork replies.
Details/Watch now keep the displayed preview as their target while replacement
art loads. Catalog/genre selection syncs with Settings; result caches stay local
and never display disabled/removed addons or another genre's results. The
featured row does not alter Continue Watching or Discover's catalog state.

More keeps Calendar and Downloads first on phones, followed by Profiles, Sync,
Devices, Watch party, Settings and About. Sync is account-wide and uses the
existing separately compiled Settings shell in `standalone_sync` mode; its
overview/search entry is hidden. The shared More entry/return transitions cover
Sync and the other routed subpages; Profiles has its own reversible transition.
Theme’s default-on `transparent_navbar` overlays the phone bar with a dark gradient;
Home, Discover, Library and More reserve scroll-content end space and keyboard
reveal room so their last items remain accessible. The sidebar stays opaque;
Theme disables the bottom-bar-only toggle in sidebar layouts and keyboard
navigation skips it, while keeping the saved preference for narrow layouts.

### Personal data, planning and session policies

| Module / UI | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `backup/{archive,adapters,ui,mod}.rs`, `documents.rs`, `backup.slint` | Settings → Data selective profile/component backup, bounded checksummed ZIP/JSON, semantic Merge/Replace preview, atomic sync-store import, durable activation receipt and three retained compensating recovery snapshots. Platform document adapters use desktop dialogs or Android SAF `DocumentActivity`; package bytes reuse existing archive staging. |
| `watch_queue.rs`, `watch_queue/model.rs`, `watch-queue.slint` | Profile-scoped v2 membership/order/cursor with atomic v1 migration; receiver-owned explicit queue runs reuse existing EOF source discovery/countdown. Item membership and order are independent records; remote cursor changes never retarget active playback. |
| `smart_offline.rs`, `headless_profile.rs`, `smart_offline_android.rs`, `smart-downloads.slint` | Synced per-show next-N intent with device-local opt-in, shared transfer ownership/storage reservations, metered-network/power constraints, durable retry/suppression and eligible automatic cleanup. Explicit background profile/provider capabilities avoid publishing UI sessions. Smart downloads retains its Downloads list beneath a slide/fade subpage on entry and return, with shared header Back (including TV focus), input/repeated-Back guards through return, and immediate completion when page motion is disabled; `OfflineBridge.visual_retained` coordinates the window Back guard. |
| `crates/download/src/offline.rs`, `crates/torrent/src/storage_guard.rs` | Physical byte/reservation accounting and admission; HTTP/HLS pre-write limits and torrent storage middleware enforce automatic budgets through the existing coordinator. Manual/shared owners protect media from automatic eviction. |
| `release_reminders.rs`, `release_reminders_android.rs`, `calendar.slint` | Synced subscriptions, local daily notification receipts, shared inventory refresh, typed calendar identities and portable export ledger with stable ICS UID/sequence/cancellation. Android OS notifications are device opt-in; desktop uses an in-app digest while running; file exports are snapshots. Detail’s top-right options and Calendar expose per-title episode/full-season modes; complete-season notices require an explicit count/finale and complete dated inventory. |
| `sleep_timer.rs`, `crates/player/src/sleep_timer.rs`, `sleep-timer.slint` | Session-local elapsed/end-item/episode-count timer and receiver controls. Suspend-inclusive native deadline enforcement works independently of Slint; EOF priority is explicit Stop, sleep, party, queue, then existing series autoplay. |
| `profile_policy.rs`, `profile-pin.slint` | Plaintext numeric PIN and coherent profile policy record, per-flow transient management grants, entry/exit gates, typed-title/source-addon allowlists and shared UI/backend eligibility checks. |

These workflows extend the existing shared UI bridges and factory pages; `run.rs`
wires callbacks on the UI thread. Android document/job services are declared in
Cargo Android metadata, with Java adapters in `android/java/dev/misob/nova/`.
Source and lifecycle details live in the corresponding feature behavior docs.

### Threading model (important)
- **All Slint property/callback access is main-thread only.** `Bridge` methods are called from UI callbacks on the main thread.
- Background work (HTTP, decode, torrent, sync) runs on worker threads or tokio runtimes and hops back with `slint::invoke_from_event_loop(...)`.
- Installed JavaScript packages run in a fresh QuickJS runtime on the fetch worker. The runtime bounds memory, stack, execution time, response size, redirects, request count, and domain-scoped HTTPS. Its host APIs provide GET/permission-gated POST, three-way GET batches (including `batchEach` completion callbacks on the invoking QuickJS thread), per-request soft timeouts within a shared operation budget, CSS and safe XML selection, bounded v1 torrent inspection, session storage, durable scoped settings/cache, redacted logging, and encoding helpers. Resolver selections run on workers with the existing playback request token, addon revision and settings generation; Android suspension cancels pending resolution. HTTP bodies, redirect origins and public DNS addresses are checked before requests. The optional manifest `limits.scriptTimeMs` defaults to 5 seconds and is capped at 10 seconds; Supernova uses 10 seconds for matching/metadata/sorting while AniKoto retains 5 seconds. The overall operation deadline still applies. Only actual blocked time on the invoking thread is excluded from the script-processing budget; concurrent IO never credits executing callbacks. `nova.streams.publish` sends bounded replacement snapshots during stream lookups; registry validation matches the final response before `nova-media::net::fetch_bytes_with_progress` forwards them to the app. Ordinary remote addons remain final-only.
- HTTP text and binary responses have separate representations (`body` vs `bodyBase64`), avoiding duplicate large buffers. Installed provider warnings use bounded `novaWarnings` alongside ordinary stream rows; typed resolver errors never expose upstream response bodies. Credential-bearing final playback links require manifest-declared HTTPS CDN/path/query permissions. The host derives `novaSensitivePlayback`, and `PlaybackIntent`/`nova-player::play_with_preferences_and_policy` retain in-app-only playback through resume prompts and Android decoder recovery; links are never persisted or synced.
- Providers may opt into `capabilities.streamPreferences` for an ephemeral owner-scoped `request.streamPreferences` snapshot. `nova.streamPreferences.accepts/rank/compare/sort` applies the shared `nova-config` policy; context fingerprints isolate both provider caches and late publications. `src/app/plugin_settings.rs` publishes the active profile/activation's in-memory policy before debounced settings saves; inactive owners read their own durable snapshot. Supernova applies resolution exclusions, preferences and strictness with the other shared rules before candidate/result caps, hides duplicate quality/audio/codec controls behind a Settings → Player summary, and retains source, credential, subtitle and remaining sort controls. Older Supernova packages receive a transient compatibility overlay without changing synced plugin settings.
- Supernova currently declares `domains: ["*"]` for HTTP and final playback, allowing all public domains. Other providers retain scoped domain lists. HTTPS/public DNS/private-address checks, operation limits and authenticated redirect restrictions remain enforced independently of this wildcard.
- Supernova adds opt-in Nyaa, AnimeTosho, Knaben v2, StremThru and The Pirate Bay / APIBay sources; the clearer Pirate Bay label preserves the stored `sources.apibay` preference. Knaben uses its public GET API and bounded advertised pagination, ignoring untrusted download proxies in favor of hash-verified metainfo. StremThru ports Comet's Torznab inventory query through a configurable public HTTPS URL, using an IMDb ID when available and the official title/year/category fallback otherwise; safe upstream labels retain `StremThru|Knaben` provenance. The Nyaa/AnimeTosho adapters port Comet's broad title discovery and request/parsing patterns, following advertised pagination up to six canonical-title pages and two episode-query pages, with an AnimeTosho JSON fallback. `matching.js` separates numeric title suffixes from episode ranges and admits compatible season inventories only for exact-file inspection. A 20-second lookup and 48-request ceiling retain at most 300 ranked candidates per wave, while visible results stay capped at 50. Configurable priorities use only cached availability, parsed resolution, bytes, season-pack labels and reported seeders; missing values stay last, stable row IDs break ties, and every combined TorBox/P2P snapshot sorts before the display cap. The existing preferred quality still biases the bounded discovery/inspection pool. First-page TorBox availability publishes before deeper discovery; subsequent waves check only new hashes, and better later releases may replace earlier candidates. Native three-way completion callbacks publish cumulative cached rows and then verified P2P rows without waiting for every request. Cache-hit metainfo retries retain the original discovery expiry; failed empty searches are not retained. Exact-context nonempty, warning-free lists also persist in the OS cache directory for up to 15 minutes (`nova.cache.get/set`), bounded by the original discovery expiry; lists with pending P2P file inspections bypass this cache so retries can proceed. Reopens, including after restart, avoid HTTP while TorBox selection still resolves afresh. Each TorBox availability batch proves and renders each candidate once rather than repeating that work for cumulative snapshots. Unverified candidates and direct torrents when P2P is disabled are omitted without status messages in the stream picker. Shared release evidence enriches language/codec/resolution ranking and presentation from Nyaa descriptions, optional APIBay details, Knaben descriptions and StremThru descriptions/media attributes; AnimeTosho retains native Nyaa references only for enabled-source detail fetching. The generic parser recognizes labelled inventories, tables and MediaInfo, ignores prose/categories/links/comments, and leaves conflicting scalar claims unknown. Source-owned detail IDs and matching returned hashes bind fetched descriptions to a release; at most six 1-second optional GETs share the 48-request cap and 2.2-second wave deadlines. Normalized detail facts persist in the OS cache for 24 hours, misses for 60 seconds; normalized evidence/references may accompany existing durable discovery rows, but raw descriptions do not persist. Supernova 0.1.4 preserves exact integer heights from 144 through 8640 in structured release evidence and selected-file video probes; explicit release tokens include 240p, 8640p and nonstandard heights such as 800p, while malformed/conflicting claims remain unknown. Selected-file probe heights override release-level hints; absent heights preserve those hints, and malformed/conflicting explicit heights override them as unknown. Normalized evidence/probe cache namespaces are v2. Metadata upgrades retain stable row IDs. Existing identity/file verification remains unchanged.
- Re:ANIME is independently packaged in `../nova-addons/addons/reanime/`, porting Yuzono’s JSON catalogs, episode inventory and FlixCloud sub/dub server discovery. Deferred resolution uses the upstream `enc-dec.app` service. The opt-in `permissions.flixcloudPlayback` and resolver-only `hlsProxy` response authorize `crates/providers/src/hls_proxy.rs`: random loopback capabilities, public DNS-pinned/domain-scoped HTTPS fetches, nested playlist/key/map rewriting, same-origin token inheritance, and PNG/WebP prefix removal plus XOR segment decoding. Relays are memory-only, tied to selection cancellation, limited to four sessions/four readers per session, 16 MiB responses and 4096 resources, expiring after ten idle minutes or six hours. Source IDs and native episode numbers remain stable; the addon reuses generic metadata enrichment/episode proof and capability toggles. Re:ANIME 0.1.2 bounds family queries and source loading, prioritizes the requested season, follows same-family prequel/sequel entries, and uses explicit source season labels. Only the declared regular prefix participates in canonical episode matching; appended extras keep their native playback IDs. Matching uses the shared Rust mapper and permits alternate names only with explicit matching IMDb identity, rejecting conflicts. QuickJS/source-shaped regressions live in `crates/providers/src/reanime_tests.rs` with snapshots in `test-fixtures/addons/reanime/`. Direct MKV preparation, font installation and chapter markers are not ported.
- `nova-providers::registry` adapts installed JS packages to existing Stremio catalog/detail responses at `nova-provider://<provider-id>`, deriving descriptors from manifests. Startup restores explicitly installed packages without registering defaults; legacy bundled-provider markers are retained but no longer read. ZIP updates preserve existing membership, enabled state and order. AniKoto keeps its existing URL and `anikoto:` media/episode IDs. JS owns site parsing, related-entry discovery, and fresh-token playback extraction; Rust's `metadata.rs` and `sequence.rs` own reusable enrichment, canonical candidate selection, and episode alignment. A bounded `nova.metadata.checkpoint` preserves fetched native details if optional discovery times out. Metadata capability calls require `permissions.addonMetadata`; contextual lookup is declared by `capabilities.contextualStreams`.
- Metadata enrichment resolves explicit IMDb/TMDB/MAL/AniList connections before searching enabled, available installed addon catalogs through ordinary catalog/meta endpoints; already connected addons skip speculative title searches. Blank native fields permit fallback, and display fields are selected only after conflicting connections are removed. Later confirmed providers can fill missing episode artwork/text while the first accepted mapping keeps its display numbering; duplicate aliases retain independent provenance until conflict filtering and final deduplication. Cinemeta participates in general enrichment only as an enabled installed addon. Official v3 Cinemeta series responses, including metadata candidates used to enrich bundled sources, also get a bounded, optional live-endpoint artwork repair (`metadata/cinemeta.rs`): only one-to-one normalized-title matches within the same IMDb series and matching special/regular classification can replace Metahub URLs; globally unique titles tolerate missing/differing dates, while repeated titles require a unique match within one calendar day, leaving IDs, numbering and other metadata intact; confirmed corrections survive failed/malformed/unrelated/partial refreshes. `nova-media` injects the bounded transport; immutable snapshots keep workers away from app locks. A nested guard and visited requests prevent recursion. Optional enrichment has a five-second deadline, 32 requests, 100 previews per search response, six detail candidates, and 2 MiB response cap. Nested providers inherit the remaining deadline; Android transport retains preconfigured rustls. See [metadata fetching and reliability](metadata-pipeline.md) for the complete pipeline, cache policies and remaining gaps.
- `SourceSequence` describes native titles/aliases/year, season/part labels, declared counts, and available stable episode IDs. The shared Rust mapper supports separate/split/merged seasons and cours, unequal parts, continuous numbering and unique-title anchors, rejecting gaps/ambiguity/conflicts. Explicit later-season titles also align standalone season-1 numbering when complete regular counts and absolute numbering prove the single-season layout. Opened AniKoto entries are included even if absent from search and reuse fetched rows. Two globally unique positional title anchors plus equal counts can confirm shortened series names without conflicting anchors. Show connections never imply episode aliases by themselves.
- `detail.rs::{source_stream_lookup,episode_stream_ids,stream_endpoint_owned}` routes contextual streams through provider capabilities and confirmed aliases through recipient manifest restrictions, without AniKoto-specific app branches. Private IDs remain owner-scoped; non-global aliases additionally require addon provenance. Confirmed external fields enrich native entries; ordinary main-series numbering and established saved episode IDs remain stable through coverage reconciliation. Standalone/bundled entries retain native availability. Library/history/download/sync identities and fresh-token resolution are unchanged. `novaExternalIds` retains typed show claims; `novaStreamIds`, opaque `novaConnections`, `novaMetadataRevision`, and `novaSourceUrl` retain aliases and ownership in existing JSON episode metadata caches. Conflicting claims suppress aliases; inventory revisions invalidate obsolete cached routes across addon changes/restarts. Fresh aliases update pickers and restart stream searches; `stream_generation` rejects stale replies and selected captions/thumbnails refresh without disturbing playback.
- `catalog_grouping.rs` is a separate pure catalog-presentation engine: owned native previews retain their IDs and order while confirmed parent/season evidence yields display groups or reversible virtual-season partitions. Grouping-specific IMDb/TMDB-TV/TVDB parent types do not change tracker identities. `catalog_grouping/cache.rs` stores bounded derived evidence using immutable read snapshots and separately serialized background persistence; the narrow resolver queries at most two accepting exact-ID metadata endpoints, with two concurrent lookups, a two-second total deadline and 2 MiB response bound. Original mode starts no grouping lookups. Inventory-bound external broker parent proof survives specials; those inventories stay whole in Season mode. Optional inferred grouping is enabled only in Series mode by `catalog_grouping_infer_series` (false by default): bounded explicit prequel/season relationships, consecutive season labels and a matching title family must lead to an independently confirmed canonical series anchor. `GroupingProof::InferredSeasonChain` and `GroupingEvidence::is_inferred()` distinguish this inference from confirmed identity. Inferred membership has unknown season and no partitions or episode aliases; Original and Season modes retain confirmed-only behavior. Bounded catalog lookup can recover confirmed current broker results only after the exact owner returns the same globally identified native inventory; opaque legacy cache identities cannot cross installations. Source-shaped fixtures under `test-fixtures/catalog-grouping` cover compatible AIOMetadata canonical episode output and distinguish it from the optional explicit Nova extension.
- Host enrichment caches use `metadata:v2:<digest>` in bounded session storage shared with JS (2 MiB total; 256 KiB host entry cap, 16 KiB JS entry cap). Confirmed mappings/artwork (including direct Cinemeta thumbnail corrections) also persist locally in `provider_metadata_cache:v1` through `metadata/cache.rs` and `src/app/io.rs` (128 entries, 8 MiB total). Complete confirmed movie results are reused for 30 days; all non-movie episode inventories and incomplete fields refresh on the next fetch after five minutes, since a previously complete series can gain a new season; failed refreshes retain prior confirmed results for the same fingerprint, while explicit conflicts invalidate them. Missing/ambiguous results stay session-only for 30 seconds. Native sequence/availability, artwork matcher version and canonicalized addon inventory revisions are part of the key, preserving restart reuse while invalidating changed inputs. Oversized results remain usable uncached. AniKoto's direct MP4/HLS, MegaPlay and Mewcdn resolution, headers/subtitles, in-app playback, and MP4 download forwarding remain in their existing paths; finite HLS downloads use the shared offline-bundle worker. Image-wrapped MegaPlay segments remain unsupported. See `crates/providers/README.md` for the JS service contracts.
- `nova-media::net` uses a **fire-and-continue** API: callers pass a continuation, never block or spawn threads.
- Remote sync callbacks (`set_on_remote`, `set_pair_callback`) arrive on the tokio runtime thread and are marshalled to the UI thread in `start_sync` (`sync.rs`). Playback protocol callbacks follow the same rule through `remote_playback.rs::attach_playback_service`; only the UI thread inspects snapshots, checks the live session and dispatches player commands.
- HTTP and provider-addon HLS downloads share the same queue. `crates/download/src/hls.rs` chooses the highest-bandwidth non-I-frame variant and retains its audio/subtitle groups, segment boundaries, initialization maps, byte ranges, discontinuities and ordinary AES-128 keys. Provider headers accompany every request. HTTP 429 responses retry up to six times through the shared HTTP worker, honoring seconds/date-form `Retry-After` or using exponential backoff when absent/invalid. Cooldowns are cancellable and bounded to five minutes total per request; a longer server deadline fails rather than retrying early. Header timeouts apply to each attempt, including initial and nested playlists, segments, maps and keys; committed segments remain reusable. Live playlists and unsupported encryption fail explicitly; DASH and website-only YouTube links remain unsupported. Each job stores a local `.m3u8` entry plus an `hls/` subdirectory in its existing `downloads/http/<job-id>/` directory. Segment completion markers verify source URL and byte length before resume reuse; incomplete segments restart. HLS reports actual downloaded bytes plus a stable resource-completion percentage for the label, rail and Android notification; it never alternates an estimated byte total with an unknown one. Resource counts are transient (`serde(skip)`) and reconstructed on resume. The entry is published only after all assets are complete, so orphan adoption cannot mistake individual segments for finished episodes. Automatic watched cleanup retains the actively playing HLS episode until player-close finalization so its remaining segments stay available; manual removal still deletes immediately. Removing an episode download (including auto-delete-watched) recursively removes the entire job directory, keys, subtitles, markers and partial files included; active removals cancel first and clean up when the worker exits. No new KV schema is needed.
- Season downloads start from a season card’s right-click/long-press menu → Download season → All episodes / Unwatched episodes. `season_downloads.rs` skips known-future, invalid and already queued/downloaded episodes, uses the first manual downloadable stream as a fixed primary recommendation baseline, and tries the latest manually selected outlier only when primary matching fails. Unmatched episodes require another explicit choice; no unrelated top result is selected. Planning performs only stream-list lookups (one episode at a time, up to 16 provider routes and 500 episodes); resolver/link resolution, HTTP probing, torrent metadata and enqueueing wait until Confirm download. The existing Streams leaf renders a responsive review of episodes/releases, estimated known bytes, explicit unknown-size count and actual filesystem space; projected remaining is shown only with all sizes known. Confirm revalidates all captured authorities and space before queue admission. Failed Downloads rows retain Retry and add Choose another stream: a one-episode replacement plan leaves the failed artifact untouched until confirmation, and removes the old failed attempt through the coordinator only when its identity still matches and the confirmed job differs. Plans are neither persisted nor synced.
- Stream downloads use the `nova-download` worker/queue; progress is read by the 250 ms UI tick, so no Slint access occurs on transfer threads. The tick refreshes the pinned download rows **in place** (`set_row_data`) when the row set/order is unchanged, instead of replacing the whole `streams` model: replacing the model destroys every row delegate, including the Android hold-to-open action-sheet timer, which then never fires while a transfer is active. The same tick reaps a transfer worker that ended without finalizing (panic/unwind), so a dead worker cannot pin the single active slot and stall every queued download.
- Deferred provider streams can be downloaded through the same queue. Each attempt resolves a fresh link on a worker and verifies the installed owner/settings revision; navigation retains the captured episode. Only `nova-resolver:stream-<stable hash>` markers persist in `downloads:v1`; resolver tokens, signed links and headers stay in memory. Private HTTP transfers omit resume sidecars and validators, restart partial files on retry, sanitize transport errors, and reject HLS before writing playlists. Incomplete jobs require reselecting Download after restart; completed files remain available offline. Ordinary HTTP/HLS and torrent resume behavior is unchanged.
- New HTTP jobs persist a fixed-length SHA-256 artifact path before transfer, independently of readable `title`/`display`/`file_name` labels. This reserves room for `.part.resume.json` even with long Unicode source names. Valid legacy paths and completed files remain usable; oversized legacy partial/validator pairs migrate on the worker without overwriting existing resumed files, using a streaming copy when hard links are unavailable. Cleanup still owns the complete job directory.

---

## 4. UI crates (`crates/ui`, `crates/ui-shared`, `crates/ui-*`)

- The root depends directly on `nova-ui-shared` so inline `slint!` test fixtures can import the canonical compiled globals. Root `build.rs` forwards its `DEP_NOVAUISHARED_SLINT_LIBRARY_*` metadata to rustc and enables experimental bridge types for these macros; `tests/theme.rs` exercises actual shared controls against `@NovaUiShared`, without a second Theme definition.
- `crates/ui/build.rs` compiles AppWindow with `slint-build` (AOT). The five page build scripts compile `NovaSettings` (navigation shell), `NovaHome`, `NovaDiscover`, `NovaCalendar` and `NovaDetail`; `ui-detail-*` compiles its nested bodies; `ui-library`, `ui-more`, `ui-player-osd` and `ui-onboarding` compile their additional roots; the thirteen `ui-settings-*` build scripts compile individual `NovaSettings<Name>` bodies, and `nova-ui-shared` compiles `NovaUiShared`. All include `crates/ui/build-support.rs` for experimental components, matching catalogs and `SLINT_EMIT_DEBUG_INFO` inspection metadata. The helper sets `SLINT_BUNDLE_TRANSLATIONS_DOMAIN=nova-ui`; every unit watches the original catalog directory/files, never its generated output, so unchanged builds stay fresh. They clear `SLINT_LIVE_PREVIEW`; the retained opt-in feature warns and remains compiled because interpreter globals/weak handles cannot use the native factories.
- `crates/ui/src/lib.rs` exposes `backend_text`, generated globals, canonical model types re-exported from `nova-ui-shared`, and the `src/window.rs` AppWindow wrapper. AppWindow retains the backend API, navigation, transitions, model handles and persistent page state. Nonvisual `page-bindings.slint`, `collection-bindings.slint` and `playback-bindings.slint` components connect backend fields to the shared bridge pools once for the AppWindow lifetime. Lightweight conditional `*-host.slint` components supply mount-specific one-way inputs and mount a `ComponentContainer`; AppWindow does not import those page visual trees. Reconnecting an already-joined backend/global pool on remount is forbidden: Slint 1.18 two-way linking is not idempotent and can create a recursive binding. `src/{settings_factory,page_factories,collection_factories,playback_factories}.rs` install factories and weak callback forwarding to public root `*_bridge_*` routing functions. Each fresh embedded page reuses AppWindow's `NovaUiShared` globals bundle before its bindings and initialization run. Theme, Anim, navigation, input modality and page bridge globals therefore have one owner; there are no child global copies or property-by-property global relays. Page-local menus, focus scopes, timers and crossfade snapshots stay inside their original components. A deferred root teardown callback releases bridge-only image/model inputs after conditional trees are removed, rechecking the mount guards so rapid remounts retain their values; backend-linked fields are refreshed rather than cleared. Containers own page instances, while root viewport, safe inset, active-state and outgoing More-transition bindings retain their existing placement. The release timer also runs once at construction to publish default Home, whose initial mount has no guard transition. Root mount guards preserve Back focus before teardown; `page_factories::recover_back_focus` forces factory publication and repairs absent/hidden native focus unless a separate popup or completion dialog owns input. More-submenu action forwarding runs this guard immediately and once after event-stack teardown; asynchronous Downloads, Calendar, Devices and Watch party model updates call `AppWindow::recover_back_focus` after releasing app locks. `focus_mounted_page` restores the active page’s native keyboard scope, and only while the persistent window scope still owns focus, so pointer-focused inputs are not displaced. Onboarding stays inside that persistent scope and receives Back before underlying pages; its weak restoration callback checks native tree ancestry to retain wizard editors while replacing focus held by the covered page. Its held-repeat protection works before the first pointer press. Home, Settings and About use the guarded restoration rather than unconditional local focus timers. Pending More-return children do not gain focus; the Detail branch delegates to `focus_mounted_detail` to select the shell, Tracking or Streams scope and exclude category/duplicate overlays. Selector open/close changes explicitly schedule the same handoff while the Detail shell stays mounted. The inactive More content host hides its focus scope while Detail or Player is mounted, keeping that retained layer out of pointer hit testing.
- Addon ZIP import uses `SettingsBridge` / AppWindow `addon_zip_import_busy`, `addon_zip_import_status` and `addon_zip_import_requested`, forwarded by the compiled Settings factory to `src/app/run.rs` and `addon_picker.rs`; backend completion publishes package availability.
- Settings submenu visuals live in `crates/ui-settings-*/<name>-module.slint`, each mounted through `settings-section-host.slint`. The shell in `settings.slint` retains the overview, header, single scroll viewport, scroll restoration, keyboard routing, transitions, scope modal, addon configuration and Home catalog overlay. `ui-settings/src/submenus.rs` resets transient state before a new shell is constructed and then installs weak shell callbacks/factories; saved values and model handles remain in the existing `SettingsBridge`. `ui-shared/settings-content.slint` shares shell-local editor/focus indices and layout inputs. Submenus report minimum/preferred layout height at the actual slot width, guarded by the selected ID; Addons retains its preferred-height scroll extent. `SettingsSectionHost` explicitly positions its `ComponentContainer` at `(0, 0)` so the embedded root does not participate in the host’s intrinsic layout negotiation and recurse through its allocated width; published submenu heights remain authoritative. The original selection predicates still control lifetime: closing the narrow detail pane retains its body, and switching sections replaces it. All bodies reuse the parent window adapter, models and globals; there are no interpreter components or new persisted keys.
- `vendor/i-slint-compiler/NOVA_PATCH.md` documents the pinned 1.18.0 patch. `new_embedded_with_shared_globals(context, app.shared_globals_novauishared())` attaches a compiled root to its dynamic parent slot and reuses initialized imported global objects. It validates the supplied bundle and window adapter, initializes only fresh local globals, and does not create/show/hide a second window. The older `new_embedded(context, prepare)` remains available. Cross-component alias folding into imported globals is disabled to preserve runtime two-way links. Ordinary constructors retain fresh global ownership. Slint dependencies are pinned exactly to match native layouts; factory products must not be shown, hidden or run independently.
- [`experiments/modules/README.md`](../crates/ui/experiments/modules/README.md) describes an isolated Slint 1.18.0 compile experiment using the real Settings page: ordinary imports, `experimental-module-builds` library imports, and a `ComponentFactory`/`ComponentContainer` boundary. Its nested Cargo workspace is excluded from production builds; no interpreter or runtime tests are involved. The factory prototype does not implement Nova's settings/focus/global-state wiring. The same README records the real Settings split: the largest generated file is about 39% smaller. A subsequent one-sample default-Nix UI-library build comparison reused the existing target directory and measured 12% faster fresh Rust UI compilation and 35% faster rebuilding after an identical Settings edit; `build-time-results.json` retains timing evidence and qualifications. With the subsequent shared-globals/four-page split, AppWindow generated 14.4 MiB and Settings 27.2 MiB. Splitting all Settings submenus then reduced the shell to about 5.2 MiB and its largest submenu to about 4.3 MiB; before the nested Detail/remaining-page split, Detail generated 16.6 MiB and AppWindow 14.4 MiB. The nested split reduces them to about 2.3 MiB and 3.7 MiB; Tracking is the largest new leaf at 6.4 MiB. These are source-size measurements; additional build-time/RSS comparisons, full application/release timings and runtime CPU/GPU memory profiling remain unmeasured. Headless regressions exercise focus, input, layout, retained state and teardown.
- `appwindow.slint` imports Roboto Regular/Bold from `assets/fonts/` and sets the window's default family to Roboto on every platform; the embedded Settings root uses the same family. Build scripts embed font/image files in packaged builds. `crates/ui/src/fonts.rs::initialize_fonts` registers both Roboto faces and Noto Color Emoji, then orders generic text fallback as Roboto → Noto → existing system families before the window is shown, covering symbols, keycaps, flag pairs and joined emoji independently of host fonts; `nova-ui` uses the shared `i-slint-core` font context for this registration. System fallback still supplies other scripts. The bundled full COLRv1 vector font retains the previous bitmap font’s entire cmap and all 4,176 Unicode GSUB ligature inputs through generated compatibility aliases (`assets/fonts/tools/prepare_emoji.py`, `emoji-coverage.txt`); no vector glyphs are subsetted. Desktop keeps Slint 1.18/FemtoVG 0.27 and enables the `colrv1` feature from the revision-pinned `misobarisic/femtovg` Git fork (`feat/colrv1`, Slint-compatible 0.27 with upstream’s shared-borrow fix backported). Its `src/text/colr.rs` uses skrifa 0.44/tiny-skia 0.12 for CPU rasterization into the existing color glyph atlas. The revision-pinned `misobarisic/tiny-skia` fork corrects partial sweep angle scaling. Root `[patch.crates-io]` entries and `Cargo.lock` pin both forks (including `tiny-skia-path` from the same revision for shared SVG geometry types); neither renderer is vendored locally. Android keeps its native Skia/FreeType COLRv1 path. Font provenance and Apache-2.0/OFL-1.1 licenses are in `assets/fonts/`; UI Roboto removes only six overlapping emoji cmap mappings and carries a Nova modification notice; glyphs, metrics and shaping remain intact. `assets/fonts/tools/prepare_text_fonts.py` reproduces/checks these faces and the unmodified `Roboto-Subtitles.ttf`, retained separately because Android libass has no system fallback.

### TV interface mode

Settings → Display → Interface mode selects Automatic, Standard or TV for this
installation. `nova_config::InterfaceMode` is persisted as the independent
`interface_mode` JSON KV key, outside `CacheSettings` and every sync domain.
`src/app/settings.rs` resolves Automatic with Android `UiModeManager` television
detection (standard on desktop), applies live edits and enables initial keyboard
focus. `InterfaceStyle.tv` in `crates/ui-shared/globals.slint` travels through the
existing shared globals bundle; factories do not duplicate mode state.

TV uses wider labelled navigation, larger text/cards/controls and derived grid
limits without changing the saved standard layout preferences. AppWindow keeps
Android platform capabilities separate from phone/touch presentation. Core pages
retain semantic arrow-key navigation; Home featured actions, Library tools,
stream output selection, Calendar and Downloads add explicit TV focus routes.
Menu/held OK exposes card actions; pointer clicks, context menus, wheel scrolling
and slider input remain usable on desktop TV mode. Async row removal clamps focus.
Shared dropdowns/sheets and the Devices, Remote Controls, Party, Onboarding and
completion surfaces support directions/OK/Back. `PlaybackLanguagePicker` exposes
`focus_picker()` and `reveal(length,length)` for the onboarding viewport;
`RemoteTargetMenu` exposes `focused`, `open_menu()`, `focus_launcher()` and
`dismissed()` so the selector can retain its persistent keyboard scope.

TV navigation reserves a 224px rail for English/Croatian labels. `NumericSetting`
measures its 20px value plus padding. Addon configuration uses 22px labels and
18px supporting copy, a 1440px panel cap and a 1120px two-column breakpoint in
TV mode. Episode cards use 22px season labels, 20px metadata and measured content
height to keep badges clear; Overview synopsis matches the 24px hero body. Resume
uses an on-accent focus border. Platform safe areas remain the display boundary;
physical-TV overscan still requires device validation.

The TV player uses timeline/button focus rows and preview-on-hold seeks committed
once on release; details and guards are in `docs/player-behavior.md`.
`vendor/i-slint-backend-android-activity/androidwindowadapter.rs` maps Android media
buttons to reserved U+E100–E107 events because Slint 1.18 has no media Key variants.
PlayerOverlay handles them; AppWindow consumes them outside playback so they never
enter text fields. System volume remains owned by the platform in TV mode.

The existing APK also declares `LEANBACK_LAUNCHER`, optional Leanback/touchscreen
and optional camera/autofocus features. `android/res/drawable/{tv_banner,tv_wordmark}.xml`
reuse Nova branding. `flake.nix` builds cargo-apk2 from upstream `mzdk100/cargo-apk2`
at merge commit `32b91331ff2371f3f06505ea6ceea3743e423a1c` (PR #28), which adds
the application banner attribute. `android/cargo-apk2-Cargo.lock` pins its CLI-only workspace;
the flake excludes example apps and copies that lock into the source before
building. The receiver metadata extension is applied by the flake as described above.
`android/cargo-apk2-readonly-libs.patch` fixes repeated staging of read-only native libraries;
`android/test_runtime_packaging.py` builds a small universal APK twice against the actual
Nix inputs in release mode, verifying all three ABI payloads, uncompressed
16 KiB-aligned native entries, the direct-loading manifest flag and unchanged
inputs in Android check CI.
The probe also links mpv and checks the extracted media libraries' dynamic
imports against their actual packaged providers and API-26 system stubs; a
successful Rust/shared-library link alone does not establish loadability.
`python3 -B android/test_tv_packaging.py` checks metadata and assets without
building Android; the Linux CI packaging step runs both
Python suites. TV regressions live in the existing UI
suites (`player/tv_controls.rs`, `downloads/tv_navigation.rs`,
`settings_preferences/interface_mode.rs`, Library/Home/stream layout tests and
`settings_control_feedback.rs`).

### Translations (i18n)

- **User-facing strings are marked with `@tr("…")`** in the `.slint` files. The whole interface is covered; string *values* that reach the UI as data (addon names, stream labels, disk sizes) stay untranslated. Mark new strings as you add them — Slint falls back to the source text when a catalog has no entry, so marking is always safe.
- **Counts that inflect use Slint's plural form**: `@tr("{n} episode" | "{n} episodes" % count)` (the syntax is `"singular" | "plural" % n`, extra `{}` args follow). The `hr` catalog carries the three Croatian forms and the header's `Plural-Forms` selects them.
- `build.rs` points `slint-build` at `translations/`, so the gettext catalogs are compiled into the binary at build time (domain = `CARGO_PKG_NAME` = `nova-ui`, i.e. `translations/<code>/LC_MESSAGES/nova-ui.po`). The directory must hold at least one catalog. `translations/en/` registers English and includes explicit source-language entries; English is the source language, and missing entries fall back to the source text.
- `compile_page_library` reads the same source catalogs directly, setting the compiler patch's `SLINT_BUNDLE_TRANSLATIONS_DOMAIN` override to `nova-ui` while preserving each library's package identity. No catalogs are copied into watched build outputs. All units bundle the same language list/order and use context-free entries; Slint's shared language selection therefore indexes each unit consistently. Edit only `crates/ui/translations/`.
- **Catalogs are context-free**: `build.rs` sets `DefaultTranslationContext::None`, so an entry is keyed by the source string alone. Slint's default context is the *component* a string sits in, which would silently unmatch an entry when a `@tr` literal moves to another component. Regenerate/verify catalogs with `slint-tr-extractor --no-default-translation-context`; a string that means two different things in two places can still name a context (`@tr("ctx" => "…")`).
- **Text the Rust backend formats goes through `crates/ui/src/backend_text.rs`** (download states, sync status, stream/episode hints, relative air dates, count labels). Fixed strings are keyed by the English source (`text::tr("Queued")`); templated text uses named helpers, plus Croatian 1/2-4/5+ noun forms. This API is owned by `nova-ui` alongside the Slint UI and translation catalogs. `Bridge::apply_language` updates it and refreshes already-materialized Rust text in visible models (library badges/category labels, Home rows, detail rows, Discover hints, and Settings status).
- **Stored identifiers are never translated, only their labels.** The automatic library buckets (`Plan to Watch`, `Watching`, …) and the `WatchStatus` labels are *keys*: the filter comparison, `activity_bucket` and the persisted values use the English identifier, while the UI shows a localized label — `library.rs` pushes `category_names` (values) plus `category_labels` (display); Library’s filter pills render the latter while callbacks report the stored value. Watch-status pills are translated at the two display sites (`text::tr(badge_label())`).
- **Languages**: English (source) and Croatian (`hr`, `crates/ui/translations/hr/…` + backend helpers in `crates/ui/src/backend_text.rs`). Croatian addresses the user in the formal plural ("vi"); labels stay short, which the Croatian-width phases in `addons/addon_row_fit.rs` / `settings_sync/sync_overflow.rs` enforce (a longer label overflowed a 320px phone row, and one unwrapped card heading widened the whole Sync subpage).
- Switching language selects Slint's bundled catalog with `slint::select_bundled_translation(code)` in `src/app/i18n.rs`, which marks every `@tr` binding dirty. Rust-built strings are values rather than bindings, so the bridge separately refreshes localized model text from existing state without refetching content or rebuilding poster pipelines. `"en"` restores the source strings. The chosen language is Settings → Display → Language (`nova_config::Language`), synced by default with optional per-device overrides.
- Adding a language: catalog directory + a `Language` variant (`ALL`/`index`/`code`/`label` arms in `crates/config/src/lib.rs`; the picker list is built from `ALL`, so the row follows automatically) + backend translations/helpers in `crates/ui/src/backend_text.rs`. Untranslated strings stay English.
- The retained `cargo dev` / `live-preview` feature currently uses AOT generation too, so bundled translations work as in ordinary builds. Interpreter hot reload is deferred until its dynamic globals and page attachment can use an appropriate bridge.

### Slint files

`theme.slint` owns semantic colors, surface and gradient brushes, borders,
shadow colors, scrim tints and shared control geometry (`card_corner_radius`,
`card_spacing`, `button_corner_radius`). `Theme.standard` defines the default appearance;
`Theme.current` is replaced on the UI thread by `nova_ui::apply_theme`.
`AccentColor` in `crates/config/src/lib.rs` selects Purple (the original default),
Orange, Blue, Teal, Rose, Cyan, Amber or Lime. Accent fills, selected states,
focus rings and action/progress gradients change together; bright presets use
contrasting dark foreground ink while semantic status colors and service branding
keep their own roles.
`BackgroundStyle` selects Default, Accent-tinted or True black independently of
the action accent. Default preserves the original surface palette; Accent-tinted
derives a dark, stepped surface ramp for page canvases, navigation, cards, menus,
controls and matching artwork fades. Player dimming stays neutral black.
The private `AccentChoices` component
in `crates/ui-settings-theme/theme-module.slint` maps responsive picker rows to
one persisted setting and keyboard row. The picker sits first above Background style;
its 44px-tall buttons use each preset’s actual fill and a centered contrasting
checkmark, retaining translated accessibility names and selected state.
`AppWindow` re-exports `Theme` and `ThemePalette`. All main pages and shared
controls consume the palette. Stream-selector filter pills use My Library's
40px rounded chips, selection gradient, label weights and outlined border,
retaining their fixed-width loading spinner and focus ring. The exported
`LibraryFilterChip` and `StreamFilterPill` use pressed feedback on Android
(`touch_menus`), so release/cancel clears the tint even if hover persists;
compact desktop layouts retain mouse hover. True-black mode makes page canvases,
neutral cards and controls pure black with subtle outlines, retaining artwork and
accent/status colors. Home and Detail artwork ramps use theme-selected baked
image masks so their opaque edges match the active canvas.
The player uses a viewport-anchored, uniform black dim layer while controls
are visible. Settings and Discover use a flat page canvas without
a decorative top glow, including behind the desktop navigation rail.
Settings → Theme persists `background_style` (`default`, `accent_tinted`,
`true_black`), `accent_color`, `card_corner_radius` (0–24 logical px,
default 10), `button_corner_radius` (0–24 logical px, default 8),
`card_spacing` (0–32 logical px, default 8),
`status_bar_gradient` (0–100%, default 80), `transparent_navbar` (default true; explicit saved choices are preserved),
and independent
`home_backdrop_size` / `detail_backdrop_size` (`BackdropSize`: Small, Medium,
Large; default Medium), plus `hero_title_alignment` (`HeroTitleAlignment`: Left, Center, Right;
default Center) for narrow Home featured and Detail title logos/fallback text and Home’s inline metadata row, synced by default
with device overrides. Legacy `true_black` booleans and their device-override
baselines migrate to `background_style`; legacy sync records apply only when no
canonical style record exists. Onboarding keeps its compact Dark/True black
choice and forwards it to the same style setting. `Theme` publishes four baked
artwork-fade images selected by mode/accent; `assets/tinted-scrims/` contains
matching 1024px SVG variants, avoiding runtime GPU recoloring on page remounts.
Radius and spacing use five preset buttons instead of
sliders: radius Square / Subtle / Soft / Rounded / Extra rounded maps to
0 / 4 / 10 / 16 / 24 px (Soft is the default); the names also appear in sync
value comparisons. Button radius uses 0 / 4 / 8 / 12 / 24 px and updates
shared buttons, segments, selectors and page actions live, capped to fit each
control. Circular navigation and the shared Back control retain their geometry.
These controls use one row on wide Settings panes and two
rows (three plus two choices) on narrow panes. Spacing None / Tight / Compact /
Comfortable / Spacious maps to 0 / 4 / 8 / 16 / 32 px (Compact is the default),
including in sync value comparisons. Older custom values remain effective until
a preset is chosen; Left/Right steps to the adjacent lower/higher preset.
Radius applies to Home/Discover/Library artwork cards,
Detail artwork/synopsis/stream/season/episode cards and Settings cards; spacing
drives media grids/carousels and Detail season/episode geometry, including
keyboard reveal calculations, plus stream list spacing. Restore theme defaults
resets all nine fields while retaining device override choices. On narrow Home
and Detail, a fixed `assets/status-bar-scrim.svg` overlay darkens the status-bar
region and fades out below it; 0 disables it. Home places it outside its scroll
