layout, while Detail paints it above artwork and below controls.
Backdrop presets resize artwork and its scrims live; Home retains a measured
caption/actions minimum to avoid clipping, and Detail also adjusts hero top
spacing and scroll-fade distance. Home artwork targets are shorter than Detail\nfor the same preset; long captions can increase Home's content minimum.\nDetail's Medium preset preserves its previous geometry.

| Backdrop preset | Home narrow height (width multiplier, min–max px) | Home wide height | Detail narrow / wide artwork height | Detail narrow / wide hero top spacing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Small | 0.85×, 280–360 | 0.30×, 320–480 | 420 / 560 px | 140 / 20 px |
| Medium | 1.05×, 340–480 | 0.42×, 420–640 | 560 / 740 px | 250 / 50 px |
| Large | 1.45×, 440–620 | 0.55×, 540–840 | 720 / 920 px | 390 / 180 px |

Top safe-area insets are added to artwork heights; Detail scrims extend another
60 px to meet the page canvas.
`tests/theme.rs` covers default colors, live palette/radius replacement, black surfaces,
outlined secondary buttons, unchanged control geometry and full restoration.

| File | Component / role |
|---|---|
| `appwindow.slint` | Root `AppWindow`: all backend properties/callbacks, screen switching, player overlay wiring. Owns `show_about`, independent About/Licenses scroll and nested-page state, and the UI-only `more_open` root page and `more_parent` submenu origin. `more_service_route` distinguishes Devices/Watch party visits from direct Home/player overlays. More-family pages share stationary navigation chrome and a clipped, separately animated content host; `more_scroll_y`, `more_choice` and `more_focus_zone` survive child visits. Route-generation guards invalidate pending returns on explicit navigation or modal/player entry; window capture routes Back through the active page, with a persistent held-key guard. Android Back clears shared keyboard markers; `SettingsStyle` is re-exported to verify its global mode across page recreation. |
| `types.slint` | Shared structs compiled by `nova-ui-shared`: `MediaCard` (localized library status/type plus an untranslated `status_key` for category colors, watched/total episode counts, and `activity_action` for await/resume viewing menus), `StreamRow` (full accessible text plus separate release title, metadata, description, file size, pinned download state and one-shot `reveal_new` eligibility), `EpisodeRow` (stable episode ID, requested/displayed artwork identities and optional per-video runtime), `AddonRow` (decoded manifest logo and fallback initial), `CategoryRow`, `HomeCatalogRow` / `HomeCatalogSection` / `HomeCatalogCard` (Home source settings, section labels, and poster rows), `SyncPeer`, `SyncInvite`, `TrackRow`, `SeasonCard` (stable season ID and requested/displayed artwork identities), `ContinueRow` / `UpcomingRow` (separate episode title/badge and selected artwork URL; Continue also carries remaining-time text), `SheetItem`, `DownloadEpisode` / `DownloadGroup`, `SettingsDestination`, and the tracking account/link/candidate/mapping/setup row structs (setup includes typed source/target display ranges). |
| `*-host.slint`, `../ui-shared/*-bridge.slint`, `../ui-*/{settings,home,discover,calendar,detail}-module.slint` | Typed hosts, shared contracts and separately compiled page roots. Add a parent-bound property to the original page, shared bridge, module forwarding, host and AppWindow binding. Add callbacks to the page/bridge/module, weak Rust forwarding in `src/{settings_factory,page_factories,collection_factories,playback_factories}.rs`, and AppWindow's public `*_bridge_*` routing functions. Keep the backend API unchanged. Import shared models/globals once from `@NovaUiShared`; backend state stays in bridges; shell-local transient state uses the corresponding content contract. |
| `discover.slint` | Compiled in `nova-ui-discover`. Discover browse grid + addon/type/catalog/genre dropdowns in a horizontal Flickable (readable selector widths, early gesture-axis hints keep the whole-page scroll from stealing horizontal drags). Keyboard focus reveals off-screen filters. `DiscoverFilters` shares the dropdown rail between browse and search. Focusing Search immediately hides browse cards, selectors, hints and loading indicators, even before typing; the persistent input, recent searches and temporary addon/type/catalog/genre selectors form an isolated search session. Header height animates between Discover and Search, incoming controls fade in, and restored browse cards fade and rise; motion switches bypass these transitions. Search defaults to all enabled searchable sources and lists only supported choices. Each source's results appear as soon as it responds and are reranked as more arrive; an in-grid spinner stays visible until the current fan-out completes. Results retain their controls and own model and scroll offset. Typing starts a debounced search after 2 characters; results remain open while editing shorter queries, and each result delegate owns a one-shot Home-style fade/slide reveal, independent of poster/model updates. Results fade through on submit/back; browse selection and scroll position are preserved throughout the search session. Viewport restoration ignores temporary content-clamp writes and pagination during context changes. Discover uses a flat page canvas, navy search and catalog controls, and rounded recent-search/empty panels. An 8px canvas-to-transparent fade below the pinned controls matches Library in browse and search results, in both scroll branches. Its left-aligned 160–230px poster grid uses Home-style bordered artwork cards with a 72px caption for two title lines and release metadata; placeholder and empty-state icons come from `icons.slint`. The card stride used by keyboard scrolling and artwork prefetch includes that caption height. A measured Discover title/description hero scrolls away while search, temporary search/history and browse filters remain pinned over fixed viewports in both platform branches. Column count / card width use the active ScrollView's measured viewport, including rail and cutout spacing. Keyboard scroll writes reach either mounted viewport without cancelling native inertia, and each branch samples its geometry before restoring focus. Results headings wrap to their measured height; release metadata elides at high density, and 8px above the grid keeps pointer-only hover lift inside the clip. |
| `home.slint` | Compiled in `nova-ui-home`. Home landing: view changes and AppWindow’s guarded focus restoration after factory publication and Detail teardown keep Android Back routed from row grids to the landing page. A rotating backdrop showcase whose artwork fills the top and sides of the landing viewport (behind Android’s status bar), with safe-area insets applied to captions and controls rather than artwork. When Featured is absent, the fixed status-bar gradient ends at the top safe-area boundary so it does not shade the first rail heading. Configurable title logo/text alignment on narrow screens and left-aligned titles on wide screens, optional addon taglines, inline type/rating/release/runtime metadata and descriptions on both phone and desktop. A purple Watch Now pill sits at the lower left; arrows and a counter sit at the lower right on both layouts, moving below the action when translated controls cannot share one row. A stationary banner tap opens details; swipes page without opening them. Caption layers reserve their measured heights during crossfades. The backdrop uses `assets/home-bottom-scrim{,-black}.svg` and, on wide layouts, `assets/home-side-scrim{,-black}.svg`, with fully opaque edge stops that blend into the Home canvas and matching SideNav panel colour; title/description colours and the scaled Watch Now button follow Detail’s hero styling; artwork and `FeaturedCaption` content crossfade while paging controls and the action stay steady. Continue Watching / Upcoming remain horizontal carousels (full-width scroll clips, with a half-card scroll hint on phones and theme-controlled spacing), with tappable headers opening vertical "see all" subpages. Configured catalog rails use the same headers and subpage transition/back navigation; `home_view >= 3` selects the catalog section grid; `home_view == -3` selects New episodes. Opened personal category grids (Continue, New episodes, Upcoming and both Watch history views) use Library columns; opened addon category grids use Discover columns. Home landing rails keep independent sizing, with two full cards and part of the next on phones. `HomeBridge.catalog_min_cols` flows through HomeHost and the Home module alongside Library `min_cols`; the UI-only `HomeCatalogSection.is_history` flag identifies history independently of translated titles. Its `tmdb_attribution` flag credits actual TMDB rows below the displayed rail and See-all grid, after recommendation exclusions and limits. The bottom link reads View full watch history. AppWindow carries `home_new_episodes`, its rail offset, and `new_episode_{picked,enter,remove}` callbacks through `run.rs`. Section headings wrap and the narrow Upcoming subpage puts its Calendar shortcut below the heading. Subpage grids use the measured scroll viewport and reserve 8px for pointer hover lift. Both rails are interactive `Flickable`s (native inertia); card `TouchArea`s hint the gesture axis early, blocking the landing page pan while a horizontal drag belongs to the rail and handing vertical gestures back to the page, like Detail's season rail. Landing cards own taps, hold/right-click menus (native `ContextMenuArea` on desktop, page-level `MenuSheet` on touch), and drag-to-tap suppression; cards and rails disable while the subpage covers them. Scroll offsets (`home_continue_x`, `home_upcoming_x`, etc.) ride on `AppWindow` to survive detail navigation. The subpage slides in and staggers rows; keyboard focus follows only keyboard navigation, and a card press clears keyboard modality before a rail can capture the drag. Both card types have navy footers, artwork-bound episode-number badges, full-width two-line episode titles (`HomeEpisodeBadge` supplies the artwork badge); the unsupported text menu glyph/button is removed. Continue cards keep Next up / New Episode pills and show a purple resume rail with a percentage only for positive playback; the footer uses known saved duration for localized minutes remaining, otherwise identifies the series. Upcoming shows the series and air date; tapping enters the series. Continue’s hold/right-click menus remain available. Header labels expose See all at the right. Footer flicks use the card’s early axis hints; hover lift is pointer-only to avoid sticky touch hover. The Upcoming subpage’s Calendar shortcut (`CalToggle`) enters the full planner through `run.rs::on_upcoming_cal_open`; it does not open the embedded Home month view. Legacy Home month-model helpers remain in `home.rs`, and the full planner reuses its date/grid helpers. |

Responsive Month/Week/Timeline page, date grid with equal-width day columns and aligned weekday headings independent of release content or selection, selected-day cards and release timeline; the Month sidebar begins at 1000 logical px so medium widths retain readable date/count columns and show releases below. Month/Week expose Previous/Next/Today; Timeline replaces month paging with incremental continuous scrolling, Earlier releases and Today. Transient `calendar_agenda_*` counters/revision plus `calendar_has_{earlier,later}` and `calendar_agenda_more` bridge its bounded loading and scroll reset. `CalendarDay` / `CalendarRelease` models and `calendar_*` AppWindow properties/callbacks are wired through `run.rs` to `calendar.rs`. Artwork visibility/active callbacks report measured card geometry, including the nested desktop day viewport, and drive `page_artwork.rs`; child pages hide phone navigation. |
| `downloads-page.slint` | Shared `PageHeader` matches Discover/My Library title/summary placement at both widths; narrow More children show Back, while desktop sidebar destinations omit it. Collapsed cards unmount their episode rows immediately; the header stays anchored when card height changes. Episode rows below 480 logical px place metadata above the badge and wrapped actions, preserving room for titles, transfer details and translated action labels. `DownloadGroup` / `DownloadEpisode` and `downloads_page_*` AppWindow properties/callbacks connect through `run.rs` to `downloads_page.rs`. Short touch landscape (wider than tall, below 600 usable px) scrolls the title and groups together while search and the independently pannable filter rail stick at the safe top. Group artwork identities and visibility/active callbacks drive `page_artwork.rs` using measured header geometry in either column layout; child pages hide phone navigation. |

| `detail.slint` | Compiled in `nova-ui-detail`. Detail modal: edge-to-edge backdrop hero with square 1024×1024 rasterized bottom/side scrims (`assets/detail-{bottom,side}-scrim{,-black}.svg`); baked standard/true-black colours avoid offscreen GPU colorization and thin-texture sampling lines during resizing/page recreation, a clipped hero layer retired when fully faded, an opaque themed episode-list canvas, and a scroll-driven fade of both the image and its black backing to the uniform themed artwork canvas (linear from the first movement, fully hidden after 360px on phones / 520px on desktop) and upward parallax (12% of scroll distance, capped at 56px / 80px), floating circular top controls, title/genre chips and contextual Start/Continue plus library actions above underlined tabs. A shared underline glides between tab slots using `Anim.transition_fade`; viewport changes recalculate its geometry directly without animating the content or focus targets. Phones place the hero text below the artwork; desktop uses the shaded left column. Clicking/tapping a truncated hero description smoothly expands or collapses its measured, top-aligned text independently of the Overview synopsis; scroll drags do not toggle it. Overview places an artwork card beside the expandable Synopsis card on desktop and stacks them on phones; Synopsis measures the full paragraph separately, top-aligns and elides collapsed text without cutting glyphs, and animates expansion/collapse with `Anim.transition_slide`; episode/stream tabs use the full padded content width without a metadata sidebar; season and episode grids size their columns from that width. Narrow season selectors use a clipped, horizontally flickable single row with selected-card reveal and Home Continue Watching's existing card-axis hints and row-movement page-pan hold (automatic reveal does not lock the page); wide screens retain the centered season grid. The shell owns tabs; season/episode bodies and the fullscreen Streams/Tracking pages mount through `DetailContent` factories; movies have a Start/Continue button beside the library action and omit the Episodes tab using the explicit `detail_is_movie` flag. The page scroll is a raw `Flickable` (not a `ScrollView`: the axis lock needs the inner Flickable's `interactive`) without a visible scroll indicator. Its top bar is **icon-only** (`TopIconButton`): Back is always present; saved entries show a sync/Tracking button followed by a Library options menu button at the right. The hero library button uses a plus when unsaved and a purple bookmark when saved, with the same add/remove toggle also available for movies. Library options opens a native popup below its button on desktop (including keyboard activation), or `MenuSheet` on touch, with the library card’s watched/status/remove actions plus category assignment. `detail_library_action` passes through AppWindow/run.rs to `src/app/library.rs`, resolving the open saved identity independently of library filters; `detail_library_watched` follows progress refreshes for the watched/unwatched label; `detail_library_activity_action` exposes Wait for new episodes / Resume watching without editing history. Shared `DetailTabs` exposes Overview (0), Episodes (3) and, for saved titles, the Tracking entry (4). Picking Tracking does not replace the underlying content tab. The sync icon opens Tracking as a shortcut. The Tracking entry opens a full-screen page with the shared Back control, a title header and its own scroll viewport; the hidden Detail hero and Episodes remain mounted so Overview/Episodes retain their scroll and search state. Back leaves the nearest Tracking layer before the page, then leaves Detail; opening Streams or closing Detail invalidates the tracking worker context. Detail snapshots restore the preceding content tab instead of a stale tracking draft. Episode cards retain the same full canvas height even without decoded artwork or a synopsis. Episode cards use full-bleed thumbnails under `assets/episode-card-scrim.svg`, a purple season/episode badge, a watched disc, and bounded title/synopsis text; release date and optional per-video addon runtime sit together above the synopsis. Playback rails and percentages appear only for unfinished episodes with recorded nonzero playback; completed or manually watched episodes keep only the watched disc. The Episodes tab renders **one page of 50 episodes** at a time (pagers above and under the grid; card picks pass `episode_page_start + i`, so they still resolve against the whole season). |

Overview and every detail page use a flat canvas, including safe-area/content gutters and the desktop rail; `SettingsHeader` supplies captions and controls only. Fixed layout slots keep the overview header’s collapse and pinned search/shortcuts from resizing the touch-scroll viewport; an opaque canvas behind the pinned controls masks rows scrolling underneath, including their gaps and top gutter; narrow layouts use header Back throughout Settings and hide the bottom navigation on both overview and detail pages; wide layouts retain the sidebar on desktop and Android. Unavailable controls retain section-level results. Navigation clears stale requests; rotation reveals the current target at its new geometry. Scope pills in `settings-sync.slint` cap their natural text width by the available width, including vertical cards and comparison modals. Existing actions, confirmation flows, QR pairing and platform player choices remain wired. The independently synced disk limit travels through `cache_disk_limit_index` / `cache_disk_limit_names` in SettingsBridge, SettingsBindings, AppWindow and the Image cache module; it uses the existing `settings_edited` callback. Its optional `show_readout` property lets the player submenu use a larger separate speed heading while Settings retains the inline readout. |
| `bottomnav.slint`, `sidenav.slint`, `kbnav.slint`, `icons.slint`, `anim.slint` | Navigation, icons (`IcEdit`, `IcOpenInNew`, `IcHistory` and `IcLink` for Tracking/addon actions, plus `IcChevronDown` / `IcChevronUp` for download groups and compact/prominent dropdown selectors and Simple Icons brand glyphs `IcMyAnimeList` / `IcAniList` for Tracking service buttons, and `IcCalendar` / `IcDownload` for the extra wide-rail destinations), animations, keyboard-nav helpers. Home/Discover/Library/More use the outline SVGs in `assets/icons/navigation` in both bars, with existing theme colors, sizing and feedback. Page navigation is responsive and **per page** — Home/Discover/Library host their own bars and switch on `narrow`; the More-family pages share an AppWindow-owned stationary rail: `bottomnav.slint` (`BottomNav`) is a full-width dark phone bar with square corners, no outer border or panel shadow on any edge, and translated Home / Discover / Library / More labels, a compass Discover icon (`IcExplore`), and a purple selected icon and label on the page-coloured canvas, with no selected pill or glow; `sidenav.slint` (`SideNav`) supplies a borderless full-height left sidebar with six destinations (Home, Discover, Library, More, Calendar, Downloads) listed top to bottom in a scrollable rail for short windows: below 700px the three root pages use BottomNav, from 700px upward a 56px icon-only panel always reserves a 64px footprint, including on large windows. AppWindow forwards `nav-viewport-width` to each page, which passes it to the sidebar as `viewport-width` (separate from intrinsic page width to avoid a layout binding loop); x/y are pinned inside the component because Slint centres plain children of non-layout parents. Pages reserve the same fixed footprint through `NavMetrics.width-for(root.nav-viewport-width)` in their own left padding; grids measure the cleared scroll viewport, and the rail also eats stray taps in that strip. Wide subpages keep the rail (window chrome); narrow Home subpages and Calendar/Downloads/Settings/About hide the bottom bar. More opens the responsive `more.slint` root page with Calendar/Downloads/Settings/About links; it uses BottomNav on narrow layouts and the desktop rail on wide layouts. Calendar/Downloads/Settings/About/Devices/Watch party/Stats/Watch queue/Bookmarks hide the phone bar; Devices and Watch party use the same shared content transition when entered from More. The stationary More bar returns at the start of a submenu return and remains interactive during content motion. Back returns to More when entered there, otherwise Home; More Back returns to Home. More, Calendar and Downloads show header Back only on narrow layouts, while Settings/About retain it as More subpages at both widths. Keyboard/system Back keeps working without a visible desktop arrow. A persistent window focus scope protects page replacements. Crossing to wide layout keeps More open. Keyboard navigation uses four phone slots and six rail destinations. Selection colours snap immediately on phones; the desktop rail marker stays circular at every wide size. BottomNav receives existing safe-area insets and each host’s page padding to extend its panel to the left/right screen edges while protecting labels; host layouts remove bottom padding whenever the bar is visible, so its background reaches the bottom edge and safe-bottom padding stays inside the bar. Phone hosts have no gap before the bar; its 68px content height removes the extra top inset while retaining 60px tab touch targets and 8px bottom padding; keyboard rings stay inside the equal-width slots; shared `NavFeedback` (in `sidenav.slint`) briefly enlarges only the incoming icon by up to 2px, then settles over 220ms; it observes selection changes on retained chrome as well as page creation. `NavState.from` suppresses feedback on same-page rebuilds; the existing persisted `anim_nav_slide` switch now controls this icon-only effect, labelled Navigation feedback. |

**Title logos:** `MetaPreview.logo` flows through `MediaItem.logo`, the Stremio adapter and metadata enrichment into optional `MetaHeader.logo_url`. Old cached headers default to an empty logo. Detail uses the modal opening token and current URL to reject stale completions; Home uses list generation, slide index and URL guards, with independent optional fetches and retry cooldowns so logos never block rotation. Home completions directly paint the displayed slide or park pixels only for its explicit pending target; typed keys include media/source identity, list generation, index, URL, recipe and settings revision. Matching UI-owned logos survive LRU eviction without another download, and target/list/recipe changes invalidate pending pixels before they can commit (`invalidate_home_pending_logo` also releases obsolete targets during settings projection). Logos use the Original recipe and reject supplied opaque logo images in favour of text. Cache JPEG encoding writes transparent artwork as PNG; WebP retains alpha.

**Home showcase:** `home.slint` renders the rotating featured backdrop above—but
independently of—the ordered Home catalogs (including the built-in Continue Watching and Upcoming rails). The artwork reaches
the top and sides of the landing viewport; captions respect safe-area insets.
Landing rows retain their initial safe padding while their Flickable clips extend
to both viewport edges; the original card dimensions and gaps are unchanged.
Subpages apply their own padding, and keyboard follow includes each row's inner
padding and uses outer-scroll coordinates even though rows sit in a nested layout. Narrow layouts align the title logo or white fallback title using Theme’s Title alignment setting (centered by default); wide layouts align it left.
Home’s inline metadata follows the same alignment on narrow screens.
Both layouts retain an optional addon tagline, inline media type / IMDb rating /
release info / runtime, and a description. `home_featured_release_info` and
`home_featured_tagline` flow from catalog previews through `AppWindow` into the
slide snapshot; missing values leave no fabricated metadata. The purple Watch Now
button stays at the lower left, with paging arrows and a counter at the lower
right on phones and desktops. If the measured controls cannot share a row, the
pager moves below the action; both caption layers reserve their measured heights
above them throughout a fade. A stationary banner tap opens details, while
horizontal swipes page and vertical scrolls do not open the title. Backdrops and
`FeaturedCaption` content crossfade using `Anim.transition_fade`; controls
stay outside the fading layers. `home_featured_revision` is advanced after all
incoming metadata/art properties (including the optional title logo) are published, so `FeaturedSlideData` snapshots
are coherent. The outgoing image is released after the fade, rapid changes
retarget the incoming content, and disabling transitions switches immediately.
The one-shot fade timers use `start()` after stopping; `restart()` alone does
not start an inactive Slint timer and would leave the outgoing layer opaque.
`FeaturedActionButton`, `FeaturedBadge` and `FeaturedPagerButton` share the
action/inline-metadata/control styling in `home.slint`.
Home uses `assets/home-{bottom,side}-scrim{,-black}.svg` image ramps because FemtoVG can
flatten brush gradients. Both masks have square intrinsic dimensions and baked
standard/true-black colours, avoiding thin textures and the offscreen
`Image.colorize` pass that can produce lines during resizing or page recreation.
Their edge stops reach the opaque themed artwork canvas
before the artwork ends, and Home supplies the same colour to SideNav’s
`panel-background`. The bottom ramp fills the measured featured banner on phones;
wide layouts also fill those bounds with the side ramp.
Caption colours, title weight and the Watch Now gradient/border match the detail
hero, with Home’s smaller measured text and button sizes retained. `settings.slint` adds Settings → Home,
where users manage a shared ordered list of built-in and addon landing catalogs, plus separate featured sources;
each featured catalog contributes up to five titles. Home preloads the current
and next backdrop at source resolution, restarts the rotation delay when a step
is requested and when a new selection is displayed, and opens a picked title
without borrowing Discover's current grid model. Home's banner tap and Watch Now
have separate callbacks through `AppWindow` and `run.rs`: the banner tap opens the
normal detail flow; Watch Now shares the detail page's automatic selection of
the most recent resumable episode, otherwise the first dated, released,
unwatched episode across all seasons (extras last). Both primary actions
use a vector play icon and a localized Start / Continue
label from `episodes.rs::watch_action_label`, with that same target’s
season/episode when known. Home publishes `home_featured_watch_label` through
AppWindow/HomePage; sparse previews show the action without an episode suffix
until metadata hydrates the episode cache, and movies have no episode suffix.
Known episode lists with no eligible target show Choose an episode on both
Home and Detail. The featured action width and pager stacking depend only on
viewport space (200 px narrow / 300 px wide minimum before stacking,
280 px / 340 px button caps), so label changes never resize the button.
Caught-up Detail titles offer Choose an episode. Labels refresh with metadata,
local/remote progress and language; Home also refreshes on displayed slide changes.
Stream choice remains manual; if no eligible episode exists, the episode picker
remains available. Cached metadata resolves immediately when possible; otherwise
`ModalItem` holds a one-shot Watch Now origin and an episode-loading flag. A
per-opening token rejects stale metadata callbacks, including same-title reopens;
manual navigation cancels pending selection. Home Watch Now uses the same Back
shortcut as Continue Watching; detail-page Watch Now returns to the episode list.
The featured backdrop also forwards early touch-axis hints to Home's page-pan
lock, just like Continue Watching and Upcoming: horizontal drags block vertical
scrolling; vertical drags, release and cancellation return control to the page.
Settings → Home also independently toggles the Continue Watching and Upcoming
rows; both default on and sync with other general settings. The Episode artwork
on Home toggle defaults off, so cards use series posters. Enabling it selects
episode thumbnails; explicit saved preferences remain unchanged. It syncs as a
general setting and supports a device-local override.
Because you watched (`HomeRowSource::BecauseYouWatched`) is appended enabled
below existing Home catalogs when older layouts migrate. Settings → Home can
toggle, reorder, remove and restore it like other built-ins. The synced
`home_recommendations_migrated` flag is paired atomically with `home_rows` in
the JSON settings `home_layout` record; legacy independent row records remain
readable without allowing stale migration flags to restore a removed catalog.
`home_recommendations_hidden` stores permanent typed-title exclusions in the
profile's settings JSON. Sync projects these as independent grow-only
`home_recommendation_hidden:<JSON type/id tuple>` records in the existing
settings domain, so concurrent hides and stale snapshots retain every hidden
title; legacy set records remain readable. Native right-click, touch hold and TV menus reuse Home's
typed action callback, with action 3 hiding recommendations. The existing
history actions keep their meanings. Recommendation results and similar-title
requests share the profile-scoped local `similar_titles:v1:*` metadata cache;
Home's assembled row stays memory-only and artwork uses the normal image cache.
`AppWindow.home_featured_refresh_pending` forwards to Home's
`featured_refresh_pending`: a cached single-title list also gets a rotation
boundary for a pending refresh, without changing its visible title/count early.

**Animated feedback:** `menusheet.slint` stays mounted in Home, Library and
Detail: callers bind its `open` property instead of conditionally creating it.
The backdrop fades and the bottom panel slides using `Anim.transition_*`;
closing disables row actions immediately and retains the tap-swallowing exit
layer until the slide ends. Reopening cancels the exit timer, and disabling
motion snaps immediately. `player.slint` keeps its click-through gesture pill
mounted so both reveal and dismissal fade/slide with `Anim.player_*`; seeking
and restoring the held playback rate still happen immediately.

> When adding a UI property/callback: declare it in the page component **and**
> in `AppWindow`, forward it in the `AppWindow` child wiring block, then handle it
> in `src/app/run.rs`.

---

## 5. Persistence

### Primary store: redb (`nova-storage`)
Single database at `<data_dir>/nova.redb`, table `kv` (`&str → &str`). Open once
via `nova_storage::init_at(dir)` before any read/write. The `try_*` APIs distinguish
missing keys from database/transaction failures; legacy convenience wrappers
report failures through `last_error`. Unreadable app snapshots are write-blocked,
and Settings displays a persistence warning. The typed `local_sequences_v1` table
(`&str → u64`) holds device-local monotonic counters, separate from JSON KV values
and synced records. `playback:boot_epoch:v1` reserves a boot epoch atomically
before the playback transport starts; its initial value is `1 << 63` to exceed
normal epochs from older wall-clock-based clients. Later allocations increment
the committed value, and storage/schema/exhaustion failures stop setup rather
than reusing an epoch. Keys used by the app:

| Key | Content |
|---|---|
| `onboarding:pending:v1`, `onboarding:completed:v1` | Device-local markers (`1`), never synced. Pending is written before any first-run defaults; completion removes it atomically after preference persistence. A preexisting database without pending is conservatively an upgrade, even when empty. Pending resumes only with readable authoritative settings/addon/library/sync state. |
| `interface_mode` | Device-local `nova_config::InterfaceMode` JSON: `automatic`, `standard` or `tv`. Automatic checks Android television mode; this key never enters settings sync. |
| `settings` | `CacheSettings` JSON (image cache + display + player backend/decoder + playback rate). Image use limits are optional `u32` longest-side values: `detail_poster_max_side` (480), `detail_backdrop_max_side` (960), `episode_thumbnail_max_side` (360), and `player_episode_backdrop_max_side` (Original). Legacy `downscale: true` sets only an absent player limit to 1024; retired lazy/downscale controls are discarded. Disk caching, compression format/quality, decoded memory budget and all resolution controls remain in the existing atomic settings JSON and existing coupled sync group; no new key or ALPN. Display’s `language` and Theme’s `background_style` (Default / Accent-tinted / True black, migrated from legacy `true_black`), `accent_color` (snake-case `AccentColor`, default `purple`), `card_corner_radius`, `button_corner_radius`, `card_spacing`, `status_bar_gradient`, `transparent_navbar`, `home_backdrop_size`, `detail_backdrop_size` and `hero_title_alignment` sync by default and support device overrides. Display's `catalog_grouping_infer_series` defaults off and syncs independently with device overrides; its boolean controls only optional inferred Series presentation. Display's `discover_catalog_addon_names` defaults on and syncs as a settings field; hiding prefixes changes only dropdown labels, not catalog identity. Home's selected catalog/genre list, `home_devices_enabled` section toggle (true by default), and `home_episode_artwork` preference also sync as settings fields; episode artwork supports device overrides. Player backend choice (`player_external`, `desktop_external_app`), decoders (`android_hwdec`, `desktop_hwdec`; Windows defaults to copy-back, Linux to direct decoding with fallback), Android return behavior (`android_auto_continue`, false by default), PiP auto-entry (`android_pip_enabled`, true by default), playback auto-rotation (`android_auto_rotate`, true by default), and Sync’s receiver opt-out (`remote_control_enabled`, true by default) are device-local. Turning off remote control retires cached playback replies and closes incoming playback connections without removing sync membership. Global audio/subtitle language defaults (`default_audio_language`, `default_subtitle_language`; comma-separated language codes in priority order, existing single codes remain valid, empty = Automatic, subtitle `off` = Off) and playback speed (`playback_speed`, 0.5–2.0×) sync by default and support device overrides. Episode start behavior (`episode_start_behavior`: Start over / Resume / Ask) syncs as an independent settings field and supports device overrides. Image disk limit (`image_disk_cache_limit_mb`, optional MiB, default 1024; null = Unlimited) syncs independently with device overrides, outside the coupled image recipe group. Chapter timeline display (`show_chapters`, true by default) syncs independently with device overrides; it hides only dividers and the clickable current-title shortcut, preserving embedded chapter data and the Chapters menu. Screenshot preferences (`screenshot_format`: `png` / `jpeg` / `webp_lossless` / `webp_lossy`, JPEG/WebP quality fields and `screenshot_show_folder`) sync independently and support device overrides; defaults retain PNG, 85 quality and the plain Pictures/Nova folder. Their Player controls travel through SettingsBridge, page bindings, AppWindow and `settings.rs`; the single quality row follows the selected lossy format in keyboard navigation. `sync_overrides` is a device-local map of override fields to fallback shared values; local values stay in the existing fields. General-setting publication masks overridden fields (including members of the coupled cache group), incoming records update their shared baselines while preserving effective values, and override removal replaces the map rather than reviving absent keys through unknown-field preservation. |
| `updates:v1` | Device-local automatic-check preference, next-check/server cooldown/notice deadlines, ETag and validated stable-release tag. Never synced. Feature-disabled builds never read or write it. |
| `desktop:window:v1` | Device-local desktop normal logical width/height, maximized state and playback fullscreen choice. Restored by `desktop_window.rs`; never synced. |
| `library` | `watch_status` supports explicit `PlanToWatch` alongside `Auto`, `OnHold` and `Dropped`. The shared Detail/library options pin Plan to Watch without altering progress, history or categories; Back to automatic restores progress-based placement. Manual pins stay out of Home active episode rails and sync/backup with their library entry. Logical `Vec<LibraryEntry>` JSON, physically one ordered `state_rows_v1` record per JSON tuple `[type_, id]` after its next write. `CompositeArray` atomically rekeys legacy single-ID rows; logical entry JSON remains unchanged. Library membership, upsert/removal and sync use the same typed identity. Optional `imported_tracking_status` preserves an imported MAL/AniList shell's category until exact local playback/activity or a manual status takes precedence; absent in legacy entries and ignored by older readers. It never fabricates episode history. |
| `addons` | `Vec<AddonStore>` JSON (desired addons, including entries with unavailable manifests, and optional package-declared Streams/Metadata capabilities). Configure-page reachability is device-local. |
| `catalog_grouping_evidence:v1` | Device-local catalog parent/season evidence cache (`crates/providers/src/catalog_grouping/cache.rs`, `src/app/io.rs`), envelope version 5, bounded to 128 entries / 512 KiB. Positive evidence lasts seven days; unconfirmed lookup cooldown is 30 seconds. Keys include exact addon ownership, metadata inventory revision and separate confirmed/inferred policy namespaces; disabled-policy negatives cannot suppress later inference, and confirmed parent evidence wins. Inferred proof retains a minimal native parent preview/provenance for display, not a duplicated episode inventory. Derived evidence never syncs. |
| `provider_metadata_cache:v1` | Device-local confirmed provider mappings and episode artwork (`metadata/cache.rs`), bounded to 128 entries / 8 MiB / 256 KiB per entry; never synced. Corrupt storage is preserved. |
| `providers:bundled:v1`, `providers:supernova:v1` | Retained legacy registration markers; production startup no longer reads them or registers bundled providers. |
| `manifest:{url}` | Cached addon `Manifest` JSON (one per addon). |
| `home:showcase:v1` | Device-local featured catalog results and hydrated preview fields keyed by the full addon URL/type/catalog-ID/genre selection. Restored before startup network work; successful catalog refreshes persist immediately and replace the visible list on its next carousel step, retaining richer known fields when the new preview is sparse. Failed requests keep cached batches. Opaque IDs retain catalog ownership; recognized global IDs deduplicate across sources. Images use the ordinary poster cache, with backdrop → poster fallback. Not synced. |
| `discover:search_history:v2` | Local-only JSON MRU list of 20 completed query/context pairs (2–256 character queries), including addon URL, catalog identity, type and genre/category plus display caption. Exact available scope replays first; a missing catalog falls back to its addon, and an unavailable addon falls back to global search. Legacy `discover:search_history` query strings load as global entries when v2 is absent/unreadable. Clear history empties both keys; never synced. |
| `settings.stream_filters`, `settings.smart_collections` | Typed fields in the existing profile settings JSON; both default to inactive/empty. Nested `stream_filters` projects fourteen virtual field-level sync keys for enablement, resolution cap, excluded resolutions, resolution priority, resolution strictness, size, allowed/blocked codecs, codec priority, audio source/custom order, audio strictness, codec strictness, sort order and unknown metadata, each with an independent device override baseline. The backward-compatible `excluded_resolutions: Vec<u16>`, `resolution_priority: Vec<u16>` and `require_preferred_resolution: bool` fields default to empty/false; normalization retains exact heights 144–8640, deduplicating in order. Their independent scopes are `stream_filters_excluded_resolutions`, `stream_filters_resolution_priority` and `stream_filters_require_resolution`. Legacy aggregate records/overrides migrate without publishing local values; explicit new fields take precedence. Preferred audio is inherited by default, while legacy custom lists remain custom. Smart membership is derived rather than persisted. No wire/ALPN change. |
| `viewing_journal:v1:{session_id}` | Profile-owned durable dated sessions, actual watched time, UTC day/month allocations, episode and title completion dates, rewatch flags. Each session is a separate record in the profile-qualified `viewing_sessions` domain; checkpoints replace the same session rather than adding time twice. New session IDs include device identity; existing rows seed without resurrecting tombstones. Legacy migration runs once per owner per process on a background queue. Differential projection validates outside mutation locks and rechecks record versions under per-owner checkpoint serialization before committing only changed rows. No legacy date inference; no wire/ALPN change. |
| `watch_queue:v1`, `watch_queue:v2` | Legacy v1 is migrated once into v2 snapshot plus profile-scoped `watch_queue_items`, `watch_queue_order`, `watch_queue_cursor`; membership, coherent LWW order/autoplay and cursor are independent. Active queue runs are device/session-local. |
| `stream_family_preferences:v1` | Profile-scoped durable release-family preference mirror and `stream_family_preferences` records. Original selection timestamps/expiry remain intact; queued cache flushes compare expected data before replacing imported state. |
| `profile_policy:v1` | Profile-scoped whole-policy LWW record in `profile_policy`: plaintext PIN, entry lock, restricted flag and typed-title/exact-addon allowlists. Missing policy defaults locally without creating a live sync record. |
| `offline_policies:v1`, `smart_downloads:device:v1` | Personal show rules sync in `offline_policies`; enabled profiles, allowance, network/power execution and local suppression remain device-local. Transfer manifests retain physical smart/manual ownership and reservations. |
| `release_subscriptions:v1`, `release_delivery:v1`, `calendar_export:v1` | Profile-scoped subscribed typed titles with a default-compatible episode/full-season mode (synced `release_subscriptions`), device notification receipts and portable stable-UID export ledger. Delivery activation/receipts are not synced. |
| `backup:recovery:v1:*`, `backup:activation:v1` | Device-local selected-component recovery snapshots and durable post-transaction activation receipt. Undo writes compensating edits instead of rewinding sync clocks or peers. |
| `scene_bookmarks:v1`, `secondary_subtitles:v1` | Profile-owned timestamp/note lists per typed title/episode and device-local semantic second-language subtitle choices per typed title. Bookmarks decompose into individual scene records in the profile-qualified `scene_bookmarks` domain, including note edits and deletion tombstones; stable IDs, creation dates and source display metadata remain in the compatible local archive. New IDs include device identity; legacy IDs remain readable. `secondary_subtitle_positions:v1` retains the local Automatic/Top/Bottom choice per title. Secondary subtitle choices/placement remain device-local; no wire/ALPN change. |
| `playback_preferences:v1` | JSON map keyed by `(media type, title id, control)` tuples: semantic audio/subtitle choices (language and kind; untagged titles match only uniquely), explicit subtitle Off, and speed overrides. Independent LWW records in the `playback_preferences` string domain; resetting a control tombstones only that record. Titles need not be in the library. Track/reset edits persist promptly; speed drags debounce 600 ms and flush on close/replacement/background. Remote edits apply on the next playback, with corruption-protected projection under `ApplyingGuard`. |
| `episode_progress` | `HashMap<String, EpisodeProgress>` JSON (watch history). Each played record may include `history` (typed title/poster/episode metadata, actual-playback timestamp and rewatch flag); older JSON remains readable, and the existing progress sync domain carries these fields. Tracking = 250 ms tick mirroring mpv props (`playback.rs::note_player_progress_from_ui`); closing rebuilds Home Continue/New episodes/Upcoming cards from finalized progress before returning to the page: saves throttled to 30 s / 5 s delta (time-based saves skipped while paused), finalize-on-close, Android external player untracked (desktop video handoff is disabled). Series and movies both tracked: a movie's record is keyed `id\x01id`. |
| `viewing_activity:v1` | `HashMap<String, ViewingActivity>` JSON; phase Active / AwaitingReturn per title. Synced in `viewing_activity`, retained with history after removal, and written atomically with local episode progress. Metadata may initialize a missing completed checkpoint but cannot activate viewing. |
| `new_episodes_hidden:v1` | `HashMap<String, Vec<String>>` JSON; hidden available episode-ID batches per title. Synced in `new_episodes_hidden`; another released episode resurfaces the card independently of Continue Watching hides. |
| `watch_history_hidden:v1` | `HashMap<String, u64>` JSON; typed title identity encoded as a JSON `(media_type, id)` tuple maps to a removal timestamp. Synced in `watch_history_hidden`; preserved progress and watched status are untouched. A newer actual-playback clock resurfaces the title; metadata/manual progress edits do not. |
| `continue_hidden` | `HashMap<String, u64>` JSON — Continue Watching items the user removed, `id -> removal unix secs`. Local mirror of the synced `continue_hidden` domain (so the choice survives with sync off); read at startup in `run.rs`, cleared for an item when playback of it is armed. |
| `plugin_settings` | Ordinary JSON plugin/field map, including masked TorBox keys. Synced as independent `plugin_settings` records (`plugin/field`), with tombstones for explicit deletion and opaque unknown fields retained. Startup restores records retained by older clients before seeding a missing snapshot. Removing/disabling an addon preserves configuration; the separate confirmed removal action deletes it across paired devices. |
| `addon_packages:v1` | JSON map from provider ID to `{id, version, sha256}` desired package descriptors; syncs in the `addon_packages` domain. Archives remain separate from record values. |
| `addon_package_ids:v1` | Local known provider IDs retained across removal/unavailability to protect native identity routing. |
| `cache_gc:image:v1`, `cache_gc:providers:v1` | Plain local KV scheduling markers containing the cache directory and last successful cleanup time. Missing/corrupt markers, changed directories or future clocks make a pass due. They never sync. |
| `<app_cache_dir>/posters/.image-usage-v1.json` | Local versioned artwork usage index beside the `.variant` files (the actual image directory is returned by `poster_cache_dir`). Last-used times and size accounting batch every two minutes; clean shutdown flushes, while missing/corrupt/unclean indexes rebuild incrementally from bounded variant headers. Image payloads are unchanged. |
| `<app_cache_dir>/plugins/cache-v1.json` | Device-local provider discovery, verified-list and normalized metadata cache; TTL/LRU, 20 MiB logical value budget, 1024 entries and 1 MiB per entry. `src/app/plugin_cache.rs` atomically writes the disposable cache under `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/nova` (normally `~/.cache/nova`) on Linux and Android’s app cache directory. Startup migrates and removes legacy `plugin_cache:v1` from app data. Missing/corrupt files are cache misses. Settings fingerprints and generations reject stale values; successful cache clearing invalidates old operations and preserves settings, images and torrents. Supernova discovery lasts up to 24 hours, verified lists up to 15 minutes bounded by discovery expiry, normalized metadata up to 24 hours and misses 60 seconds. A worker checks an in-memory daily deadline every two minutes and on resume/settings wakes; a successful pass persists `cache_gc:providers:v1`. Due passes prune expired, invalid and obsolete-settings entries without changing settings generations. Unchanged cache snapshots skip backend writes; expiry removed on reads is persisted on the next write or due pass. Expiry is also enforced on reads. No credentials or expiring playback URLs are cached. |
| `<app_cache_dir>/last-stream-v1.json` | Local mirror of the single `last_stream/selection` JSON sync record (version, SHA-256 source fingerprint, selection time) in `nova.redb`. Fixed seven-day TTL, bounded 512-byte reads, expiry/future-clock/corruption rejection and background atomic writes/deletion. Reads/remote projection never extend expiry; the worker tombstones expired owner values under the owner lock, keeping the original version so newer local/offline selections remain intact. Only a returned matching row is promoted after downloads; filters and all remaining owner ranks stay intact. |
| `providers:supernova:v1` | Retired bundled-provider registration marker; existing rows are left untouched, and production startup no longer reads it. |
| `torrent_settings` | `TorrentSettings` JSON (runtime mirror). |
| `torrent_cache` | Tracked torrents (`infohash → dir/len/file/last-used`) so restarts adopt downloads instead of orphaning them from trim/clear accounting. Retained offline owners are restored from `downloads:v1` before sweeping; cache-origin bundle records remain tracked while any download owner uses them. |
| `stremio:state:v1:<JSON account>:<JSON remote ID or (remote ID,native type,native ID) tuple>` | Device-local Stremio raw title/mapping snapshots, independent canonical/native-binding baselines and pending delivery/retry state. Defaulted `selection` retains reviewed per-title consent/mapping snapshots; defaulted `binding` retains native identities; legacy single-source rows migrate atomically to tuple-suffixed rows. Defaulted `progress_merged` defers each binding's initial history union until metadata is available. Mapping `version` and `local_inventory_revision` default to zero for older rows and trigger refresh under current rules. Shared account and field records remain remote-title scoped. |
| `list_services:boundary:v1:<JSON(service,account,type,ID,grant)>`, `list_services:watched:v1:<JSON(series ID,episode ID)>`, `list_services:simkl_cache:v1:<JSON account>` | Profile-local per-device grant journal boundaries, semantic local watched-transition sequence and verified Simkl activities/library checkpoint. `profiles::scoped_key` namespaces the complete `list_services:` prefix; unowned global rows are ignored rather than adopted as a profile's upload authority during legacy migration. They never enter peer state; incoming grants capture the receiving journal boundary. See [list-service persistence](list-services.md#persistence-and-paired-devices). |
| `tracking:state:v1` | Versioned local-only tracking accounts (no tokens), active account choices, service automatic-tracking pauses, targets, explicit episode bindings, projection checkpoints, target display snapshots, manual field edits, link-time journal cursors, local completion-prompt queues/watermarks/lifetime receipts, and the progress outbox (including authentication pauses and service cooldowns). Completion score intents carry an only-if-still-unrated guard plus mapping/account evidence; handled receipts alone sync through the separate `tracking_completion` domain. Tracking checkpoints and intents commit together; interrupted delivery markers recover as uncertain and require a remote read. The envelope is schema 5 (schemas 1–4 remain readable); per-source accepted-progress contributions allow revoked bulk grants to drop unsent progress while retaining acknowledged remote baselines. The storage key stays stable so older binaries refuse the newer schema. `src/app/sync.rs` journals semantic watched and meaningful internal playback-start transitions alongside history; `journal.rs` uses link-time cursors and excludes paired-device origins from upload. `nova-tracking::Store` validates before committing and publishes memory only after a successful write. Unreadable snapshots retain their primary value plus a deterministic `tracking:quarantine:*` backup; unsupported schema versions remain unavailable for writes. |
| `tracking:library:v1:<JSON AccountKey>`, `profile:<profile ID>:tracking_library` | Retired MAL/AniList discovery caches and full-library policy records. They remain inert on disk; the app no longer discovers or imports account libraries or projects this policy domain. The serialized `Binding.bulk_origin` marker still identifies retired bulk-only links so upgraded devices remove those links without changing explicit per-title grants or deleting imported titles/history. |
| `tracking:event_counter`, `tracking:events:<sequence>`, `tracking:journal_error` | Local-only monotonic semantic playback journal. Saved atomically with `episode_progress` and sync records; consumed rows are deleted atomically with tracking checkpoints/intents. Position-only saves create no watched transitions, and paired-device origins do not authorize uploads. An unreadable/exhausted counter retains its primary value and sets a recovery marker without preventing history saves. Explicit reset holds the sync/history store lock, backs up journal rows, and clears the journal/counter/marker atomically. |
| `tracking:clients:v1` | Public client IDs and registered redirect URLs only, local to this device. Nova registrations are bundled as defaults in `tracking/worker.rs`; saved custom registrations take precedence and previously empty service cards receive the defaults. Malformed originals receive `tracking:clients:quarantine:*` backups; credential tokens live in separate records shared through the tracking domain. |
| `tracking:credentials:mal:v1`, `tracking:credentials:anilist:v1` | Plaintext JSON credentials (version 1), shared through the tracking domain: verified account, public client registration, access/refresh tokens and expiry. Account choice and credentials commit together; startup verifies identity before resuming. MAL refresh rotation persists before subsequent network reads. Disconnect deletes one record and syncs the removal; local tracking reset deletes both and publishes configuration tombstones without backing up tokens. Protected platform storage is a documented future addition in [tracking behavior](tracking-behavior.md#credential-storage-and-future-protection). |
| `tracking:source:<JSON(type,id)>` | Latest typed source identity snapshot, up to 32 displaced historical ID hints, bounded aliases, title, year and retrieval time, keyed by the unchanged addon source identity. The defaulted `historical_ids` field keeps older JSON readable; hints cannot establish a current identity or seed alignment automatically. |
| `tracking:catalog_cache:v1` | Metadata-only search and release-details cache, sharing a 128-record bound (20 media per search, 16 aliases / 64 relations per release). Search API provenance and expiry remain one hour / five minutes; release details expire after one hour. The optional JSON release-details field keeps older caches readable. Transport errors never become negative results. |
| `downloads:v1` | Local-only `DownloadManifest`: queued/downloading/paused/completed/failed stream jobs, source identity (including required HTTP headers), progress, validators, artifact paths, and optional `torrent_directory` / resolved `torrent_file_idx` (older jobs recover their bundle from `torrent_cache` or the default hash directory). Missing headers in older jobs deserialize as empty. Corrupt manifests are quarantined as `downloads:v1.corrupt.<ts>`. On load, interrupted transfers return to `Queued`; completed jobs are validated against disk with **canonicalized** paths (Android reports the same dir as `/data/user/0/...` and `/data/data/...`), and completed HTTP artifacts found under `<data>/downloads/http/` that the manifest no longer references are re-adopted as best-effort entries (torrent artifacts are not, since a sparse partial file is indistinguishable from a complete one). |
| `meta_header:{type}\x01{id}` | Cached detail-header snapshot, including optional `details` (runtime/rating/status/country/language, cast/crew, safe trailer links and source-supplied related previews; old JSON defaults to empty), including season-specific backdrop URLs (`season_backdrops`, defaulting to empty for older caches). Fresh nonempty backdrop/season artwork updates cached URLs even for finished shows; Home can reuse these snapshots. `poster_url` stores the last successfully decoded poster for Discover reuse (defaults to empty for older JSON); this remains device-local. |
| `similar_titles:v1:{type}\x01{id}` | Profile-scoped, device-local metadata cache of bounded similar-title previews, fetch timestamp and installed-source/plugin-settings/TMDB-configuration fingerprint. Runtime generations are excluded from disk identity so unchanged configurations reuse results after restart. Fresh results last 15 minutes; matching stale rows remain usable for seven days during refresh failures. It uses the metadata compression/eviction policy and is never synced. |
| `sync:identity` | Ed25519 secret key hex (stable endpoint id). |
| `sync:settings` | `SyncSettings` JSON (enabled, app-running interval, background-sync toggle, device name, confirmation, local-discovery toggle). Local only, never synced. |
| `sync:records` | Legacy whole-store JSON blob; migrated once to the per-record rows below, then removed. |
| `srec:{len}:{domain}{key}` | One sync record row: `Record` JSON (`value` + `version`). Written individually (batched in one transaction on save), so a change touches one row instead of the whole store. |
| `sync:records:hlc` | Persisted hybrid logical clock. |
| `sync:quarantine:<hash>` | Content-addressed JSON evidence (`key`, `raw`), outside active row prefixes; quarantining commits evidence and removal atomically. Old corrupt backups are retained/recovered without recursive suffixes. |
| `sync:baseline:<domain>` | Materialized app values used as deletion/edit authority; unseen remote rows never participate in snapshot diffs. |
| `sync:projection:pending` | Durable set of remotely changed domains awaiting projection; replayed on attachment, the UI poll, and restart. |
| `sync:library_keys_v2`, `sync:library_legacy_removals:<domain>` | Startup-only typed-key migration marker and local legacy bare-ID deletion Versions. Barriers materialize canonical tombstones when matching older records arrive, then ordinary sync propagates them. |
| `sync:self_authorized_at` | Local explicit participation-grant Version, committed atomically by either pairing role or manual add; rejects older forwarded self-removals. |
| `sync:peer_acks` | Per-peer ack HLC map (local only; gates tombstone GC). |
| `sync:invites` / `sync:pending_join` | Outstanding invites / in-flight join. |

The `settings` JSON also includes `home_catalog_sources` (featured banner) and
`home_rows` (ordered `HomeRow` entries: a tagged `HomeRowSource` identifying
Continue Watching, Watch history, New episodes, Upcoming, or an addon catalog, plus an enabled flag). Fresh installs enable all four built-ins in that order. Addon
identity stores install URL, media type, catalog ID and optional genre. A missing
or null `home_rows` lazily migrates legacy `home_row_sources`,
`home_continue_enabled` and `home_upcoming_enabled`; an explicit empty list means
no landing catalogs. Missing legacy visibility flags default to enabled; explicit
disabled, reordered or removed rows remain unchanged. Layout edits write both the new list and legacy compatibility
fields. Disabled addon selections keep their local cached metadata. Selections
survive unavailable/removed manifests, and the list syncs as one settings field;
no sync wire schema changes are required.

Local metadata keys (`episodes:*`, `meta_header:*`, `similar_titles:*`, `manifest:*`,
`provider_metadata_cache:v1`, `catalog_grouping_evidence:v1`, legacy `plugin_cache:v1` (migrated out at startup), `home:showcase:v1`, `tracking:catalog_cache:v1`)
use `metadata_cache_v1` in the same database. The logical format remains JSON.
`crates/storage/src/codec.rs` wraps values with an envelope version, codec and
uncompressed length; zstd level 3 with a checksum is used from 1 KiB when the
complete encoded value saves at least 10%. Small/unprofitable values stay raw
inside the envelope. Decoding bounds output and frame windows to 64 MiB; larger
writes retain legacy plain storage. Existing `kv` rows remain readable and move
atomically on their next metadata write. `home:catalog-rows:v1` currently remains
plain JSON because it is absent from the app's metadata compression policy.
Library and progress (plus `sync:baseline:library` / `sync:baseline:progress`)
use registered collections in `state_rows_v1`; settings, credentials and sync
record rows remain in `kv`. Compression does not alter sync wire formats,
metadata refresh policy or image files, and does not shrink existing database
allocation immediately. Equal JSON values (ignoring object key order) skip
writes; timestamps, array order and unknown fields still count as changes.

Helpers: `src/app/io.rs` (`read_json`/`write_json`), and per-domain
`read/write_persisted_*` functions. `nova-storage` exposes `write_batch`
(single transaction for many rows) and `scan_prefix` (ordered prefix scan),
which the sync store uses to persist per-record rows without rewriting a blob.
Synced app snapshots, baselines, dirty rows, and HLC commit in one batch through
`sync.rs::persist_sync_snapshot` and `nova_sync::prepare_snapshot`. Dirty work is
retained until commit succeeds. Settings enum recovery is field-isolated and
preserves unsupported raw values until that field is deliberately edited.

`collections.rs` keeps a versioned `__nova_rows_v1` descriptor (kind, count and
revision) under each collection's original `kv` key. Physical keys include the
collection-name byte length followed by the collection name and item identity;
array records carry stable ranks to preserve saved Library order. Deletions and
appends retain surviving ranks/bytes; deliberate reorders renumber them. `try_get_str`
and prefix scans rebuild the same logical JSON for callers. Legacy plain JSON
migrates on its next write in the same transaction as the sync records,
baselines, journal and HLC. Only changed rows are encoded/written; removed rows
are deleted. Small/unprofitable records stay raw, and oversized records retain
plain UTF-8 rather than exceeding the compressed codec's bound. Corrupt rows,
missing rows or invalid order fail closed and prevent replacement; explicit
collection removal still deletes corrupt payloads. The collection table is
authoritative user data and is never treated as a disposable metadata cache.
Older binaries cannot read this storage layout: the descriptor intentionally
fails their typed snapshot/baseline parsing. Downgrading requires a compatible
reader or restoring a pre-migration backup, not deleting the descriptor.

`src/app/persistence.rs` moves normal Library/history serialization, diffing,
compression and atomic writes to one ordered worker. App helpers expose the
newest queued snapshot, while the worker derives activity/journal transitions
from committed state. Adjacent periodic progress saves may coalesce; manual
edits retain ordering. Failed heads stay queued for retry, and errors reach the
existing Settings persistence warning. The queue applies backpressure at 64
pending snapshots. Pause/completion/close, Android backgrounding and orderly
shutdown flush; other synchronous snapshot writes and remote sync exchanges
drain the queue before taking the record-owner lock. Stremio materialization
queues record-sized external patches alongside local snapshots, recreating both
`ApplyingGuard` and external-origin guards on the writer; its actor awaits the
durable receipt off-thread. Queued logical readers overlay these patches in
order. Other remote projection remains synchronous under `ApplyingGuard` so
projection receipts follow durable saves. `Store::clock` exposes a cheap HLC
observation token for optimistic UI delivery without copying a full digest.
Diffing and logical reads still traverse collections; this avoids whole-blob
recompression and disk rewriting, not all linear in-memory work. No wire
schema/ALPN change or new compression setting is introduced.

### Legacy files
Legacy desktop `settings.toml`, `library.toml`, and `addons.toml` files are no
longer imported. Current settings, library entries, and addons are read only
from the KV store in `<data>/nova.redb`; any old files are left untouched.

### Paths (`nova-config`)
- Platform data and cache paths are listed in [platform-storage.md](platform-storage.md).
- Addon ZIP archives live under `<data>/addons/packages/<lowercase-sha256>.zip`; `src/app/addon_packages.rs` validates immutable content and registers only desired descriptors.
- Downloaded media lives under `<data>/downloads/` (private and durable; HTTP jobs use per-job `.part` files with `.part.resume.json` representation validators and torrent jobs use a retained subdirectory).
- `NOVA_DATA_DIR` / `NOVA_CACHE_DIR` are Android test/device-farm fallbacks.
- Linux and macOS use XDG roots; the platform-specific path helper honors custom
  `XDG_DATA_HOME` and `XDG_CACHE_HOME` values only when absolute; empty/relative roots fall back to HOME, then the temporary directory.

### Image cache (`nova-media::cache`)
The byte-budgeted decoded LRU and disk cache share a key made from source URL
and the resolved processing recipe, so consumers with identical recipes share
decoded entries. Each disk variant is one atomic `.variant` file: `NVIMAGE2`
magic, a length-prefixed JSON header, then processed encoded bytes. The
versioned header stores source URL, registered uses, dimensions, resolved
recipe and a checksum of the encoded payload. No separate metadata/use files or
original-image backup are retained. Source pixels are transient and are
resized before Slint receives them. Compression uses JPEG or WebP at the
selected quality, with PNG fallback for alpha that JPEG cannot preserve;
resizing without compression writes PNG, while Original with compression off
keeps source bytes. Disk caching off is a pure in-memory mode. Same-recipe fetches coalesce,
and a fresh download is encoded and decoded at the recipe's quality before
publication, matching disk content. Atomic writes preserve the prior valid
variant on failure. A recipe change fetches the source again and never upscales
an existing processed variant. Per-URL locks serialize disk changes. Legacy
`.img`/`.cfg` files are invalidated only after a successful replacement;
startup does not sweep them. Cache clear advances a generation, and loads also
capture the image-settings revision; stale work cannot repopulate cleared cache
state or publish pixels for superseded settings. Per-recipe publication tickets
order overlapping ordinary loads, forced refreshes and maintenance snapshots:
an older completion cannot replace a newer successful source in disk, memory
or delivery, even if the newer pixels were evicted or exceed the LRU budget.
Tickets retain completion pixels only while an older load or refresh needs the
newer result; queued maintenance snapshots retain ordering metadata only. The
decoded LRU remains the sole retained pixel cache. Maintenance also watches
its use-registration snapshot, including settings changes away and back, and
skips registrations rebuilt by a newer load. Disk envelope/storage schemas are
unchanged.
`cache_gc.rs` tracks successful disk publications and memory/disk hits in a
single `.image-usage-v1.json` index beside the images. Hits only update memory;
dirty recency batches every two minutes and never rewrite variant payloads.
An orderly shutdown flushes a clean marker; missing/corrupt/unclean indexes
rebuild from bounded headers. Daily reconciliation and orphan-temp cleanup are
gated by local DB timestamps supplied by `src/app/cache_gc.rs`; cheap checks and
Android resume do not themselves scan. Workers yield after 128 files or about
20 ms and pause when disk caching is off. Going over the configured limit uses
the index immediately to evict toward 90% of the cap. Invalid variants go first,
then older browsing artwork, then Library artwork if needed. Library preference
is soft, not unlimited retention. Recent accesses and active publications defer
eviction; existing maintenance/generation/per-URL guards protect clear,
re-encode and load races. Screenshots and downloads are outside this cache.

The memory budget counts decoded pixels owned by this cache. Visible Slint
images, crossfading artwork, transient processing buffers and GPU allocations
remain outside it; it is not a limit on total process memory.

The re-encode action snapshots settings, fetches each registered source URL
once, rebuilds its registered uses under current recipes, and supports cancel
or per-entry failure. It may download those sources again. Clear cache removes
all variant files and usage metadata. Resolution dropdowns offer Original and
240, 360, 480, 720, 960, 1024, 1280, 1920 or 2560 pixels; changes autosave
immediately and work with disk caching or compression disabled. Home keeps its
existing original-resolution showcase/backdrop and logo behavior, with 480px
cards. Four resolution preferences extend the existing settings JSON; no sync
domains or wire schemas were added.

---

## 6. Cross-device sync (`crates/sync` + `src/app/sync.rs`)

The most intricate subsystem. Sync is **opt-in** (`sync:settings.enabled`);
nothing binds a socket until `SyncEngine::setup()` runs.

Correctness and recovery work is tracked in
[`sync-hardening-plan.md`](sync-hardening-plan.md) (implemented contracts, regression matrix, and remaining device/power validation).

### Design
- Generic, app-agnostic store: `domain -> key -> Record { value, version }`.
- `Version` = HLC (`physical_ms`, `counter`) + `dev` id + `deleted` flag; LWW merge with tombstones. See `merge.rs`, `hlc.rs`, `store.rs`.
- Socket-free `local_store` owns records even with networking disabled. Fresh untouched settings seed only a baseline; explicit settings edits publish intent even when selecting the default. Legacy persisted settings conservatively seed absent keys. Receive/reload reject or quarantine clocks beyond the one-hour drift bound.
- Progress values carry JSON activity/watch/unwatch action registers (`__nova_progress_v1`), merged by deterministic action version. Watch stays sticky unless superseded by explicit unwatch; rewinds use the activity register, position/duration stay coherent, and play count takes max. Legacy values initialize registers from their record version.
- Anti-entropy: each peer exchanges a **per-domain hash** of its version map, then full version maps only for domains that differ, then just the records the other lacks or has stale. One bidirectional QUIC stream per exchange (`protocol.rs`); an unchanged domain transfers no digest at all.
- **Connection reuse:** the dialer keeps one connection per peer and opens a fresh bi stream per pass; the accept handler serves streams for the connection's lifetime. A closed/failed connection is evicted and redialed (`lib.rs`).
- **Local change scheduling:** `crates/sync/src/lib.rs` batches ordinary successful edits for 2 seconds with a 10-second maximum; periodic playback checkpoints use a fixed 120-second automatic window. `prepare_snapshot` reports actual record mutations, so no-op writes and remote projections do not schedule echoes. Pause, completion, close and episode replacement save progress then immediately flush pending changes once through the existing worker, preserving peer backoff. Ordinary automatic edits wait while backgrounded; Android Pause checkpoints through the app bridge before UI timers suspend and allows one live-owner handoff pass. Periodic sync remains unchanged and may transmit playback earlier. No new settings, persisted fields or protocol changes.
- **Recovery/cadence:** periodic wakes honor monotonic per-peer backoff; explicit `sync_now` and completed network refreshes reset backoff for one bounded, coalesced pass. Overlapping network refreshes coalesce, and recovery wakes only after iroh refresh finishes. Interval/foreground changes recompute the pending periodic deadline immediately (`lib.rs`).
- Sync frames are **postcard** inside a codec byte with optional **zstd level 3** (`frame.rs`, shared zstd dependency); pairing and removal keep raw postcard. postcard is non-self-describing, so schema changes require an ALPN bump. Current ALPNs:
  - `nova/sync/6` (`ALPN`) — record exchange (per-domain digest hashes + compressed frames). During testing the compressed codec changed from DEFLATE (1) to zstd (2) without an ALPN bump; old compressed frames are rejected and both devices must update. Bodies from 256 bytes compress only when smaller; output and decoder windows are capped at 32 MiB. Encoders disclose the known postcard length to reduce small-frame workspace; declared oversized content is rejected before decompression.
  - `nova/pair/5` (`PAIR_ALPN`) — invite-ticket pairing; version boundary requires both devices to update for typed Library keys and authorization-based membership, despite unchanged postcard frame layout. Local JSON and legacy Library records migrate without resetting their clocks.
  - `nova/remove/1` (`REMOVE_ALPN`) — one-shot "you were removed" notice
  - `nova/party/3` (`nova-party::ALPN`) — independent lazy endpoint/router, not the sync endpoint. Version-2 `NVP2` expiring capability invites bind temporary guests to authenticated endpoint identities; only bounded media identity, clock/timeline and readiness frames are shared. No record sync, pairing or addon transfer is registered; no guest becomes a sync peer.
  - `nova/playback/5` (`PLAYBACK_ALPN`) — separate ephemeral playback requests/snapshots on the existing paired endpoint, with bounded 256 KiB postcard frames, an eight-value subtitle-style snapshot including background opacity (schema expanded from seven values), peer membership checks, connection reuse, request-ID replay protection and revocation of queued work. It does not enter record anti-entropy or persisted sync state.
  - `nova/addon-package/1` (`PACKAGE_ALPN`) — raw content-addressed ZIP transfer, capped at 8 MiB, separate from record exchange. Paired membership is checked before and during transfer; cancellation and engine shutdown interrupt pending work.

### Crate files
| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `lib.rs` | `SyncEngine` (owns iroh `Endpoint`, tokio runtime, router, worker loops), settings, peer allowlist, `run_pass`/`sync_one`, per-peer connection cache, singleton `install`/`engine`, peers-domain helpers, per-peer acks. |
| `protocol.rs` | Sync `Handler` (serves streams for a reused connection), `Wire` frames, `run`/`exchange`, digest hashing, `apply_frames`, `RemoveHandler`/`notify_removed`. |
| `playback.rs` | Independent playback protocol handler/client on `PLAYBACK_ALPN`; authenticates current paired membership before dispatch and while requests are pending, binds request IDs to peer/boot epoch/content, bounds cached responses, and releases held previews on connection loss. `SyncEngine::set_playback_handler` injects the foreground app service; a headless engine reports unavailable. |
| `package.rs` | `PackageBlobStore` injection, strict lowercase SHA-256 request IDs, bounded raw transfer, membership/cancellation guards, per-peer and total timeouts; fetch returns bytes for app validation. |
| `pair.rs` | `PairHandler`, invite lifecycle, join/accept flow, `PairEvent`. |
| `ticket.rs` | Invite ticket codec (`NV1` + base32) and match code. |
| `store.rs` | Durable `Store` (per-record rows in redb via `nova-storage`), digest/outbound/apply/GC, legacy-blob migration. |
| `local.rs` | Socket-free record owner, atomic app snapshot/baseline preparation, unknown object-field preservation. |
| `progress.rs` | Convergent activity/watch/unwatch registers embedded in progress JSON and action-clock validation. |
| `merge.rs` | `Version`, `Record`, `resolve` (LWW + tombstone tie-break). |
| `hlc.rs` | Hybrid logical clock (tick/observe, forward-drift cap). |
| `frame.rs` | Length-prefixed postcard framing (raw + codec byte with zstd, checksums and bounded output/window memory). |

Live playback uses `nova-playback-api::{Request, Response, PlaybackAction}` rather
than sync records. The UI-thread coordinator rejects stale session commands,
keeps local playback and remote control mutually exclusive through loading,
and sends receiver-owned stream selections as opaque offers. Active control
snapshots are requested from the 250 ms UI tick (at most 4 Hz, with one request
in flight, alongside a separate stream lookup so controls remain responsive); paired-device summaries refresh every five seconds only while
Home, Devices or the stream selector is visible. Foreground readiness is
required; Android background jobs do not install a playback handler. Screenshot
commands save on the playing device using its existing screenshot preferences.
Receiver addons are queried concurrently and merged in installed order; lookup replies
carry up to 256 opaque offers, including receiver-computed display sizes, with
25-row selector paging. The controller keeps at most 32 per-peer display-only lookup results for the current selector, reusing matching media for under five minutes from lookup start when toggling output devices. Close, refresh, Start, expired entries, peer removal, or engine replacement invalidate reuse; receiver source/revision checks still apply. Offers expire after five
minutes, are checked against addon/settings revisions, and keep sources local. A lookup retains its original settings generation through addon batching and rejects stale batches before publishing offers.
Cached and fetched episode metadata use the same resolver: exact native IDs take
precedence; otherwise only a unique current confirmed alias is accepted. Cached
aliases can therefore resolve receiver downloads without a live metadata addon.
Interactive commands follow normal receiver persistence and sync notifications;
controller snapshots never apply local progress or preference writes.
Detaching retires the controller connection without stopping receiver playback;
queued requests and held-speed cleanup remain bound to that connection so a
