reopened controller cannot inherit stale input. Continuous control updates coalesce
only with a matching queue tail, preserving intervening relative/reset commands.
Receiver resolver failures retain a failed session until Stop closes the error
overlay; snapshots report a generic failure without forwarding provider errors.
Requester boot epochs come from the durable local sequence allocator, preserving
stale-process rejection across restarts even when the system clock moves back.
The ordered epoch field is unchanged. Playback protocol v3 adds player-tool
commands and bounded snapshots for chapters, bookmarks, subtitle appearance,
secondary subtitles and intro skipping. Dynamic selections use receiver-owned
opaque tokens so a changed track list cannot retarget an old command. Alternate
source lookup stays on the receiver and preserves its current playback context.
Offers retain the receiver-formatted size (bounded to 32 bytes) for stream size
pills. Both playback participants must support v5. Profile policy separately
requires sync/pair semantic versions 6/5 across paired devices.

### Domains (app side, `src/app/sync.rs`)
Library records use collision-free JSON tuple keys `[type_, id]`. `migrate_library_keys` rekeys legacy records and materialized baselines without restamping actions; startup moves untyped legacy tombstones into durable local `sync:library_legacy_removals:<domain>` maps behind the `sync:library_keys_v2` marker. Those maps retain original deletion authority over older typed records without confusing opaque native IDs with current tuple keys. `Store::migrate_record_keys` captures all live sources before removing/rekeying legacy physical rows in the same save, including when a native ID equals another title's new typed key.

`library`, `progress`, `addons` (one record per addon URL plus a whole-value
LWW `order` record listing installed URLs, so reorder syncs mesh-wide), `addon_packages` (one `{id, version, sha256}` desired package descriptor per provider ID; raw archives transfer separately),
`last_stream` (one `selection` record containing only version, fingerprint and original selection time; LWW selection, seven-day expiry and guarded expiry tombstones),
`settings`, `plugin_settings` (independent plugin/field values including unknown fields and explicit tombstones; no wire schema/ALPN change), `category` (per-name union), `continue_hidden` (one record per Home
item the user removed, value = removal unix secs; tombstones carry the
"un-remove" when a resume or prune drops the key, and a stamp written while sync
was off is kept and published like `progress`), `watch_history_hidden` (one removal timestamp per typed title; existing generic string domains need no wire schema or ALPN change), plus
`tracking` (version-2 typed source/account/release links with intrinsic bulk provenance; version-1 links remain readable as explicit choices, plus automatic pauses and
shared sign-in tokens; projected by the tracking actor with local cursors and
atomic credential/link saves), `stremio` (shared version-1 auth-only `connection`, including plaintext auth key and explicit sign-in epoch; only browser auth replaces it and disconnect tombstones it. `session:<JSON(epoch)>:automatic` is an independent boolean LWW preference; `account:<JSON account>:mode` is an independent full/selected scope preference retained across sign-in epochs (active legacy session modes migrate once), with account-native `selection:*` records and an atomic `excluded_remote:*` index for reviewed mappings and exclusions; `...:started` and `...:bootstrap` are monotonic true-only markers. Independently timestamped `event:<JSON(account,type,native ID)>:<field>` and `title:<JSON(account,remote ID)>:<field>` rows retain source and merged actions; fields are `saved`, `resume`, or `watched:<JSON episode ID>`; `title:<JSON(account,remote ID)>:remote` stores the acknowledged server `_mtime` and canonical clocks as a JSON tuple, reusing original action times only when the server revision and field values match), `list_services` (version-1 verified `connection:<service>` credentials (0 Simkl, 1 MDBList, 2 Trakt; optional Trakt refresh token/expiry), account-qualified `account:*` automatic/full preferences, account-native `selected:*` enable/exclusion/history grants and retained `identity:*` proof that blocks canonical re-import after native removal; per-device journal boundaries and Simkl checkpoints remain local), `peers` (mesh membership) and `presence`
(engine-owned sightings, see below).
`sync_records` compares against the materialized baseline, not the live store;
only baseline keys absent from the edited snapshot become tombstones. New domains ride the existing
record exchange with no wire-schema change (domain names travel as strings);
the app ignores unknown domains. Restricted-profile enforcement uses semantic sync/pair versions6/5 so all paired clients must understand the policy.

### Peers / membership model
- The `peers` domain holds one record per peer endpoint id: value `{name, added_at, authorized_at}`; `authorized_at` is the explicit grant Version and routine name refreshes preserve it. Legacy JSON without the grant uses original `added_at` conservatively; deletion = tombstone.
- The **live allowlist** (`SyncEngine.peers`) is derived from the store (`reconcile_peers`) and is the trust boundary (`Handler::is_peer`).
- **Additions** happen only via explicit pairing / manual add (`peers_add`, may resurrect a tombstoned peer). A routine sync only refreshes an existing live peer's name (`peers_refresh`) and never inserts/resurrects.
- **Removals** are tombstones (`peers_remove`). Removal is made symmetric:
  - `SyncEngine::remove_peer` sends a best-effort `nova/remove/1` notice so the removed device drops the remover and shows a UI notice.
  - If the notice is missed, a structured QUIC application-close code (`REVOKED_CODE`) triggers mutual removal. An unknown/not-yet-authorized peer does not get that code. Authorization is rechecked for every stream and under the store lock before apply; cached dialed connections close on removal.
- **Membership merge** compares explicit grants against removals before name-update versions. A later sync ack or rename cannot override removal; unequal peer versions are exchanged even when the winning membership action has an older transport version. A newer explicit grant can restore trust.
- **Presence (last-seen):** the engine-owned `presence` domain holds one record per `{observer}\x01{peer}` sighting (value = unix secs), written after each completed exchange and throttled to one rewrite per 15 min per peer. Each device writes only its own observer keys (no merge conflicts); readers take the max across observers plus the local ack clock (`SyncEngine::peer_last_seen`, shown as `SyncPeer.last_seen`). `peers_remove` tombstones sightings involving the removed peer.
- **Self-records** are never applied (you never add yourself). A tombstone keyed by your own id sets a "removed us" signal only if newer than the local `sync:self_authorized_at` grant. Incoming-host, outgoing and manual pairing queue that local grant atomically with peer trust; routine sync does not renew it.
- **Tombstone GC** retains membership removals permanently until explicit re-pair supersedes them. Other domains are gated by per-peer acks (`ack_floor`): a tombstone is only reclaimed once every current peer has acked past it (30-day TTL floor).

### App wiring
- Startup establishes/migrates baselines and replays only pending projection receipts before deriving Library/Home models, independently of networking. Stores without projection metadata require one migration replay. `sync_apply` publishes progress-derived models once per batch. Desktop defers sync/torrent creation and download queue start until the first completed render; engine construction runs on workers. `Bridge::start_sync` attaches callbacks, replays any records received during setup and requests a pass; a generation guard prevents a completed desktop setup from reinstalling sync after disable. Android retains foreground/background lease startup.
- `sync_apply` reads the socket-free owner; thread-local, nesting-safe `ApplyingGuard` suppresses echo writes. Deferred addon callbacks retain origin and generation, while desired order/flags are read at completion. `settings_edited(field)` preserves explicit default choices; `persistence_failed` exposes storage health through AppWindow and SettingsPage.
- Durable apply marks pending domains before callback delivery. `Done` and valid stream completion are required for success/ack; writer tasks abort on every cancellation path, including joining. Pairing commits invite consumption and trust together, and the joiner installs trust before closing its completion connection.
- Pairing UI: More → Sync (`ui-settings-sync/sync-module.slint`), invite create/join, confirmation prompt with match code. The created ticket is also rendered as a QR image (`qr.rs`); on Android the invite card offers a camera scanner (`android_qr.rs` + `android/java/dev/misob/nova/QrScanActivity.java`) that decodes a ticket and joins automatically.

---

## 7. Platform specifics

| Concern | Desktop (Linux) | Android |
|---|---|---|
| Entry | `src/main.rs` → `app::run()`; Windows uses the GUI subsystem without a console window | `NovaActivity` (framework `NativeActivity` subclass) → `android_main` (`src/lib.rs`) → `app::run()` |
| System bars / safe areas | Window chrome remains outside the app content | Catalog and player retain edge-to-edge drawing from startup; visible status bars are transparent, captions and controls use Slint safe-area insets, and immersive playback hides the bars. Restoring bars keeps Home’s artwork behind them (`crates/player/src/external.rs`). |
| Playback rotation | Window orientation stays user-controlled | `Player::play` / `wait_for_source` request sensor landscape through `external::set_playback_active` and `NovaActivity.setPlaybackActive`; pause and EOF retain it until player close. Settings → Player’s default-on, device-local `android_auto_rotate` toggle controls this behavior; disabling restores Android’s normal orientation policy immediately. Closing releases to sensor-driven rotation when enabled, so an upright phone returns to portrait while one held sideways stays landscape, independently of the system rotation lock and PiP setting. Java coalesces source handoffs, suspends orientation requests while stopped/in PiP/split-screen and on Android TV, and reapplies the latest state on resume or expansion. The toggle lives in the existing settings JSON; no manifest orientation lock. |
| App icon | `assets/logo.png` is the Slint window icon | Density-specific `android/res/mipmap-*/ic_launcher.png` resources from the same source image |
| Renderer | `winit` + `femtovg` (mpv composites under the Slint scene) | Slint android-activity backend (Skia/GLES); mpv composites into same framebuffer |
| Picture in picture | — | `NovaActivity.java` uses Android 12+ auto-entry on Home and Android 8–11 `onUserLeaveHint`; paused, unloaded, failed, audio/cover-art and modal/controller playback cannot auto-enter. `crates/player/src/android_pip.rs` publishes selected-video display dimensions and pause state; Java clamps Android's aspect-ratio range, uses the video bounds as source hint, and exposes system play/pause actions. AppWindow `android_pip_active` hides its entire scene while the existing mpv underlay and renderer recovery remain active. Dismissal, activity Stop and screen lock pause/checkpoint through `src/lib.rs`. A retained pinned Activity can return as Started/Paused without Resume: Java handles Start/screen-on/unlock with a deduplicated visible-PiP event, restores native recovery and remote availability, and republishes eligible Play controls; auto-continue still applies only to previously playing sessions, and disabled PiP remains paused. Expansion restores ordinary controls. The PiP visibility transition clears interrupted Back presses and schedules the existing guarded focus recovery after the scene is visible, so native Back works immediately after expansion without a pointer press. The pinned cargo-apk2 manifest schema gains `supportsPictureInPicture` through `android/cargo-apk2-pip.patch`. Native action labels use Android resources (English/Croatian). Settings → Player offers a device-local, default-on Picture in picture toggle (`android_pip_enabled`) in the existing settings JSON; disabling immediately blocks future entry and pauses an existing PiP window until expansion/dismissal. No new KV key, service or sync domain. Device gesture, permission-denial, PiP resize/return/dismiss and lock behavior still need physical-device validation. |
| Video | mpv via `libmpv2`, X11; external video playback is temporarily disabled, including automatic fallback. `CacheSettings::uses_external_player` ignores dormant saved desktop preferences; Settings greys out External. Browser links still open normally | pinned nova-vendor mpv/FFmpeg bundles from the Android Nix shell (PGS and bitmap subtitle decoding enabled), JNI for MediaCodec/EGL; external-player `ACTION_VIEW` (Settings → Player backend choice, or fallback). Volume starts pinned to 100% (no volume UI; a vertical swipe adjusts it after) and the transport (⏪10 ▶/⏸ ⏩10) floats centered over the video. |
| Remote playback | Paired foreground receiver uses native mpv; controllers show a separate controls page and cannot start local playback until disconnected. Fullscreen commands affect the receiver window. | Same protocol and role guard; controller volume uses the receiver's normalized music-stream volume (`android_player.rs` / `PlayerFx.java`), brightness remains session-scoped, and background readiness follows activity lifecycle. |
| HTTP | `reqwest` blocking (+ addon `client` feature) | `reqwest` rustls stack (`default-features=false`) |
| Tracker browser sign-in | Loopback OAuth listener with a five-minute approval window. | `NovaAuthService` (`dataSync`) enters foreground before the browser opens, holds a bounded wake lock through credential persistence and stops when the last login lease ends. Independent of downloads; pending OAuth state remains process-local. |
| Stream downloads | `nova-download` progressive HTTP / finite HLS offline bundles + retained `nova-torrent` jobs; one active transfer with a persisted queue. HTTP connects/headers/body-reads are gap-timeout guarded (60 s) and a torrent with no progress is failed after 10 min, so one hung transfer cannot pin the single slot (and every job queued behind it) forever. | Same private app-data queue; process-bound and resumed on next launch. Active transfers keep the process alive through a `dataSync` foreground service (`NovaBackgroundService`, started/stopped from the 250 ms tick via `android_bg.rs`) with a partial wake lock; the notification shows count + progress. Android 15 caps `dataSync` at 6 h/24 h, after which `onTimeout` stops it and jobs resume on the next foreground. |
| Poster loading | worker pool + dual-priority channels; recipe-keyed display pixels | per-grid `net::fetch_image`, existing rustls transport; shared cache/encoder |
| Image compression | Shared `nova-media::cache` JPEG/WebP/PNG variants and atomic replacements | Same encoder, NDK-built bundled libwebp; recipe variants are shared with the desktop cache contract |
| Allocator | jemalloc | system |
| Storage dir | XDG dirs | app files dir + sibling cache dir |
| Legacy config-file import | no | no |
| Permissions | — | INTERNET, ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE, FOREGROUND_SERVICE, FOREGROUND_SERVICE_DATA_SYNC, WAKE_LOCK, POST_NOTIFICATIONS, RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED, CAMERA (runtime; camera feature declared optional) |
| Invite QR | Ticket displayed as a QR image; paste to join | Same QR display, plus "Scan QR code": `QrScanActivity` (framework Camera2 — cargo-apk2 can bundle no AAR) feeds the luma plane to Rust (`rqrr`), which validates the ticket and joins automatically. |
| Screen stay-awake during playback | Session-bus D-Bus `ScreenSaver` inhibit with system-bus logind `idle` fallback (also without a session bus); failed acquisition retries only after pause/close, converged in `Player::tick` + play/toggle/close | `FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON` via `converge_screen_on`, same convergence points; no permission needed |
| Video across lock/unlock / backgrounding | n/a | The local Slint 1.18 Android backend patch (`vendor/i-slint-backend-android-activity`, root `[patch.crates-io]`) suspends on `TerminateWindow` while the old native window is valid and recreates on `InitWindow`; rendering is suppressed without a ready window. Nova frees mpv only in `RenderingTeardown` with its original EGL context current, and refuses to reuse/free it under a different context. `android_main` feeds pause/resume into `crates/player/src/android_recovery.rs`; teardown arms one session-scoped recovery, which waits for both renderer setup and foreground activity in either order. `Player::tick` queues the reload before the scrub guard, retaining position/pause snapshots through missing properties (including initial loading), source headers, external subtitles and the selected decoder. Per-file starts use named `loadfile` arguments (compatible with the bundled older mpv and newer desktop mpv); file-load errors remain session-local so they cannot force all later streams into the external-player picker. Close/new playback cancels old recovery even for the same URL. Pause with no window loss keeps the renderer and needs no reload; activity Pause outside visible PiP immediately pauses mpv and any queued load; screen-off and PiP dismissal use the same pause path, and returning reopens the OSD. Settings → Player → When returning to playback uses device-local `CacheSettings.android_auto_continue` (false by default, excluded from sync) to choose Stay paused or Auto continue, which only resumes previously playing sessions; Stay paused still reloads and buffers the source at the saved position without advancing playback; Android paused controls stay visible by default; the synced `CacheSettings.keep_controls_visible_while_paused` preference can restore the ordinary five-second paused idle hide delay (desktop defaults off). `adb logcat -s nova-player` covers teardown, setup, scheduling, submission and failures; device switching, lock/unlock and rotation still require physical-device validation. |
| System back | n/a (desktop backends never synthesize `Key.Back`) | An AppWindow capture scope consumes Back press/release, ignores key repeats, and dispatches one request to the active page; dialogs, menus, focused Discover search, nested pages, and the player pop their nearest layer first. Dropdown's separate `PopupWindow` captures Back itself. Home root calls `exit_to_background` → `move_task_to_back` (process kept, state restored on return). Slint 1.18 bridges API 34+ predictive-back callbacks into the same key events, and finishes the activity if both press/release are unhandled. Tracking dismissal and episode-search tab/stream teardown restore Detail navigation focus; returning from Adjust or reloading suggestions focuses the persistent Tracking panel scope. Settings subpage closes and responsive input rebuilds restore their surviving navigation scope, and Discover keeps one search field mounted across phone/landscape layouts, so removed inputs do not leave Back without a capture chain. Current targetSdk is 35; verify device gesture behavior before raising it to 36 ([Android 16 behavior changes](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/16/behavior-changes-16#predictive-back)). |
| Background sync | n/a | While Android allows process execution, the sync tokio `interval_loop` runs independently of Slint: More → Sync's "Auto-sync while the app is running" (30 s / 1 / 5 / 15 min) governs it, clamped to a 5-min floor when backgrounded. A cached process can be frozen while retaining that engine. A periodic `JobScheduler` job (`NovaSyncJobService`, fixed 15 min, network-required, persisted) therefore runs one bounded protected pass both with a retained Activity engine and after process death (`android_bg.rs` + `nova_sync`). Existing equivalent registrations are retained, so reopening does not reset the schedule or stop an active job. A borrowed engine refreshes network paths and shares an outbound-pass gate with its ordinary worker; cancellation preserves foreground ownership. Headless merged records project on the next app open. The separate "Background sync" toggle gates the job; no foreground service is started. Doze defers jobs to maintenance windows, so background sync is opportunistic; a desktop always-on peer makes convergence more reliable. |

`nova-config` exposes `android_fonts_dir()` (libmpv subtitle fonts).

Windows/Linux in-app playback uses `configure_desktop_decoder` in
`crates/player/src/lib.rs` and `desktop_decoder.rs`. Settings → Player stores
`CacheSettings.desktop_hwdec` locally, excluded from sync like `android_hwdec`.
HW (the unchanged Windows default) requests `auto-copy`; HW+ (the Linux default)
requests `auto,auto-copy` to try compatible direct decoding before
copy-back/software fallbacks; SW requests
`no`. Direct decoding still depends on the host graphics context and driver;
choosing HW+ on Linux does not promise direct interop. Windows greys out HW+
in Settings and the OSD via the shared `hw_plus_available` mount input; keyboard
navigation skips it, and `DesktopHwdec::supported` resolves older HW+ preferences
to HW before display or playback. The OSD decoder submenu changes
mpv for the current stream only, with live `hwdec-current` feedback; every new
`play` reapplies the saved preference. Windows/Linux switch in place through mpv,
which reinitializes the decoder and seeks within the existing source, retaining
headers/tracks and pause state. The shared `desktop_decoder_available` mount
input exposes the row and its keyboard index in Settings and Player without
changing macOS or Android controls. Other desktop targets retain `hwdec=auto`, and Android
retains its MediaCodec preference/fallback chain. Copying frames can cost
additional memory bandwidth, especially at 4K.
`crates/player/src/gl_state.rs` isolates mpv from Slint's cached GL state:
Windows render-context creation and each video draw save the host state,
reset blending/clipping/depth/stencil/culling, write masks, samplers and
pixel-transfer state (including PBOs),
then restore the host on scope exit, including failed creation. Android keeps
its render-state restoration with GLES external-texture bindings; Linux
production rendering is unchanged. A Linux surfaceless EGL regression poisons
the incoming state, checks framebuffer readback and CPU texture uploads, and
checks restoration after drawing and an error return (including core VAO 0).
The Linux CI job runs it with the dev shell's matching Mesa EGL driver via
`NOVA_TEST_EGL_VENDOR_LIBRARY_FILENAMES`; this is a GL regression, not Windows
driver or playback validation.

---

## 8. Testing

- **Addon package backend**: `src/app/addon_packages.rs` runs its real worker/event-loop installer in an isolated child process to verify updates, rejected imports, state preservation, archive repair, and cancellation after removal.
- **Remote playback**: `crates/sync/src/playback.rs` tests strict framing, headless/membership readiness, request replay/content binding, live QUIC revocation/preview release, durable boot epochs across backward clock changes and revoked queued UI work; `crates/storage/src/lib.rs` tests atomic sequence allocation, reopen/concurrency and failure preservation. `src/app/remote_playback.rs` tests offer display sanitization, exact/unique episode-alias resolution (including offline downloaded media), exclusive loading roles, settings-invalidated addon batches, selector lookup reuse/expiry, and continuous-command ordering; `src/app/playback_actions.rs` tests Stop before an overlay exists and rejects nonfinite mutations. `tests/player/remote_controls.rs` verifies remote shortcuts/callbacks, compact device rows, desktop split, separate Stop placement, compact secondary actions, captured-session preview release, TV offer/action reveal and viewport-capped long-description paging in both directions, and controller/local-player separation in the headless `ui_player` suite.
- **Root addon routing fixtures**: `src/app/test-fixtures/{anikoto,supernova}.zip` preserve identity/manifests/licenses with inert scripts; `src/app/tests.rs` installs scoped snapshots and restores the prior registry.
- **Unit tests**: inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in most modules; app tests in `src/app/tests.rs`; Cinemeta artwork matching/transport regressions in `crates/providers/src/metadata/cinemeta.rs` with a real two-endpoint numbering fixture in `metadata/fixtures/cinemeta-numbering.json`; download tests in `crates/download`; sync tests in each `crates/sync/src/*.rs`.
- **Emoji rendering:** `tests/emoji_font.rs` checks every baseline codepoint in the bundled font. Private painter, foreground-cache, transformed-atlas and independent Skia reference tests live in the pinned FemtoVG fork (`src/text/colr.rs`, `tests/fixtures/colrv1/`); its opt-in `external_color_font_rasterizes` test takes Nova’s `assets/fonts/NotoColorEmoji.ttf` through `COLRV1_TEST_FONT` for exhaustive glyph rasterization. `crates/ui/src/fonts.rs` checks the entire baseline sequence repertoire without system fonts. `tests/ui_emoji_render.rs` is an opt-in real FemtoVG snapshot test for sizes, rotation and atlas reuse; run under a display or Xvfb with `SLINT_BACKEND=winit SLINT_RENDERER=femtovg cargo test --test ui_emoji_render -- --ignored --test-threads=1`.
- **Integration tests** (`tests/`, headless via `i-slint-backend-testing`, no display):
  - `tests/settings_preferences/support.rs` shares mock-time settling, viewport reveal and keyboard input for loading-text, paused-controls and casting-buffer preferences; their individual behavior, translation and sync assertions remain in separate case files.
  - Consolidated UI suites: 20 `tests/ui_*.rs` entry points each import separate case files from the corresponding `tests/<suite>/` directory. `tests/support/isolation.rs` reexecutes the same binary with one exact case, isolated data/cache/config/temp roots, and a body-entry receipt so a zero-match filter cannot pass silently. The parent never initializes Slint; child failures retain captured output. Add ordinary UI regressions to the existing suites instead of linking another standalone Cargo test target. Existing assertions, mock/system clocks, feature guards, native ignored attributes and preview environment switches stay with their case files. Normal Cargo filters remain available: `cargo test --test ui_library -- scroll_restore::`, `cargo test --test ui_discover -- styling::`, `cargo test --test ui_settings_sync -- sync_overflow::`, and `cargo test --test ui_player -- double_tap::`. Run Player's native fixture with a display and `-- osd_header::render_loading_dimmer_keeps_status_bright --ignored`. The inline Slint harnesses `filter_pill_touch.rs`, `settings_control_feedback.rs` and `theme.rs` keep separate executables to avoid recompiling the other macro expansions after a small edit. `tracking_sync.rs` remains a separate small backend target; `tmp_snap.rs` retains its existing diagnostic layout. Player measurements are in [player behavior](player-behavior.md#player-integration-test-pilot).
  - Suite boundaries: `ui_home_{cards,motion}`, `ui_detail_{episodes,layout}`, `ui_streams_{layout,motion}`, `ui_settings_{navigation,controls,preferences,sync}`, `ui_android_back_{navigation,inputs}`, plus `ui_{addons,navigation,downloads,tracking,shared_layout}`; Discover, Library and Player keep their existing suites. Settings stays split by navigation/layout, interactive display controls, persisted preference and Stremio account controls, and sync. Navigation includes profile selection/startup/preferences/layout cases in `navigation/profiles.rs` (filter with `cargo test --test ui_navigation -- profiles::`); Tracking includes completion ratings; Player includes Watch party controls and intro skipping. Android Back separates general routing/rotation from focused-input/removed-row teardown. Shared layout includes the ordinary SearchField regression and automatic next-episode countdown; inline control harnesses retain their original files and process isolation.
  - The 2026-10-17 follow-up folds the newer standalone completion-rating, Watch party and Stremio Settings targets into `ui_tracking`, `ui_player` and `ui_settings_preferences`. Filter with `completion_rating::`, `party::` or `stremio_` in those suites. Comparing the same six targets before with the three merged targets after, the local stripped default-Nix executables total 1,236,607,192 → 622,263,368 bytes (about 586 MiB saved). All 49 test names remain after the module-prefix changes; 48 default cases run and one native Player case stays ignored. The independent inline Slint harnesses retain their measured edit/build isolation.
  - The 2026-10-18 follow-up folds the seven profile cases into `ui_navigation`, retaining all 15 navigation/profile cases and per-case subprocess isolation. The two local stripped default-Nix executables total 421,274,184 bytes before consolidation; the combined executable is 210,909,256 bytes (about 201 MiB saved).
  - `shared_layout/animation_feedback.rs` — bottom-sheet entrance/exit geometry, inert closing rows, interrupted exits and animation-off behavior; player gesture-pill entrance/dismissal and independent player/master switches, using mock time.

  - `tracking_sync.rs` — shared tracking links/tokens, refresh rotation, disconnect/unlink tombstones and local history checkpoints through the generic sync store.

The retained `live-preview` feature now stays compiled and bundles the same catalogs; the test still has a generic fallback for builds without catalogs.
  - `android_back_navigation/nav.rs` — synthetic Back press/release/repeat via `Window::dispatch_event`: resume and category dialogs dismiss first; detail streams→episodes→close, settings subpage→landing→home, player close, Home subpage→landing without a held press also backgrounding, Home root → `exit_to_background`; focused Discover search yields focus before leaving the page. One test fn (backend inits once per process); per-phase ticks because page create/destroy focus races sharing a tick, plus the 280 ms subpage close animation between double-Backs.
  - `android_back_navigation/focus.rs` — verifies mounted Settings, Discover search, Detail, and Player popup/close Back paths; Android press/repeat/release clears root and shared marker modes even when Settings is unmounted, while desktop Back preserves keyboard mode and arrows remain active.
  - `android_back_inputs/addon_actions.rs` — addon action focus survives row removal; Android Back closes Addons to Settings landing and then Home without exiting the activity or consuming held repeats.
  - `android_back_inputs/transient_controls.rs` — native Back press/repeat/release acceptance after download removal, group/transfer refreshes, calendar week rebuilds, device disappearance, Watch party join/end transitions and native player menu/submenu teardown; live Downloads search retains text focus through model updates.
  - `android_back_inputs/tracking.rs`, `android_back_inputs/settings_input.rs`, `android_back_inputs/detail_input.rs`, `android_back_navigation/resize.rs` — actual Back acceptance after tracking Back/tab dismissal, focused Adjust cancellation or suggestion reload, Settings input/subpage closes, episode-search tab/stream teardown, and portrait→landscape Discover search and Settings editors. Callback counts ensure one layer per press and Home-only backgrounding, including no-touch page recreation. Settings rows are found by stable destination IDs and revealed with drags spanning separate event-loop frames before editor taps. Tests using built-in input metadata run against the default compiled UI (not `live-preview`), matching Android; direct `system_back_request` updates bypass these focus-dependent failures.
- `crates/player/src/android_recovery.rs` tests cover activity/renderer ordering (with timer ticks between events), duplicate events, pause and missing-property snapshots, background pause retention (including stale properties), close/replacement (including the same source), queued reloads, seek-to-zero and initial loading. `mpv_load.rs` also tests HTTP video prefetch while paused without position advance. Run with `cargo test -p nova -p nova-player --lib` on desktop to include the root desktop backend features.
- Sync tests use real iroh endpoints with the `Minimal` preset (`presets::Minimal`) and in-memory address lookups; see `crates/sync/src/protocol.rs` tests for patterns.
- `src/app/qr.rs` unit test round-trips a real invite ticket through the QR encoder and `rqrr` decoder (the same crate the Android scanner uses), guarding the display↔scan payload path without a device.
- `src/app/settings.rs` tests exercise real headless settings controls, immediate memory capture, navigation before autosave, current-state projection, and the shared playback-speed debounce. The test runs in an isolated child process because redb and Slint initialize once per process.
- Verification commands: `cargo check`, `cargo test`, `cargo test -p nova-download --lib`, `cargo test -p nova-sync --lib`.

---

### Discover and Library suite measurements

Local default-Nix measurements on 2026-10-13 used the same symbol-stripped
profile on both sides, two Cargo build jobs, and already-built dependencies.
Each value below is the median of three samples. Executable-build samples
removed only the selected test executables and their incremental caches;
single-edit samples changed the same assertion message in `discover/styling.rs`
or `library/scroll_restore.rs`, then restored the original source. These measure
test executable builds, not whole-project clean builds.

Runtime samples launched the already-built executables directly, excluding
Cargo/build overhead. Separate targets ran sequentially, as Cargo normally runs
integration targets. Suites used default test parallelism, plus a separate
`--test-threads=1` comparison. Suite cases include the extra subprocess and
fresh data/cache/config/temp roots; the old standalone cases used their normal
environment. Original assertions, clocks and case counts are unchanged.

| Measurement | Discover: separate → suite | Library: separate → suite |
|---|---:|---:|
| Executables | 6 → 1 | 6 → 1 |
| Total executable size | 984.82 → 164.43 MiB | 984.62 → 164.28 MiB |
| Build with cached dependencies | 12.165 → 3.568 s | 11.999 → 3.414 s |
| Single-case edit rebuild | 2.964 → 3.062 s | 3.008 → 3.068 s |
| Default runtime | 19.383 → 8.812 s | 16.467 → 9.529 s |
| Sequential runtime | 19.383 → 22.676 s | 16.467 → 19.491 s |
| Total CPU time during default runtime | 13.841 → 17.847 s | 16.307 → 19.987 s |

Consolidation saves another 1.60 GiB of executables beyond stripping. The default
runtime improvement comes from parallel case execution, with more total CPU
work in this sample; sequential execution is slower. Small suites keep the
measured single-edit build penalty below 0.1 s here. These are local samples,
not general performance guarantees. Every build/test command was monitored
and stopped if free space fell below a 60 GiB safety cushion, protecting the
requested 50 GiB floor; no abort was needed.

own source file and process. The unchanged backend tracking test and existing
diagnostic probe remain outside these suites.

Single-test edits added a temporary `black_box` constant to the same first
case in each group, then restored its source. Home cards, Settings navigation
and stream layout use the median of three edits; other groups have one edit
sample. Across consolidated groups the median difference was +0.20 s, with
observed differences from −0.05 to +0.62 s. Some CPU-dominated groups have little
runtime improvement; `ui_streams_motion` was 6.62 → 6.67 s in this sample.

An initial combined inline-control suite was deliberately dropped: its small
pill test edit rebuilt in 5.48 s versus 1.71 s separately, while its cold
executable build was only 13.83 → 12.43 s. Keeping the three macro harnesses
separate preserves their independent expansion/build costs; the ordinary
SearchField test instead joins `ui_shared_layout`.

These savings are additional to symbol stripping. New suite cases include
fresh data/cache/config/temp roots and subprocess startup; old cases used
their normal environment. Default parallel runs use more total CPU here.
All build/test commands stopped on a 60 GiB free-space safety cushion, above
the required 50 GiB floor; no abort was needed.

The full workspace tests, formatting, Clippy with all targets, workspace check
and focused sync overflow test passed. Seven of nine additional Xvfb/FemtoVG
native checks passed, including rendered preview bodies. Two native checks
failed identically when re-run from their original standalone source: the
hero fade fixture reports the same stripe at width 390, phase 1, pixel
(327, 241), and Tracking’s optional preview reports the same 896 × 726 layout
against its width assertion. These failures predate the consolidation; the
assertions and native selection remain unchanged.

### Generated UI empty-harness measurements

A local default-Nix comparison on 2026-10-13 audited all 27 generated UI leaf
libraries: none contain unit tests, while the main `nova-ui` library has seven
real tests. `[lib] test = false` is now set only on those 27 leaves. Their
normal libraries remain dependencies of the app and UI integration suites;
the trial retained all 41 other test targets. Re-enable a leaf’s harness before
adding Rust unit tests there.

Measurements used two Cargo jobs, the symbol-stripped profile and cached
normal dependencies. The fresh-build sample removed only those 27 test
executables, their matching fingerprints and their individually identified
incremental caches; exactly 27 empty harnesses rebuilt. This measures avoidable
work when those caches are missing, not a whole-project clean build. Cached
build and Player-edit rows are medians of three samples. The second enabled
measurement follows the disabled trial to expose order/cache effects.

| Measurement | Harnesses enabled | Disabled | Re-enabled comparison |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Fully cached workspace `--no-run` | 0.868 s | 0.790 s | 0.807 s |
| Targeted Player test edit | 4.478 s | 3.248 s | 3.937 s |

The 27 fresh empty harnesses took **158.58 s wall / 297.77 s CPU**, generated
**3.17 GiB of incremental cache**, and linked **22.07 MiB of executables**.
Executing all 27 already-built empty binaries sequentially took a median
**0.047 s**. This optimization primarily avoids compilation and cache growth;
warm build and execution savings are small. Both enabled and disabled targeted
Player commands built zero empty leaf harnesses, so the observed edit timing
variation is not a demonstrated benefit of this flag. Plain root `cargo test`
also selects only the root package; the broader benefit applies to default
`cargo test --workspace` or default test selection on the leaf packages.
Explicit library/all-target selection may override the flag.

Changing manifests caused a **98.33 s** one-time build while switching off and
**130.75 s** while switching back, rebuilding dependent targets. These transition
costs are excluded from cached and fresh-harness comparisons. All temporary
source probes and trial settings were restored before applying the final flag.
Every build was guarded by a 60 GiB cushion above the requested 50 GiB floor;
minimum free space was **146.92 GiB**.

After validation, 54 obsolete generated-leaf harness cache directories and
54 executables across current/older variants were removed: **11.96 GiB** of
unique file contents. The caches were identified by their unit-harness
`test::test_main_static` object reference; every removed executable’s
`--list` reported zero tests. Normal library and nonempty-test artifacts were
preserved, and a default workspace `--no-run` remained warm after cleanup.
Formatting, workspace Clippy/check, the sync unit tests and full workspace
tests passed with the final manifest flags.

A separate documentation-test audit passed with **zero cases in all 39 jobs**.
Cargo’s 27 generated-leaf documentation jobs accounted for approximately
**30.37 s of the 35.71 s total**, measured between serial `Doc-tests` markers.
`test = false` does not skip those jobs. Setting `doctest = false` on the same
empty leaves is a separate possible runtime optimization; its saving is
estimated from this audit, not measured in a disabled comparison, and it is
not applied here. The main `nova-ui` and all other libraries retain their
unit/documentation coverage.



## 9. Conventions & gotchas

- **No comments policy is not absolute here** — this codebase is heavily
  commented with rationale. Match the surrounding style; explain *why*, not *what*.
- **Leaf-crate split is deliberate**: editing app logic should not recompile
  heavy deps (Slint codegen, mpv, iroh, librqbit, redb). Keep new heavy
  subsystems in their own crate.
- **postcard wire schemas are not self-describing**: adding/reordering fields in
  `crates/sync` wire structs requires an ALPN version bump (sync is `/3`;
  pairing/removal are unchanged by sync-frame compression). Local storage stays
  JSON, but the sync store is one row per record, not a single blob.
- **Row heights follow wrapped text, not line estimates**: Slint measures
  wrapped `Text` at its intrinsic width during the preferred-height pass, so
  rows that reserve a fixed/estimated height clip on narrow screens. Rows use a
  hidden zero-height measurement copy at the live width (`SettingsRow`, the
  detail stream list) to size themselves; keep that pattern when adding rows
  with variable text.
- **Slint API changes are three-step**: page component + `AppWindow` property/callback + `run.rs` wiring. Missing a step is a common compile/runtime error.
- **New user-facing strings are marked `@tr("…")`** (the `nova-ui` catalogs bundle at build time; see §4). The catalog is English-only today, so marking changes nothing on screen — it is what makes a later language possible. Translation *values* pushed as data (language names, sizes, statuses) stay untranslated.
- **UI thread only** for Slint; use `slint::invoke_from_event_loop` from workers.
- **Android player keyboard/D-pad order** (open note): the centered transport
  keeps the logical keyboard indices (play = 1, rewind = 3, forward = 4) while
  reading 3 → 1 → 4 left-to-right on screen. Left/right traversal (`kb_prev` /
  `kb_next` in `player.slint`) skips the removed volume slot (2) and the narrow
  track buttons (5/6), but does not reorder to mirror the visual layout. Remap
  those helpers if exact on-screen D-pad order is ever wanted.
- **`ScrollView` needs a direct layout child to scroll**: its content size is
  derived from direct layout children only, and a bare `if`-conditional child
  is ignored — the viewport then collapses to its own height and touch drag
  silently does nothing. Wrap view-gated content in an unconditional
  `VerticalLayout` (see `home.slint`'s subpage grid) like Library/Settings do.
- **`ScrollView` content is at least viewport-tall**: the compiler binds
  `content-height = max(flickable.height, layout.min-height)`, so a short page
  still fills the viewport and its unpinned rows/cards absorb the slack
  (`vertical-stretch` defaults to 1) and stretch. Settings subpages keep their
  content top-aligned by pinning the fixed rows (`vertical-stretch: 0`) and
  ending the scroll content with a `Rectangle { vertical-stretch: 1; }` spacer
  that absorbs the excess; long content still scrolls.
- **Reminder choice sheets** reuse `MenuSheet.keyboard_navigation` to enable arrows/Tab/Return on desktop and TV; the default retains existing TV-only keyboard behavior for other callers. Detail removes the hero reminder and opens the choice from its top-right options; Calendar uses the same modes without changing ICS episode exports.
- **Page-level overlays are siblings of the page layout, never layout children**: a `MenuSheet` (or any full-page overlay) declared *inside* the page's `VerticalLayout` is laid out as a row — opening it then squeezes/pushes the page content instead of covering it, which looks like "everything got pushed to the top" (most visible on Android). Declare it as a direct child of the page root `Rectangle`, after the layout, as the Library, Detail and Home sheets do.
- **Nested `Flickable`s do not axis-lock**: the page sees the press first and
  holds it for its drag-vs-tap delay. Home uses the Detail stream-pill pattern:
  its interactive inner rails use Slint's drag and native inertia, while each
  card's `TouchArea` reports early touch moves (`touch-finger-id` is nonzero
  before the held-back press). A horizontal hint disables page panning; a
  vertical gesture or release hands it back. The outer page disables mouse
  drag-pan so desktop rail drags reach the inner rail; wheel scrolling still
  moves the page. Covered landing rails and cards disable on subpages because
  opacity alone does not block hit-testing. Detail's pill bar similarly gates
  its page pan via `pan_hint` / `pan_release` and a short release timer.
- **`ApplyingGuard`** must wrap remote-apply code paths so writes don't echo back to sync.
- **Android packaging is `cargo-apk2`, not `cargo-apk`.** The Java background components (`android/java/`) must be compiled to DEX and the `<service>`/`foregroundServiceType` elements emitted, neither of which the old tool can do; `Cargo.toml` uses `use_aapt2`, `java_sources`, and `has_code = true` in the application metadata, and explicitly sets `extract_native_libs = false` for the direct-loading build intermediate. `android/cargo-apk2-uncompressed-libs.patch` makes the pinned packager store native libraries uncompressed in every build profile, with its existing SDK-35 16 KiB ZIP alignment. Release packaging preserves that policy for individual ABI APKs, while `.github/scripts/package-android.py` compresses only the final universal APK's native libraries and enables extraction before alignment/signing. An explicit `[[…application.activity]]` is also required (cargo-apk2 generates no implicit activity). The APK launcher icon is configured as `@mipmap/ic_launcher` and packaged from `android/res/`, derived from `assets/logo.png`. The `.#android` shell provides cargo-apk2 from the flake (nixpkgs has no such attr and upstream publishes no binstall artifacts, so it is built with `rustPlatform.buildRustPackage`) and unsets `CPATH` (host include leak breaks the NDK C build).
- **Android builds use `--no-default-features --features android`**; guard desktop-only code with `#[cfg(feature = "desktop")]` or `#[cfg(not(target_os = "android"))]`.
- **Download jobs are local-only**: `downloads:v1` and artifacts are never synced. HTTP resumes require matching `Range`/`ETag`/`Last-Modified`; retained torrent data is protected from playback cache eviction. Torrent statistics carry exact selected-file downloaded/total bytes into new and retried jobs before completion. Each torrent job owns its bundle separately; removing one episode preserves siblings and active playback. Torrent deletion goes through the engine ownership lock, including a worker whose engine was disabled, rather than generic artifact-parent deletion. Transfers are one-at-a-time, so a hung job must not pin the slot: HTTP connects/headers/body-gaps time out (60 s) and a torrent with no progress fails after 10 min; tapping Download again re-arms a `Failed` job in place. A transfer worker carries a done flag; the 250 ms tick reaps one that ended without finalizing (panic/unwind) and the state mutex is poison-tolerant, so a dead worker can never leave every later job stuck in `Queued`. Completed-job validation **canonicalizes** both paths before the root check (Android path aliases), and unreferenced HTTP artifacts under `<data>/downloads/http/` are re-adopted on startup rather than silently dropped (torrent artifacts are not re-adopted: sparse partial files look complete on disk). Deletion (`remove_owned_path`/`remove_owned_dir`) canonicalizes the target the same way — a lexical `starts_with` across the `/data/user/0` vs `/data/data` aliases previously skipped the unlink, clearing the row while leaving the file (and its app-storage footprint) on disk.
- **`Store::try_load` quarantines** unreadable rows/clocks without recursive backups — check `sync:quarantine:*` for evidence. Unreadable identity bytes are not replaced with a new identity.
- **The Android background sync job must open storage before reading settings.** `android_bg::run_headless_sync` sets the files directory and opens storage first, checks both enable flags, migrates legacy profile records before any upgraded-protocol exchange, and obtains a `BackgroundLease` that creates a headless engine or borrows a retained foreground-owned engine. An allocated engine alone does not prove background execution: Android may have frozen it. Lifecycle transitions are serialized; foreground attachment takes ownership before job release, and a second headless owner is excluded. `onStopJob` cancels a 120-second-budget `one_shot`, including outbound-slot acquisition and awaited network refresh, not the foreground worker. The shared outbound guard keeps its slot until canceled peer tasks drop and clears transient status; Java finishes at most once. The outcome distinguishes completed/cancelled/timed-out/skipped/failed work; `pass_count` is not a success signal. `crates/sync/tests/background_ownership.rs` covers borrowing, cancellation and foreground handoff; `protocol.rs` covers outbound exclusion, task drain and deadlines. Device validation remains required.
- The working tree has historically carried uncommitted work on the `iroh`
  branch; check `git status` before assuming a file's committed state.

---

Generic manifest collection/item/controller metadata builds one outlined card per source/debrid collection, with a Stream preferences card and inline editors for ordinary typed fields with compact enabled rows, editor-only gears and a predefined Add footer, retaining flat persisted keys; structural headers/pickers carry no item identity, removal lives in the opened item actions, and retained credentials display Configured rather than claiming a verified connection; generic `sort-order` fields expose Up/Down and High/Low controls, storing the complete order in one `plugin_settings` field (`sort.order` for Supernova). Supernova labels its existing `seeders` sort criterion Number of seeds, retaining movable priority, High/Low direction and unknown-last ordering. Generic `ordered-choices` fields provide Add/Up/Down/Remove lists. Older Supernova packages expose audio (`language.prefs`) and codec (`codec.prefs`) controls; capable packages take those choices from the effective universal policy and display its Settings → Player summary, while subtitle languages (`subtitle.prefs`) remain independent; missing newer sort criteria append to valid older orders without losing existing priority/directions. Provider secrets stay masked in their item editor. Source/TorBox fixtures (including `sources/knaben.js`) are in `crates/providers/src/supernova_tests.rs`; `metadata.js` optionally enriches up to three selected files from AnimeTosho torrent/file JSON probes, verifying hash, unique filename/size and IDs before separating audio/subtitle tracks and codecs; probe data uses the bounded OS disk cache (24-hour successes, 60-second misses), and reduced real API fixtures plus ownership/budget tests live in `supernova_track_metadata_tests.rs` and `crates/providers/test-fixtures/addons/supernova/fixtures/`; description parsing, source-field propagation, matched detail pages, cache reuse, enabled-source/request limits and ranking precedence are covered in `supernova_{evidence,source_evidence,description}_tests.rs` and `supernova_tests.rs`; progressive transport and identity validation live in `registry.rs`/`registry_tests.rs`, owner replacement/focus in `src/app/detail.rs`, memory-only next-episode warming/coalescing in `src/app/{next_episode,stream_prefetch}.rs`, and reveal/hold regressions in `tests/streams_motion/selector_transition.rs`. | CI checks / Android ABI assets / build packaging / Windows artifact ZIP | `Cargo.toml` (`[package.metadata.android]`), `build.rs`, `crates/player/Cargo.toml` (32-bit mpv bindings), `flake.nix`, `Makefile`, `nix/native-media/`, `.github/workflows/build-release.yml` (`android-build` three-ABI matrix → `android-package` merge/sign → release assets; `check-linux`, `check-windows`, ARM64 app checking plus `android/test_runtime_packaging.py` for repeated three-ABI APK staging on PRs/main source pushes; `android/cargo-apk2-readonly-libs.patch` fixes read-only dependency copies; `android/cargo-apk2-uncompressed-libs.patch` stores native libraries for direct loading), `.github/scripts/{package-android,test_package_android,test_package_windows}.py` (APK payload/ABI checks, compressed/extracted universal versus stored/16 KiB aligned per-ABI policies, binary manifest regressions, and repeatable Windows staging regression), `.github/scripts/package-windows.sh` (flat bundle ZIP; uploaded directly and preserved by the release job) |
| Missing seasons in main series / preserved episode IDs | `crates/providers/src/metadata/coverage.rs`, `metadata.rs::{missing_seasons,apply_enrichment}`, `src/app/detail.rs::write_episode_meta_cache_for`; shared ID/alias/episode proof prevents duplicate coverage, fills partial seasons and retains saved IDs/episodes through refreshes; Detail, Home and prefetch use the existing stable episode cache. |

| Selective backup/import/recovery | `src/app/backup/`, `documents.rs`, `crates/ui/backup.slint`, Settings Data destination and `BackupBridge`; per-source profile inclusion preserves mapping/component choices, previews bind the full selection and expose scrollable TV review rows, and recovery deletion requires confirmation of the exact reviewed inventory (`docs/backups.md`). `Choice.included` defaults to true for legacy receipts; storage keys are unchanged. Data uses the Settings push/fade and pop/fade with a retained inactive exit, repeated-Back shielding and focus recovery; master/Page transitions switches and rapid reopen are covered in `tests/navigation/backup.rs`. Same-ID option models update rows in place so toggle delegates survive, and BackupChoice resyncs authoritative checked updates after local clicks. |
| Synced queue / continuous playback | `src/app/watch_queue/`, `watch_queue.rs`, `playback_end.rs`, QueueBridge and remote receiver hooks |
| Smart offline / physical budget enforcement | `src/app/smart_offline.rs`, headless profile runtime, download offline ledger and torrent storage middleware; `crates/ui/{smart-downloads,downloads-page,page-header,appwindow}.slint`, `OfflineBridge` in `ui-shared/globals.slint`, and `tests/downloads/smart_offline.rs` for retained entry/return motion and header Back. `PageHeader.back-focused` forwards semantic TV focus to the shared chevron. |
| Release reminders / ICS snapshots | `src/app/release_reminders.rs`, Android adapter, CalendarBridge and Calendar tools panel; pointer/keyboard/TV scope changes invalidate export previews, and rejected or consumed previews clear Save until a fresh preview. `tests/downloads/release_reminders.rs` covers input parity and app tests cover stale-preview recovery. Detail and Calendar reminder choices use `MenuSheet.selected_index` with a drawn radio indicator; `tests/detail_layout/topbar.rs` and the reminder UI suite verify selection independently of keyboard focus. |
| Sleep timer / session stop precedence | App and native player `sleep_timer.rs`, `playback_end.rs`, shared OSD/remote timer menu |
| Profile PIN / content allowlist / management gates | `src/app/profile_policy.rs`, `profile-pin.slint`, `docs/profiles.md` |
| Watch-party own-source recommendations | `src/app/party_source.rs`, `party.rs`, `crates/party/src/{lib,engine}.rs`, shared stream card Recommended caption; ephemeral scoped digests only label exact matches already present in local filtered results, `nova/party/3` |
